OpenSCAD Language Reference

Semantic model47 3D primitives4 2D primitives6 Transformations8 Boolean operations3 Modifier characters4 Extrusions2 Other geometry operations9 Control flow and binding6 List comprehensions6 Modules, functions, children5 Function literals (2021.01)2 Operators, literals, lexical structure18 Math builtins22 Type-test builtins6 String builtins and string semantics4 Vector/list builtins6 Other builtins: echo, assert, version, introspection7 Special variables ($-variables)10 include vs use2 import() and export formats3 Customizer (comment-based parameters)3

The compatibility contract for a from-scratch implementation. Baseline: OpenSCAD 2021.01; snapshot-only features are flagged in their version chip. Every entry carries its edge cases and oddities — the parts that break naïve reimplementations. Items marked verify vs oracle are believed correct but must be pinned with a probe file against the official binary before being treated as contract (124 such items).

“OpenSCAD” names the language implemented for compatibility; this document is independent of and not endorsed by the OpenSCAD project. Sourced from the CC-BY-SA user manual (paraphrased), the public cheat sheet, and behavioral knowledge — never from GPL source.

Semantic model

2D geometry model (z=0 plane)

concept pre-2015.03

All 2D primitives yield dimensionally-2D geometry conceptually living in the z=0 plane. Preview draws it as a thin slab for visibility, but in the CSG tree it is strictly 2D and can never mix with 3D.

2D and 3D form two disjoint geometry worlds: booleans, hull, and minkowski require uniform dimensionality (mixing is an ERROR: 'Mixing 2D and 3D objects is not supported'); linear_extrude/rotate_extrude are the only 2D-to-3D bridges and projection() the only 3D-to-2D bridge. 2D results export via DXF/SVG (and PDF in 2021.01) only. The 2D pipeline runs a separate polygon-clipping kernel (Clipper-class, fixed-point) whose quantization behavior differs from the 3D mesh pipeline.

Edge cases & oddities
  • The preview slab thickness is a rendering artifact — it must not appear in any export or measurement.
  • 3D transforms applied to 2D children: translate/rotate with z components flatten back — VERIFY exact behavior (e.g. rotate([90,0,0]) on a square, translate([0,0,5]) on 2D: believed the z offset is discarded when the 2D shape is consumed by 2D ops/extrusion but shows displaced in preview); pin against oracle.
  • scale/mirror with z components on 2D children: z part ignored (VERIFY).
  • Empty 2D results (annihilated by offset/difference) are silent — no warnings anywhere in the 2D pipeline for emptiness.
  • Micro-scale 2D coordinates are quantized by the fixed-point clipper during booleans/offset — 2D precision semantics are observably different from 3D.
linear_extrude(3) { circle(5); square(4); } // implicit 2D union, then extruded
Implementer: Tag geometry with dimensionality in the tree and enforce the mixing error verbatim. Decide and document your 2D coordinate quantization; probing Clipper-scale effects in the oracle at ~1e-6..1e-9 scales tells you what real scripts can observe.

3D primitive set (completeness)

concept pre-2015.03

The complete set of built-in 3D solids. There is no torus, wedge, prism, tube, or any other built-in solid in any OpenSCAD version — everything else is user-space via extrusions, booleans, and hull/minkowski.

cube, sphere, cylinder and polyhedron are the only parametric 3D primitives. import() of STL/OFF/AMF/3MF (OBJ snapshot-only) and surface() heightmaps also act as leaf 3D geometry sources but are covered in the import/export and other-geometry-ops categories. A reimplementation must not add extra solids under unprefixed names, since user scripts may define modules named e.g. 'torus' and rely on the three-namespace/shadowing rules.

Edge cases & oddities
  • dxf_linear_extrude()/dxf_rotate_extrude() are deprecated-but-functional aliases of the extrusions in 2021.01 (see their entry) — not part of the primitive set, and not errors.
  • cube/sphere/cylinder/polyhedron can all be shadowed by user module definitions — VERIFY the precedence of user redefinitions of builtins before relying on it.
cube(1); sphere(1); cylinder(1); polyhedron(points=[[0,0,0],[1,0,0],[0,1,0],[0,0,1]], faces=[[0,2,1],[0,1,3],[1,2,3],[0,3,2]]);
Implementer: Keep the primitive namespace closed. Route import()/surface() through the same leaf-geometry interface as the four primitives so booleans treat them uniformly.

argument matching (calls to modules, functions, builtins)

concept 2015.03

Uniform call convention: positional arguments fill parameters in declaration order, named arguments bind by name in any order, unknown named arguments warn and are ignored — EXCEPT $-prefixed arguments, which are always accepted and injected into the callee's dynamic $-environment.

positional argsMatched left-to-right to parameters not yet bound by name.
named argsname=value pairs binding declared parameters in any order.
$-argsAny $name=value is legal on ANY call without declaration; it does not need a matching parameter and becomes dynamically scoped for the callee's entire subtree/call chain (e.g. sphere(10, $fn=64)).

After matching, unbound parameters take their default expressions (evaluated in the callee's scope, earlier parameters visible); unbound parameters without defaults become undef. The same matcher serves user modules, user functions, function literals, and builtins — builtins additionally have their documented alternate spellings (r/d etc.) resolved on top of it.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Unknown named argument to a user module/function: warning approx 'WARNING: variable foo not specified as parameter' or 'Ignoring unknown parameter' — VERIFY exact 2021.01 text; the argument is discarded, the call proceeds.
  • Too many positional arguments: warning approx 'WARNING: Too many unnamed arguments supplied' — VERIFY text; extras discarded.
  • Same parameter bound twice (positionally and by name, or two named): later binding wins with a warning — VERIFY exact behavior/order and text; probe f(1, x=2) and f(x=1, x=2).
  • Positional arguments AFTER a named argument: VERIFY — believed to continue filling the next unbound positional slot (not an error); pin with probes, libraries accidentally rely on it.
  • Default expressions may reference earlier parameters (function f(a, b=a*2)) and outer lexical names; VERIFY referencing a LATER parameter (believed undef+warning).
  • Argument expressions are evaluated in the CALLER's scope before matching (eager), including for arguments that end up discarded — VERIFY discard-still-evaluated via echo probes.
  • $-args on builtins are the tessellation idiom; unknown PLAIN named args on builtins also warn-and-ignore (e.g. cube(size=1, bogus=2)) — VERIFY.
  • Declared $-formals (module m($fn=8)) both receive passed values and re-broadcast downward — VERIFY interplay when the caller also has $fn set.
module m(a, b = 10) { echo(a, b, $extra); } m(1, $extra = 99); // ECHO: 1, 10, 99
Implementer: One shared matcher for everything; the warning set (unknown param, too many positional, duplicate) is --hardwarnings-fatal compat surface. Probe the named-then-positional corner and duplicate-binding precedence exhaustively against the oracle.

Circle tessellation formula ($fn/$fa/$fs)

concept pre-2015.03 (stable through 2021.01)

The single exact formula converting a radius plus the special variables $fn (default 0), $fa (default 12 deg), $fs (default 2 units) into a fragment count N for a full 360-degree circle. Consumed by circle, sphere, cylinder, rotate_extrude, offset(r), text outlines, and linear_extrude's default twist slices.

$fnFragment count override; 0 (default) disables; >0 forces max(int($fn),3) fragments regardless of $fa/$fs.
$faMinimum angle per fragment in degrees, default 12; caps fragments at 360/$fa; clamped to >= 0.01 with WARNING.
$fsMinimum arc length per fragment in units, default 2; caps fragments at 2*PI*r/$fs; clamped to >= 0.01 with WARNING.

For r below GRID_FINE = 2^-20 (~9.5367e-7) the result is a fixed 3. Otherwise $fn>0 wins outright (minimum 3). Otherwise the $fa-derived and $fs-derived counts compete and the SMALLER of the two angle-equivalents (i.e. whichever demands more fragments loses to min() as written — note min(360/$fa, 2*PI*r/$fs) picks the LOWER fragment count, so the coarser constraint governs) is taken, floored at 5, then ceil'd to an integer. Per-consumer radius: circle uses r; sphere uses r for meridians and derives rings = ceil(N/2); cylinder uses max(r1,r2); rotate_extrude applies the formula to the profile's maximum radius and scales by angle/360; offset(r) uses |r| for corner arcs; text uses it for outline curve flattening. All three variables are dynamically scoped $-specials, settable at any scope or per-call (sphere(5, $fn=64)).

Edge cases & oddities
  • Note the direction of min(): with defaults and r=1, min(30, 3.14)=3.14 then max(...,5)=5 — small circles get 5 fragments; large radii are governed by $fa's 360/12=30 until 2*PI*r/$fs falls below it — never blindly 'increase quality', the coarser bound wins by design.
  • verify vs oracle $fn is truncated toward zero (int cast), so $fn=5.999 → 5; pin truncation vs round/ceil via oracle.
  • Negative (finite) $fn fails the > 0 test and falls through to the $fa/$fs path; NaN or +/-inf $fn instead returns exactly 3 fragments (guarded BEFORE the $fn > 0 test — see the canonical formula).
  • $fa or $fs <= 0 or < 0.01: clamped to 0.01 with WARNING approx '$fa too small - clamping to 0.01' / '$fs too small - clamping to 0.01' — VERIFY exact text and whether the warning fires once per consumer or once per assignment.
  • The floor of 5 applies ONLY in the $fa/$fs path; $fn=3 and $fn=4 legitimately produce triangles/squares.
  • The 3-fragment tiny-radius branch applies before $fn — VERIFY: whether $fn overrides for r < 2^-20 or the tiny branch wins (believed the tiny branch wins; pin).
  • rotate_extrude(angle=a) scales the full-circle count by a/360 — VERIFY exact rounding (ceil?) and minimum fragment count for partial sweeps.
  • $fa/$fs/$fn read at consumption time through dynamic scoping: a value set in a caller module flows into library circles unless shadowed.
  • Formula unchanged 2015.03 through 2021.01 (VERIFY no snapshot drift if chasing nightlies).
  • Canonical formula with all guards: (r < 2^-20 || isinf($fn) || isnan($fn)) ? 3 : ($fn > 0) ? max(int($fn), 3) : ceil(max(min(360/$fa, 2*PI*r/$fs), 5)). NaN or +/-inf $fn returns exactly 3 fragments (same branch as the tiny-radius guard, checked first); int($fn) truncates toward zero.
$fa = 6; $fs = 0.5; circle(r = 4); // fragments = ceil(max(min(60, 50.27), 5)) = 51
Implementer: Implement once as a shared function; every consumer must call it with the documented radius. This formula plus the sphere ring rule and vertex phase conventions determine essentially all curved-mesh diffs against the oracle — freeze them first, with a table-driven golden test across radii and all three variables, including the clamp warnings.

CLI diagnostic switches

concept 2019.05

Three CLI switches escalate or enable the diagnostic surface; CI users treat them as part of the language contract.

--hardwarnings promotes every WARNING to a fatal error (exit non-zero at the first one) — the SET of warnings you emit becomes pass/fail behavior. --check-parameters and --check-parameter-ranges (both true/false) control whether builtin-module argument validity and range checks emit their warnings at all — i.e. parts of the warning surface can be switched OFF.

Edge cases & oddities
  • With --hardwarnings, warning ORDER becomes observable (which warning fires first decides the error message).
  • verify vs oracle the precise set of warnings each check-parameter switch gates in 2021.01 — pin per-warning while building the warning catalog.
  • Defaults: both check switches default to true in 2021.01 GUI and CLI — VERIFY.
openscad --hardwarnings -o /dev/null probe.scad && echo CLEAN
Implementer: Design the diagnostics engine with per-warning stable codes and a severity-escalation switch from day one; retrofitting --hardwarnings parity onto ad-hoc prints is painful.

comments and lexical structure

concept pre-2015.03

C-style comments; whitespace-insensitive token stream; identifier and keyword rules.

// comments run to end of line; /* */ block comments do NOT nest. Identifiers are [A-Za-z0-9_]+ not starting with a digit; the $ prefix creates special (dynamically scoped) variable names. Keywords: module, function, if, else, for, let, each, echo, assert, include, use, true, false, undef — echo and assert are lexer keywords since their 2019.05 expression forms ('echo = 5;' and 'module echo() ...' are parse errors), though both remain callable in statement position via a dedicated module-name grammar production (alongside for/let/each). intersection_for and other builtins are ordinary identifiers, not keywords.

Edge cases & oddities
  • /* /* */ ends at the FIRST */ - non-nesting block comments.
  • include <file> and use <file> use angle brackets with a raw path (spaces allowed inside); they are directives, not expressions.
  • Customizer metadata lives in comments, so a compatible implementation must retain or re-scan comments.
  • $ alone is not an identifier; $foo is valid anywhere an identifier is, and any $name may be invented by users.
  • Identifiers are case-sensitive; there is no reserved 'in' or 'return'.
  • No semicolon after a { } block; assignment statements and instantiations require ';'.
/* block */ x = 1; // line
echo(x);

convexity

concept pre-2015.03

A preview-only tuning parameter meaning 'the maximum number of front-facing surfaces a ray through this object can cross'. It exists solely so the OpenCSG F5 preview compositor can allocate enough stencil passes; it NEVER affects F6 geometry or any export.

OpenCSG renders CSG previews with a multi-pass depth/stencil algorithm whose pass count must be at least the scene's 'convexity' — the largest number of front-facing boundary crossings any view ray can make through an object. Convex objects need 1. Each concavity a ray can enter adds a crossing: a 10-pointed star extrusion needs about convexity 10 viewed edge-on. When the declared value is too LOW, F5 preview shows visual artifacts — faces missing, geometry apparently inverted, or objects vanishing when viewed through their concave regions — while F6 render and every export remain perfectly correct. When too HIGH, the only cost is extra preview rendering passes (slower F5). The parameter is accepted by the geometry-producing nodes that can be concave (polygon, polyhedron, linear_extrude, rotate_extrude, surface, import, render, minkowski, resize; VERIFY projection also accepts it and prints it in .csg dumps) and is absent from the always-convex-or-handled primitives (cube, sphere, cylinder, circle, square, text, offset, hull). Values are converted via double-to-int truncation and clamped to a minimum default when <= 0 (believed 1 for most nodes, 2 for rotate_extrude — VERIFY).

Edge cases & oddities
  • convexity never changes rendered/exported geometry in any release — an implementation may accept-and-ignore it everywhere, but it MUST parse it everywhere upstream does, including the undocumented spots (VERIFY projection, resize).
  • Fractional values are truncated (convexity = 2.9 acts as 2); VERIFY negative/zero values reset to the node's default rather than erroring; VERIFY non-numeric values fall back silently to the default.
  • Default values differ per node: believed 1 for most, 2 for rotate_extrude (VERIFY each via .csg dump of a bare node).
  • 'convexity = 10' is a cargo-cult idiom from the manual's twisted-star example; scripts set it reflexively — it must never warn on the nodes that accept it.
  • The parameter is echoed in .csg dump normalization (e.g. 'linear_extrude(height = 5, center = false, convexity = 1, ...)') — VERIFY per-node whether it is printed when unset; matters if you clone dumps as an oracle format.
  • OpenCSG uses convexity only on the subtraction/intersection compositing paths; plain unions preview correctly regardless — so 'wrong convexity but looks fine' is common and version-fragile; do not try to reproduce specific artifact pixels, only the accept/ignore contract.
  • Because the artifacts are preview-only, user bug reports historically conflate them with real geometry bugs; documentation for a reimplementation should state explicitly whether its preview can exhibit them (an always-exact preview never will).
  • Passing convexity to a node that does not declare it produces the standard unknown-parameter warning (VERIFY the exact accept list by probing every builtin with convexity=5 under --hardwarnings).
star = [for (i = [0:9]) let (a = i * 36, r = i % 2 == 0 ? 12 : 5) [r * cos(a), r * sin(a)]];
// with convexity 1 the F5 preview of this glitches inside difference(); geometry is unaffected
difference() {
    cylinder(r = 15, h = 3);
    linear_extrude(height = 8, center = true, convexity = 10) polygon(star);
}
Implementer: For an exact-geometry reimplementation: parse convexity on exactly the upstream node set (probe every builtin module with convexity=5 under --hardwarnings to get the authoritative accept list and warning texts), store it, ignore it, and reproduce it in any .csg-style dump you emit. Pin per-node defaults and clamping via dumps. Do NOT reproduce the visual artifacts themselves — they are an OpenCSG implementation detail, not language surface; the language surface is only (a) silent acceptance, (b) defaults/clamps in dumps, (c) zero effect on geometry.

Customizer parameter model

concept 2019.05

Which assignments become Customizer parameters: top-level, main-file-only assignments whose right-hand side is a literal (number, string, boolean, or vector of numbers); applied values behave as trailing top-level assignments.

The Customizer scans the main file's source (comments included) for top-level assignments with literal RHS. Any computed expression (arithmetic, function call, reference to another variable) disqualifies the variable — it evaluates normally but shows no widget. Documented guidance: parameters must appear before the first module/function definition to be recognized (the scanner stops there). include'd files' variables are never shown. When the user changes a value (or a preset/-D applies one), the effect is 'as if assigned at the end of the top-level scope' — winning under last-write-wins; the script cannot distinguish customized values from its own literals.

Edge cases & oddities
  • VERIFY the exact cutoff rule: 'parameters before the first module/function definition' is the documented guidance; pin whether assignments after the first definition are truly ignored or still picked up in 2021.01.
  • verify vs oracle negative number literals (a = -5;) count as literals and get widgets.
  • Vector parameters: vector of NUMBER literals only, and the panel renders up to 4 components; VERIFY behavior for length > 4 (not shown vs first 4).
  • a = true; -> checkbox; a = "text"; -> text box; a = 5; -> spinbox (no comment) — the widget kinds are fixed by RHS type plus comment.
  • An assignment reassigned later in the file: VERIFY which occurrence the panel binds to (last-write-wins evaluation vs first-scan) — pin; users hit this with 'derived = param;' patterns.
  • undef literal RHS: VERIFY (expected: not customizable).
  • Nothing in the language exposes 'was customized'; version()/feature probes cannot detect it either — by design.
  • The GUI hides the panel entirely for files with no recognizable parameters.
  • Parameters inside 'if' blocks or modules are never customizable (not top-level).
  • Comment scanning requires the implementation's lexer to RETAIN or re-scan comments — a comment-discarding lexer cannot implement the Customizer.
wall = 2.4;        // [0.8:0.4:5]
label = "REV A";
show_lid = true;
module body() cube([30, 30, 10]);
body();
Implementer: Implement as a separate comment-aware scan over the main file's token stream, independent of evaluation. This is the machine interface for your MCP parameter discovery — make the scan's output a stable JSON schema (name, type, widget, group, description, default) and oracle-pin the cutoff/duplicate rules.

Customizer preset JSON

concept 2019.05

Preset parameter sets saved beside the model as JSON; applied in the GUI or via CLI -p file.json -P setName.

fileFormatVersionString "1" in 2021.01.
parameterSetsObject mapping set names to objects mapping parameter names to VALUES ENCODED AS STRINGS (numbers as "5", vectors as "[1, 2]", booleans as "true").

The GUI's +/- preset controls read and write this file (default name: same basename as the .scad file with .json). Applying a set assigns each named parameter as-if at the end of the top-level scope (same mechanism as the panel). CLI: 'openscad -p sets.json -P setName -o out.stl model.scad' applies the set headlessly.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Values are JSON STRINGS containing OpenSCAD literal syntax, not native JSON numbers/arrays — VERIFY whether 2021.01 also tolerates native JSON types on read (be liberal only if the oracle is).
  • Vector values as strings include brackets and comma-space: "[1, 2, 3]" — VERIFY exact serialization the GUI writes (your writer should match for round-trip).
  • -P naming a set absent from the file: VERIFY warning text and whether export proceeds with defaults.
  • A set entry naming a parameter that does not exist in the model: VERIFY ignored silently vs warned.
  • A set value for a computed (non-customizable) variable: VERIFY — since application is 'trailing assignment', it may still override; pin.
  • GUI maintains a 'design default values' entry semantics for unsaved state — VERIFY whether that name appears in the JSON or is app-only.
  • String parameter values are stored as the raw string WITHOUT extra quotes inside the JSON string. VERIFY.
  • -p without -P: VERIFY behavior (expected: no set applied).
  • Malformed JSON file: VERIFY error text.
  • $-variables in presets: VERIFY whether the GUI ever writes them and whether -P applies them.
// model.scad: wall = 2; // [1:5]
// model.json: {"fileFormatVersion":"1","parameterSets":{"thick":{"wall":"4"}}}
// shell: openscad -p model.json -P thick -o thick.stl model.scad
cube(1);
Implementer: This file IS your MCP preset interface — implement reader and writer, round-trip-stable, matching the GUI's string-encoded serialization byte-for-byte where feasible. Every VERIFY here is a five-minute CLI probe.

Determinism & compatibility posture

concept 2021.01

The compatibility contract for a 2021.01 reimplementation: PROMISE full determinism (identical script and inputs give identical console output, warning set/order, and geometry), exact tessellation/fragment-formula parity, and mesh-level (solid) equivalence with OpenSCAD 2021.01; NEVER promise byte-identical STL/vertex ordering versus the official binary or across versions, cloned CGAL error strings, or bit-compatible seeded rands(); report version()=[2021,1]/version_num()=20210100 so libraries feature-detect correctly, and pin every uncertainty by differential testing against the official AppImage as an oracle.

version()Returns [year, month] on release builds ([year, month, day] on snapshots), e.g. [2021, 1]; libraries (BOSL2 etc.) feature-test on it, so the reported value gates which library code paths run.
version_num()Returns YYYYMMPP as a number, e.g. 20210100 — the more commonly tested form. Report 20210100 to claim the 2021.01 baseline.
oracle formats.csg (flattened instantiated tree), ECHO (console capture), AST exports from the official 2021.01 binary — the differential-testing ground truth for language semantics before any mesh comparison.
--enable=<feature> modelOpenSCAD gates snapshot features behind explicit flags (lazy-union, manifold, roof, textmetrics); mirror this opt-in model rather than silently enabling snapshot behavior in a 2021.01-baseline implementation.

The contract has two tiers. Tier 1 — self-consistency (absolute): the implementation is a pure function of (script, file inputs, CLI defines, feature flags, $t/camera inputs): every run produces the identical echo/warning/error text in identical depth-first instantiation order, the identical geometry with identical vertex coordinates and identical vertex/triangle ordering, and identical export bytes. No hash-map iteration order, thread scheduling, locale, or wall clock may leak into results; geometry caching must be semantically invisible. Tier 2 — parity with OpenSCAD 2021.01: language-level observables should match the official binary verbatim (echo/str 6-significant-digit double-conversion formatting, warning texts and counts, deprecation and assert/TRACE formats, the fragment formula and tessellation phase contracts, search()'s ragged results, reversed-range swapping), because scripts, CI pipelines with --hardwarnings, and golden tests parse them. Geometry parity is promised at the SOLID level: the same point sets and volumes given the same tessellation contracts — primitive vertex positions should match to double precision since the tessellation formulas are pinned, but the triangulation of polygon interiors, the vertex/triangle ORDER in exports, and near-degenerate CSG outcomes follow kernel internals (CGAL in 2021.01, Manifold in snapshots) that OpenSCAD itself does not keep stable across versions. Therefore: never promise byte-identical STL against the official binary, never promise its internal error messages, and treat rands() bit-compatibility as out of scope unless std::mt19937 plus its distribution mapping is deliberately cloned (std::uniform_real_distribution is not even specified identically across C++ standard libraries). Report the 2021.01 version numbers, add snapshot features only behind explicit flags mirroring --enable, and convert every VERIFY item in this reference into an oracle-diff test before freezing semantics.

Edge cases & oddities
  • PROMISE: identical (script, inputs, version) yields identical console byte stream — echo text, warning set, warning ORDER (depth-first instantiation), and error/TRACE formats. Users' golden tests and MCP-layer parsers depend on it.
  • PROMISE: reproducible tessellation — the fragment formula, circle first-vertex-at-+X CCW inscribed contract, sphere half-step ring phase with no pole vertices, cylinder fragment phase, and rotate_extrude +X seam produce identical vertex coordinates run-to-run and match the oracle to double precision.
  • PROMISE: geometry caching, parallelism, and lazy evaluation are semantically invisible — cached and uncached runs byte-match; instantiation-time echoes fire identically regardless of geometry cache hits.
  • NEVER promise: byte-identical STL/OFF/AMF/3MF versus the official binary — CGAL's vertex/triangle ordering is internal and not stable even across OpenSCAD's own versions; promise mesh-level equivalence (same solid: matching vertex SET for primitives, matching volume/topology for CSG results) instead.
  • NEVER promise: identical triangulation of >3-vertex polyhedron faces, polygon()-with-holes interiors, or text() glyph tessellation — only that the resulting solids/regions are equivalent.
  • NEVER promise: cloned CGAL error/assertion strings on invalid geometry — promise the detection TIMING (failures surface at exact-evaluation/render, not instantiation) and a documented error of your own.
  • NEVER promise near-degenerate CSG parity: exactly-coincident faces, zero-thickness results, and epsilon-scale 2D features may legitimately differ from CGAL/Clipper outcomes; document as unspecified rather than chasing.
  • rands(): with a seed, reproducible WITHIN your implementation forever (freeze your PRNG); bit-matching OpenSCAD requires cloning std::mt19937 AND its uniform_real_distribution mapping — VERIFY: std::uniform_real_distribution output is not pinned by the C++ standard, so the official binary itself may differ across platform stdlibs; recommended posture is to NOT promise cross-implementation rands() equality. Unseeded rands() is nondeterministic by definition — the only sanctioned nondeterminism in the language.
  • version()/version_num() gate real library code paths (BOSL2 feature-detects): report [2021,1]/20210100 for baseline claims. VERIFY: what official snapshots report (snapshot date forms like [2025,x,y]) before emulating one. Note the echo trap: version_num() echoes as 2.02101e+7 under %g — comparisons work, display looks odd; clone as-is.
  • Echo number formatting is part of determinism surface: the 6-significant-digit double-conversion formatter must be frozen and shared by echo/str; snapshots moving to shortest-roundtrip output are a DIFFERENT contract — never mix behind the 2021.01 flag.
  • --hardwarnings parity: the exact SET of emitted warnings is a compatibility gate — an extra warning your implementation adds turns green CI runs red; a missing one hides bugs. Diff warning inventories against the oracle on a torture script and freeze.
  • $t and camera ($vpr/$vpt/$vpd/$vpf) are INPUTS: per-frame determinism given their values; animation runs are deterministic frame-by-frame.
  • Locale/platform independence: '.' decimal separator always; UTF-8 handling identical across platforms; no filesystem ordering leaks (e.g. font enumeration for text() must be deterministic or pinned).
  • Preview vs render divergence (%/#/color()/$preview) is deliberate, specified nondeterminism BETWEEN pipelines, never within one — each pipeline is individually deterministic.
  • Oracle methodology: use the official 2021.01 AppImage headless; compare (1) ECHO/console verbatim, (2) .csg export (flattened normal form — catches evaluator/tessellation-parameter divergence without mesh comparison), (3) mesh equivalence via volume/topology/vertex-set matching with tolerance, never byte-diff. Convert every VERIFY item in this reference into a pinned test before freezing.
  • Snapshot posture: add 2023+ features (manifold backend behavior, roof(), fill(), textmetrics objects, import/export additions, lazy-union) only behind explicit user-visible flags mirroring OpenSCAD's --enable names; never let a flagged feature alter unflagged 2021.01 behavior (including echo formatting and color-through-render).
  • Performance posture (expectation, not semantics): subtree geometry caching, O(1) amortized list building, and fast recursive functions are required for real scripts to complete; document that timing is never part of the compatibility promise.
  • Licensing constraint on methodology: the reference implementation is GPLv2+ and CGAL is GPL — a cleanroom reimplementation pins behavior via the CC BY-SA manual and BLACK-BOX oracle observation only; never port OpenSCAD/CGAL source. Manifold (Apache-2.0) and Clipper2 (BSL-1.0) algorithms/papers are safe references.
echo(version(), version_num());   // ECHO: [2021, 1], 2.02101e+7
assert(version_num() >= 20210100);
r = rands(0, 1, 3, 42);            // seeded: reproducible per implementation
echo(r);                            // freeze YOUR values; don't promise OpenSCAD's
sphere(5, $fn=24);                  // vertex positions: promised; STL bytes: not
Implementer: Engineer determinism structurally: ordered maps everywhere results can leak, a single frozen number formatter, deterministic ID/ordering for generated vertices, and cache designs proven invisible by cache-on/cache-off byte-diff tests in CI. Build the oracle harness first — script corpus, run official 2021.01 headless, capture ECHO/.csg/meshes, diff on every commit; promote each VERIFY item from this reference into that corpus. Freeze your seeded rands() stream as YOUR contract with a documented note that it diverges from OpenSCAD. Publish the compatibility statement in these exact tiers (self-consistency: byte-exact; OpenSCAD parity: console-verbatim + solid-equivalent; explicitly excluded: STL bytes, triangulation, CGAL errors, rands bits, timing) so users and downstream tests target the right guarantees.

diagnostic message surface (WARNING / ERROR / DEPRECATED / TRACE)

concept pre-2015.03 (--hardwarnings: 2019.05)

The console diagnostic grammar: prefixes, file/line suffixes, TRACE unwinding, emission order, and the --hardwarnings contract. Users and CI parse these strings; the emitted SET of warnings is itself compatibility surface.

Messages are emitted in depth-first instantiation/evaluation order, interleaved with ECHO lines, before/independent of geometry rendering. Warnings never halt evaluation (undef propagates instead); ERRORs from assert/fatal conditions halt; DEPRECATED marks legacy constructs; TRACE lines follow an ERROR to unwind the module stack. --hardwarnings promotes any WARNING to a fatal error (serious users run CI with it, so both the texts AND the set of conditions that warn must match).

Edge cases & oddities
  • Known 2021.01 warning conditions to reproduce (texts approximate — VERIFY each verbatim against the binary): variable reassignment ('WARNING: variable a was reassigned...'); unknown variable ('Ignoring unknown variable ...'); unknown module ('Ignoring unknown module ...'); unknown function ('Ignoring unknown function ...'); unknown named parameter; unequal-length or scalar-vector +/- ('undefined operation ...'); division by zero; $fa/$fs clamped to 0.01; deprecated reversed range; deprecated assign(); deprecated triangles=; empty min()/max(); out-of-domain math (2019.05+); range step 0; too many unused parameters — plus the per-format import warnings.
  • VERIFY the file/line suffix pattern per message class: some messages embed ', in file <path>, line <N>' (comma-separated, period-terminated), some historically omitted location — pin each probe case; your MCP layer will regex them.
  • DEPRECATED examples: 'DEPRECATED: The assign() module will be removed in future releases. Use a regular assignment instead.' and the [begin:end] reversed-range message. VERIFY texts.
  • TRACE format: one line per module frame, innermost first, quoting the module name in single quotes. VERIFY whether anonymous frames (for/if groups) appear.
  • Emission count: a warning inside a loop fires per iteration; the GUI console may rate-limit repeated identical lines (app behavior, not language — the CLI stream does not).
  • is_undef(x) on an unknown name suppresses the unknown-variable warning (documented guard); bare reference warns.
  • --hardwarnings stops at the FIRST warning; exit code nonzero. VERIFY exit codes for warning-stop vs error-stop.
  • Message stream destination on CLI is stderr; -o out.echo captures (VERIFY whether warnings are included in .echo).
  • Warnings emitted during expression evaluation (e.g. inside a function) appear at the position of the triggering top-level instantiation in stream order — pin ordering with an interleaved probe.
a = 1; a = 2;        // WARNING: variable a was reassigned ...
b = [1, 2] + 1;      // WARNING (no broadcasting) and b == undef
echo(b);             // ECHO: undef
Implementer: Treat the warning catalog as a frozen enum: one probe .scad per condition, transcript captured from the 2021.01 AppImage, goldens checked verbatim in CI. Build --hardwarnings from day one (first-warning abort) — retrofitting halting semantics into a warn-and-continue evaluator is invasive.

export: 2D formats (DXF, SVG, PDF)

concept DXF pre-2015.03; SVG 2015.03; PDF 2021.01

2D geometry export surface of 2021.01: DXF (pre-2015.03) and SVG (2015.03+), plus PDF (new in 2021.01). Requires the top-level result to be 2D.

The rendered 2D polygon set (outer boundaries plus holes) is written in millimeter units. DXF writes polyline entities; SVG writes a single document with path data (holes via fill-rule); PDF writes a one-page vector document.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Exporting a 3D result to a 2D format: ERROR approximately 'Current top level object is not a 2D object'. VERIFY text.
  • DXF export entity choice (LWPOLYLINE vs POLYLINE vs LINE segments): VERIFY and pin — downstream CAM tools are sensitive.
  • SVG export styling: VERIFY exact fill/stroke attributes (2019.05-era output uses a filled path with stroke; users postprocess it) and the viewBox placement/Y-axis convention (mirrors the import flip).
  • PDF (2021.01): mm-based, single page; VERIFY page-size selection rule in 2021.01 (fit-to-geometry vs fixed A4/Letter) — snapshot PDFs later grew explicit paper-size options, implying 2021.01 had a fixed simple rule.
  • All curves are already tessellated — 2D exports contain line segments only, never true arcs/beziers.
  • % subtrees excluded; # included — same as 3D.
  • Coordinates: full precision vs %g? VERIFY float formatting per format.
  • Empty 2D result: VERIFY error vs empty document.
  • Upstream's 2019.05 release note 'New export file formats: SVG, 3MF, AMF' is sloppy — SVG export shipped in 2015.03; what 2019.05 added on the SVG side was IMPORT.
// shell: openscad -o plate.svg plate.scad
projection(cut = true) translate([0, 0, -1]) cube(10, center = true);
Implementer: Pin DXF entity types and SVG attribute boilerplate with tiny fixtures (one square with one hole). PDF is a from-scratch writer in a zero-dependency build — keep it minimal (one content stream, no compression is legal PDF) and only match OpenSCAD geometrically.

export: 3D mesh formats (STL, OFF, AMF, 3MF)

concept STL/OFF pre-2015.03; AMF 2015.03; 3MF 2019.05

2021.01 3D export surface: binary STL (default for .stl), ASCII STL, OFF, AMF, 3MF — geometry only, no color/units/names beyond fixed boilerplate.

Export always operates on fully rendered (F6-equivalent) geometry; the GUI forces a render first, the CLI renders implicitly for mesh outputs ($preview=false). Format selected by output extension, overridable/disambiguated with --export-format (needed for ASCII STL since .stl defaults to binary, and for stdout output '-o -').

Edge cases & oddities
  • ASCII STL: header 'solid OpenSCAD_Model' / trailer 'endsolid OpenSCAD_Model'; VERIFY per-facet normal values (computed vs zeros) and the exact float formatting of vertex coordinates in 2021.01 (a reproducible-output change landed around then) — golden STL diffs depend on it.
  • Binary STL: 80-byte header (VERIFY content), uint32 count, zero attribute bytes.
  • Exporting when the top-level result is 2D: ERROR approximately 'Current top level object is not a 3D object'. VERIFY text.
  • Exporting empty geometry: VERIFY — error ('Current top level object is empty') vs empty file; pin.
  • % background subtrees are excluded from all exports; # debug subtrees are INCLUDED.
  • color() information is dropped by all four formats in 2021.01 (colored 3MF is Manifold-snapshot-only).
  • 3MF: single object named/structured per lib3mf defaults; units mm. VERIFY object naming/metadata boilerplate if promising byte-level compatibility (recommend not to).
  • AMF export exists but was already de-emphasized; VERIFY it remains available in 2021.01 GUI menu vs CLI-only.
  • Vertex/triangle ORDERING in exports is not stable across OpenSCAD versions — promise mesh-level equivalence, not byte-identical files.
  • Snapshot-only additions: OBJ, WRL, POV exports; colored 3MF.
// shell: openscad -o part.stl part.scad
// shell: openscad -o part.stl --export-format asciistl part.scad
cube(10);
Implementer: Decide and document a normalization story: your own exports should be deterministic run-to-run; compare against OpenSCAD via mesh hashing (canonicalized vertex welding), not file bytes. Only ASCII STL text formatting is worth pinning byte-for-byte if your tests parse it.

export: debug/text formats (CSG, AST, TERM, ECHO, NEF3, NEFDBG)

concept pre-2015.03 (csg/ast/term/echo availability VERIFY per-format introduction)

Text-form exports of the evaluation pipeline. The .csg flattened tree is the single most useful differential-testing oracle format; .echo captures console text; .ast dumps the parse; nef3/nefdbg are CGAL internals.

.csg: the fully evaluated instantiation tree with every variable, loop, function call, and module resolved into a canonical nesting of built-in primitives (group(){...}, cube(size=[...],center=...), multmatrix([[...]]){...}, circle($fn=...,r=...), etc.) — no geometry kernel is run, so it is fast and deterministic. .ast: re-serialization of the parsed source. .term: the CSG term expression dump. .echo: the console message stream (ECHO lines and messages) written to a file. .nef3/.nefdbg: CGAL Nef polyhedron dumps (no reason to reimplement beyond accepting/refusing the extension).

Edge cases & oddities
  • .csg number formatting: VERIFY precision (it is NOT the 6-digit %g of echo; .csg historically prints more digits) — pin exactly, since .csg diffing is your oracle channel.
  • .csg represents modifiers: VERIFY how *,!,#,% appear (dropped subtrees for *, marker wrappers for #/%).
  • .csg is itself valid OpenSCAD input (re-importable via rename to .scad) — a strong self-consistency test.
  • .echo content: VERIFY whether WARNING/ERROR lines are included or only ECHO lines; pin, since CI parses it.
  • .ast normalizes whitespace/comments away — comments (and thus Customizer annotations) do NOT survive; VERIFY.
  • nef3/nefdbg: recommend rejecting with a clear 'unsupported in this implementation' error rather than emulating CGAL dumps; they are kernel-internal.
  • All of these run with $preview=false. VERIFY for .csg (no geometry evaluation happens, but the binding is still set — pin by echoing $preview during a .csg export).
  • STEP/IGES/BREP are NOT supported in any OpenSCAD version, import or export — do not promise them.
// shell: openscad -o model.csg model.scad
for (i = [0:2]) translate([i * 10, 0, 0]) cube(5);
Implementer: Implement .csg and .echo first — they are your differential-test backbone against the 2021.01 AppImage. Match the .csg canonical parameter spellings and float format verbatim (probe with every primitive). .ast/.term optional; nef* stubs.

export: PNG image

concept pre-2015.03

Renders the scene to a PNG using either the preview pipeline (default; $preview=true) or full render (--render; $preview=false).

--imgsizeOutput resolution W,H; default 512,512.
--cameraEither tx,ty,tz,rx,ry,rz,dist (maps to $vpt/$vpr/$vpd) or eyex,eyey,eyez,cx,cy,cz gimbal-free form.
--colorschemeNamed GUI color scheme (e.g. Cornfield default, Tomorrow, DeepOcean...).
--projectiono(rtho) or p(erspective).

PNG export is the one CLI path where preview semantics apply by default: $preview=true, % background geometry visible (translucent gray), # highlights pink, color() visible. With --render, geometry is fully evaluated and $preview=false. --autocenter and --viewall adjust the camera to the model bounds.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Default camera when --camera absent: the $vp* defaults ([55,0,25], [0,0,0], 140, 22.5) as possibly modified by top-level $vp* assignments in the script.
  • $preview: plain PNG export -> true; --render -> false; VERIFY --preview=throwntogether also true with the throwntogether renderer.
  • Pixel-exact PNG comparison against OpenSCAD is NOT a sane compatibility target (GPU/AA variance); OpenSCAD's own test suite uses fuzzy image diffing.
  • Color scheme names are app surface; unknown name: VERIFY warning + default fallback.
  • 2D geometry PNG export: renders the thin-slab preview look. VERIFY.
  • verify vs oracle -o out.png combined with --export-format png for stdout.
// shell: openscad -o shot.png --imgsize=800,600 --camera=0,0,0,55,0,25,140 model.scad
color("tomato") cube(10, center = true);
Implementer: Treat PNG as app-level, not language-level, compatibility: match the observable $preview/%/#/color semantics, not pixels. Implement fuzzy-diff harness if you want image regression tests.

feature flags & lazy-union (--enable)

concept snapshot mechanism (2019.05+ for most flags)

Snapshot features are gated per-run by --enable=<flag> (or Preferences > Features); one of them, lazy-union, changes core language semantics.

Flags relevant to a reimplementation: lazy-union removes the implicit union at TOP LEVEL, so multiple top-level solids export as separate objects (multi-object 3MF) instead of one fused solid; manifold selects the fast geometry backend (semantics-neutral except speed and color-through-render); roof, textmetrics, import-function etc. gate their named features. Unknown flags error.

Edge cases & oddities
  • With lazy-union OFF (baseline), every multi-solid top level is one implicit union — scripts and exporters may silently depend on either behavior.
  • verify vs oracle the full flag list of the targeted snapshot before mirroring; flags come and go between snapshots.
  • Feature-flag state is not visible from the language (no builtin reports it) — scripts cannot branch on it; only version()/version_num() gate library behavior.
// with --enable=lazy-union, these export as TWO 3MF objects:
cube(5);
translate([10,0,0]) sphere(3);
Implementer: Mirror the mechanism (named opt-in flags) rather than individual flags; implement lazy-union early — it is cheap and unlocks multi-object export.

function-value calling conventions

concept 2021.01

Call syntax generalizes in 2021.01: any postfix-position expression that evaluates to a function value is callable — variables, parenthesized expressions, vector elements, and results of other calls (currying/chaining).

callee-expressionIdentifier, (expr), v[i], v.x, or f(...) — anything in postfix/primary position.
argsMatched to the function value's parameters with the standard rules (positional, named, defaults, $-injection).

For a call name(args) with an IDENTIFIER callee, 2021.01 must resolve between the function namespace (named definitions) and the variable namespace (function values). For non-identifier callees ((expr)(x), list[i](x), f(x)(y)) only the value path exists. Arguments are evaluated eagerly in the caller's scope; the body runs in the closure's captured environment; $-variables flow from the caller's dynamic environment.

Edge cases & oddities
  • VERIFY resolution precedence when BOTH 'function f(x) = 1;' and 'f = function(x) 2;' exist in scope: pin which one f(0) calls in 2021.01 (believed: a local variable holding a function takes precedence over an outer named function, and — VERIFY — a same-scope named function beats the variable; probe all scope combinations against the oracle and clone exactly).
  • Calling a non-function value: v = 5; v(1) — warning approx 'WARNING: Can't call function on number' (or unknown-function if no variable matches) and the call yields undef — VERIFY exact text per type (undef callee, string callee, vector callee).
  • Chained/curried calls work: adder = function(a) function(b) a + b; echo(adder(2)(3)); // 5 — inner literal captures a.
  • Vector-of-functions dispatch: fs = [function(x) x, function(x) -x]; fs[1](3) == -3.
  • Named and $-arguments work on function values exactly as on named functions, including unknown-named-arg warnings — VERIFY warning text parity.
  • A call is still not a statement: 'f(1);' at statement level parses as a MODULE instantiation of f (module namespace), not a function-value call — the two worlds never mix.
  • verify vs oracle member-access callee (obj.x(args)) — .x is vector swizzle in 2021.01, so v.x(1) calls the function stored as the first vector element.
compose = function (f, g) function (x) f(g(x)); inc = function (x) x + 1; echo(compose(inc, inc)(5)); // ECHO: 7
Implementer: Grammar change: postfix call must accept any primary expression, while statement-level instantiation stays module-namespace-only. The identifier-callee precedence question is THE oracle probe to run first — libraries with shadowed helper names depend on whichever answer 2021.01 gives.

Geometry model

concept 2015.03

Geometry exists only in the instantiation tree, never as a value; every multi-statement grouping (top level, module bodies, blocks, transform children, for iterations) is an implicit union; 2D geometry lives at z=0 and may never mix with 3D in one boolean; and the language has two observable evaluation pipelines — approximate GPU preview (F5/OpenCSG, governed by convexity, $preview=true) and exact render (F6/CGAL) — whose differences (%, #, color(), late manifold errors) are part of the compatibility surface.

implicit unionAny point where multiple geometry statements share a scope — top level, module bodies, { } blocks, if/for/let bodies, children of a transform — implicitly unions them. intersection_for exists precisely because wrapping for in intersection() intersects one pre-unioned blob.
dimensionalityEvery node is 2D or 3D. Booleans/hull/minkowski require uniform dimensionality; linear_extrude/rotate_extrude accept only 2D and produce 3D; projection accepts only 3D and produces 2D. These are the only bridges.
convexityAccepted on polyhedron, polygon, linear_extrude, rotate_extrude, surface, import, render, minkowski, resize, ...: 'max front-facing surfaces a ray can cross'. Affects ONLY OpenCSG preview correctness, never exported geometry — must be parsed everywhere, may be ignored by an always-exact engine.
$previewtrue in F5 preview / --preview PNG export, false in F6 render / mesh exports (2019.05+). Scripts branch on it; an implementation must set it coherently for its own pipeline.
modifiers * ! # %Parser-level prefixes on one instantiation: * disables the subtree everywhere; ! makes it the only evaluated subtree (ancestor transforms still apply); # includes it in results but tints it pink in preview; % shows it gray in preview but EXCLUDES it from render/CSG/exports.
render()Semantically the identity; forces exact geometry for its subtree even during preview (the classic OpenCSG-artifact workaround). hull(), minkowski(), and resize() also force child geometry evaluation.
geometry cachesOpenSCAD memoizes evaluated geometry per identical subtree (GeometryCache/CGALCache); repeated instantiations of the same module with the same arguments must be cache hits — a de facto performance contract.

OpenSCAD scripts do not compute geometry values; they INSTANTIATE a tree. Statements and expressions are disjoint grammars: 'x = cube(1);' is a syntax error, modules are not first-class (only function values are, since 2021.01), and geometry can never be stored, compared, or measured from the language (resize/hull react to geometry internally, but no measurement is exposed to expressions). Instantiation happens depth-first; echo/assert/warnings fire during instantiation, before geometry evaluation, and their order is observable. Every grouping construct implicitly unions its children — top level, module bodies, { } blocks, if branches, let bodies, for iterations (hence intersection_for), and the children of transforms. 2D geometry is dimensionally 2D in the CSG tree, conceptually at z=0; preview draws it as a thin slab (visual convention only). Mixing 2D and 3D children in union/difference/intersection/hull/minkowski is an ERROR ('Mixing 2D and 3D objects is not supported'); linear_extrude and rotate_extrude are the only 2D-to-3D bridges and projection() the only 3D-to-2D bridge. Two pipelines exist in the reference implementation: F5 preview composes CSG approximately on the GPU via OpenCSG (accuracy bounded by each node's convexity parameter) with $preview=true, while F6 render computes exact meshes via CGAL (Manifold in 2023+ snapshots) with $preview=false. Preview-vs-render differences are language surface: % subtrees appear (translucent gray) only in preview and are excluded from render and every export; # subtrees are highlighted pink in preview but fully included in render/exports; * removes a subtree everywhere; ! restricts evaluation to one subtree (with its ancestor transform chain); color() affects only preview output in 2021.01; non-manifold polyhedra/imports frequently succeed in preview and only fail at render; render() forces exactness locally. An always-exact reimplementation must still emulate the observable semantics: flip $preview appropriately, honor %/#/!/* per-pipeline behavior, and accept convexity everywhere. The 2D subsystem runs in a separate fixed-point polygon-clipping kernel (Clipper) whose quantization differs from the 3D pipeline — micro-scale 2D precision behavior is not the same as 3D.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Implicit-union sites, exhaustively: file top level, module bodies, { } blocks, if/else branch bodies, for and intersection_for iteration bodies (for unions ITERATIONS; intersection_for intersects them), let statement bodies, and multi-child transform/boolean arguments. Wrapping for in intersection() does NOT intersect iterations — it intersects the single already-unioned result (the reason intersection_for exists).
  • 'x = cube(1);' is a syntax/semantic error: instantiations are statements, not expressions. VERIFY the exact parser error text. Likewise modules cannot be passed, stored, or returned (no module literals in any stable release).
  • Mixing 2D and 3D in any boolean/hull/minkowski: ERROR 'Mixing 2D and 3D objects is not supported'. VERIFY exact prefix/casing and whether it aborts the whole compile or just that subtree (believed: subtree yields empty and an ERROR is logged — pin).
  • verify vs oracle a 2D and a 3D object both at top level — believed to hit the same implicit-union mixing error at F6/export while F5 may draw both; pin the behavior per pipeline.
  • linear_extrude/rotate_extrude with a 3D child: ERROR (VERIFY text); projection with a 2D child: VERIFY (believed warning/ignore or error — pin).
  • 2D is drawn in preview as a thin slab (historically ~1 unit tall visual); this is cosmetic — the tree node is 2D and exports as 2D only. Exporting 2D to STL or 3D to DXF/SVG errors: VERIFY texts, approx 'Current top level object is not a 3D object' / '...not a 2D object'.
  • Exporting an empty top-level result is an error: VERIFY text (approx 'ERROR: Nothing to export' or 'No top level geometry to render').
  • Degenerate primitives (cube(0), cylinder(h=0), circle(-1)) emit WARNING and contribute empty geometry; evaluation continues. Empty children: union ignores them; difference with empty FIRST child yields empty; difference minus empty subtrahends is unchanged; intersection with any empty child yields empty. VERIFY minkowski with an empty child — behavior changed across versions (warning-and-ignore vs empty result); pin for 2021.01.
  • Modifier semantics per pipeline: % = preview-visible (translucent gray), EXCLUDED from render/CSG/exports; # = preview-highlighted (translucent red/pink), INCLUDED in render/exports; * = excluded everywhere; ! = only that subtree evaluated, ancestor transforms still applied. Modifiers compose (%*, #translate(...)); VERIFY resolution when multiple ! appear (believed the first one encountered wins) and nested ! inside a !-subtree.
  • % and # inside hull()/minkowski(): inner modifiers/colors are dropped by these operations. VERIFY exact interaction (e.g. hull() over a % child).
  • color() is preview-only in 2021.01: F6 render, STL/OFF/AMF/3MF exports drop it. Snapshots with the Manifold backend carry color through render and into 3MF export — a deliberate divergence to gate behind a feature flag.
  • Non-manifold or self-intersecting polyhedron()/import() geometry typically previews fine and only fails at F6 with CGAL errors. Do NOT promise cloning CGAL's error strings — promise detection timing (validity enforced at exact-evaluation time, not instantiation time).
  • convexity is accepted (and geometrically inert) wherever listed; wrong/low convexity in OpenSCAD produces EXPECTED preview artifacts in difference()-heavy models (the render()-wrapping folk fix). An always-exact implementation simply never shows the artifacts — an accepted, user-visible improvement, but it must still parse convexity everywhere.
  • render(convexity) is the identity on geometry; must be accepted with children of either dimensionality. VERIFY: render() of 2D children passes 2D through (believed yes, with the result cached as polygons).
  • Geometry caching is a de facto contract: a for loop stamping the same module 1000x must evaluate its mesh once (OpenSCAD keys caches on the subtree identity). Without memoization, real-world scripts (recursive trees, grids) run orders of magnitude slower — treat as required. VERIFY cache-key semantics only if exposing cache controls.
  • lazy-union (experimental, 2021.01+, --enable=lazy-union): removes the TOP-LEVEL implicit union so multiple top-level solids export as separate objects (multi-object 3MF). Off by default; decide whether to mirror the flag.
  • 2D pipeline quantization: booleans/offset run through Clipper-style fixed-point clipping with its own coordinate grid; results at micrometer scales differ from the float 3D pipeline. Observable in vertex coordinates of exported SVG/DXF. VERIFY the effective quantum (Clipper integer scale factor) if promising coordinate-exact 2D output.
  • Tessellation phase contracts (observable in every export): circle = INSCRIBED polygon, first vertex at angle 0 on +X, CCW; sphere = N meridians with ceil(N/2) latitude rings at half-step phase offsets (no pole vertices); cylinder = fragment 0 at +X, count from max(r1,r2); rotate_extrude seam fixed at the +X half-plane. Real models (hex nuts via $fn=6, r/cos(180/n) hole compensation) depend on these — clone exactly.
  • polyhedron() faces wind CLOCKWISE viewed from outside (reverse of STL/OBJ convention); polygon() holes come from nested paths (even-odd/nesting rule — pin exact overlap semantics); both defer validity errors to exact-evaluation time.
  • echo/assert/warning emission happens at instantiation (depth-first, pre-geometry); console order is observable surface. Geometry evaluation order behind it is not observable except through timing and cache effects.
  • The .csg export is a flattened normal form of the instantiated tree (transforms/booleans/primitives with resolved parameters) — the single best differential-testing oracle format; AST export reproduces parsed source; ECHO export captures the console stream.
  • Coordinate system: right-handed, +Z up in the default view, unitless (conventionally mm). scale() with negative factors mirrors and can invert face orientation — the exact kernel must handle inverted winding. VERIFY behavior of scale([1,1,0]) on 3D geometry at render time (degenerate — believed CGAL error or empty; pin).
module pin() { cylinder(h=8, r=1, $fn=24); }
difference() {
  cube([20,20,5]);
  for (x=[5,15], y=[5,15]) translate([x,y,-1]) cylinder(h=7, r=2, $fn=32);
}
%translate([0,0,12]) cube(6);   // reference: preview only
#translate([25,0,0]) pin();     // highlighted, still exported
if ($preview) echo("preview pipeline");
Implementer: Model the pipeline distinction explicitly even in a single always-exact engine: a boolean 'preview mode' flag drives $preview, % inclusion, # tinting, and color() visibility; keep the render-time result of a %-subtree out of every export path. Accept-and-ignore convexity via a shared parameter table. Implement subtree-identity geometry memoization from day one (hash of node type + resolved params + child hashes). Oracle probes: mixing-error text and per-pipeline behavior, multiple-! resolution, minkowski-empty-child, 2D/3D top-level mixing, export-dimension error texts, empty-export error, scale-to-zero at render. Use .csg export diffing as the primary compatibility harness before any mesh comparison.

import: 3D mesh formats (STL, OFF, AMF, 3MF)

concept STL/OFF pre-2015.03; AMF 2019.05; 3MF 2019.05

The four 3D mesh read formats of 2021.01 and their per-format feature subsets and quirks.

STL: both ASCII and binary, autodetected. OFF: Geomview text format. AMF: XML mesh format. 3MF: ZIP+XML container read via lib3mf, geometry only in 2021.01. All import as raw triangle/polygon meshes with no unit metadata applied — coordinates are taken as millimeter-equivalent model units.

Edge cases & oddities
  • STL sniffing: a file beginning with 'solid' is tried as ASCII; binary STLs whose 80-byte header happens to start with 'solid' must still load — VERIFY the exact fallback heuristic (ASCII parse failure -> binary reparse) and clone it; this is a real-world file population.
  • STL binary: little-endian, 80-byte header ignored, uint32 triangle count, 50-byte records; attribute byte count ignored.
  • STL ASCII: 'solid'/'facet normal'/'outer loop' grammar; stored normals are IGNORED (recomputed from winding). VERIFY.
  • STL vertices are matched by position to stitch the mesh; VERIFY whether any epsilon/grid snapping applies at import (suspect exact match).
  • Degenerate (zero-area) triangles: VERIFY dropped vs kept; affects downstream CSG errors.
  • OFF: header 'OFF', counts line, vertex lines, face lines; per-face color fields tolerated but ignored. VERIFY comment handling ('#').
  • AMF: reads object/mesh/vertices/triangles; curved-triangle and material/color extensions ignored; multiple objects: VERIFY unioned into one solid vs first-only.
  • AMF unit attribute: VERIFY honored (scaling) vs ignored in 2021.01.
  • 3MF (2019.05+ read): geometry only in 2021.01 — colors/materials dropped; multiple build items: VERIFY unioned; build transforms: VERIFY applied; units: 3MF is mm-based, VERIFY conversion of other unit declarations.
  • All four: non-manifold input previews fine, fails at F6/CSG with CGAL-era errors — timing is observable.
  • OBJ is snapshot-only (2023+); in 2021.01 '.obj' hits the unsupported-format error.
  • Version asymmetry: AMF EXPORT is 2015.03, but AMF IMPORT only arrived in 2019.05 alongside SVG and 3MF import.
import("widget.3mf");
translate([40, 0, 0]) import("widget.stl");
Implementer: STL sniffing and mesh stitching are the highest-risk items — build a corpus (ASCII, binary, binary-starting-with-'solid', degenerate triangles, unreferenced vertices) and pin behavior. For AMF/3MF pin multi-object and unit handling via oracle before writing the XML/ZIP parsers.

import: DXF

concept pre-2015.03

2D import of a limited DXF entity subset: LINE, POLYLINE/LWPOLYLINE, CIRCLE, ARC. Splines are unsupported in 2021.01. Yields 2D polygons at z=0.

layerLayer name to import; omitted/empty imports entities from all layers. VERIFY default-all behavior.

Entities are read in file units taken as model units (no unit conversion), connected into closed loops by endpoint matching, and converted to 2D polygon geometry with even-odd style hole nesting as with polygon(). Circles and arcs are tessellated using the $fn/$fa/$fs in scope at the import() call. This DXF reader is the legacy heart of OpenSCAD's original 2D pipeline (dxf_dim/dxf_cross read the same files for metadata).

Edge cases & oddities
  • Unsupported entities (SPLINE, ELLIPSE — VERIFY ellipse, TEXT, DIMENSION geometry, HATCH, INSERT/blocks) are skipped with a WARNING approximately 'Unsupported DXF entity ...' per entity type. VERIFY exact text and which list is warned vs silently ignored.
  • Open contours (endpoints that do not close a loop) trigger a warning and the open chain is dropped. VERIFY text ('dxf_data: not implemented' / 'open polyline' class messages).
  • Endpoint matching uses grid quantization to decide coincidence — micro-gap DXFs may or may not close; VERIFY the tolerance and clone it (real-world CAD exports sit right at this edge).
  • POLYLINE bulge values (arc segments inside polylines): VERIFY supported and tessellation rule.
  • layer= naming a nonexistent layer: VERIFY empty result + warning vs silent empty.
  • Only 2D: entities' Z coordinates ignored/flattened. VERIFY.
  • $fn at the call affects arc/circle fragment counts — same fragment formula as circle(). VERIFY radius fed per-arc.
  • DXF versions: old-style (R12-era) entity coding is the well-supported path; newer DXF features simply fall in the unsupported bucket.
  • dxf_dim(file, name, layer, origin, scale) and dxf_cross(file, layer, origin, scale) remain LIVE functions in 2021.01 reading DIMENSION entities / crossing lines from the same files — decide support or stub-with-warning, but parse them.
linear_extrude(height = 5)
  import("plate.dxf", layer = "outline");
Implementer: Build a DXF probe deck (lines-only loop, lwpolyline closed flag, bulges, arcs, circle, spline, two layers, micro-gaps) and diff the resulting .csg polygons against the oracle. The endpoint-matching tolerance and per-entity warning texts are the compatibility surface.

import: SVG

concept 2019.05

2D import (2019.05+) of SVG paths and basic shapes into polygons; fill-rule honored, <text> ignored, px units scaled by dpi.

dpiConversion for px-denominated lengths: 1px = 25.4/dpi mm. Default 96.

Supported elements: <path> (full command set including cubic/quadratic beziers and arcs, flattened to segments), <rect> (including rounded corners), <circle>, <ellipse>, <line>, <polyline>, <polygon>, with <g> group transforms applied. fill-rule (nonzero vs evenodd) is respected when resolving self-intersections and holes. Strokes are NOT expanded (stroke properties ignored); only fill geometry imports. Physical units (mm, cm, in, pt, pc) convert exactly; px converts via dpi. The Y axis is flipped so the drawing appears with the same visual orientation as in an SVG viewer.

Edge cases & oddities
  • <text> elements are ignored — VERIFY whether silently or with a warning; convert text to paths before exporting from the drawing tool (documented guidance).
  • VERIFY placement convention in 2021.01: where the SVG document/viewBox origin lands in OpenSCAD coordinates after the Y flip (top-left at origin with content in y<0, vs content shifted into y>0) — pin via oracle with a known rect; snapshots later added center= which implies the 2021.01 default is NOT centered.
  • viewBox + width/height scaling honored; VERIFY behavior when width/height are missing (viewBox-only) or when only px sizes given.
  • CSS support is minimal: presentation attributes and inline style fill/fill-rule/display honored; VERIFY <style> blocks and classes (expected: limited/none in 2021.01).
  • display:none / visibility: VERIFY respected.
  • <use>, <defs>, clipPath, mask, gradients: VERIFY — expected unsupported/ignored (fills treated as filled regardless of paint).
  • Curve flattening consumes $fn/$fa/$fs at the call site. VERIFY the radius/parameter fed to the fragment formula for beziers vs arcs.
  • Zero-area subpaths and unclosed subpaths: VERIFY (SVG fill semantics implicitly close subpaths — expected: implicit closing).
  • dpi affects ONLY px-unit lengths; a file authored in mm ignores dpi entirely.
  • Snapshot-only: id=/layer= selection of specific elements; absent in 2021.01.
linear_extrude(height = 2)
  import("logo.svg", dpi = 96);
Implementer: The placement/Y-flip convention is the most-reported user surprise — pin it first with a 10x10 rect at viewBox origin. Then fill-rule tests (self-intersecting star nonzero vs evenodd). SVG parsing scope should be documented as exactly this subset; do not silently support more (differential tests would diverge).

iteration and control-flow model (absences)

concept 2015.03

The language has no imperative control flow whatsoever: all iteration is data-driven (for over iterables, comprehensions, recursion) and every function is a single expression.

There is no while loop, no break/continue, no return statement (a function's body IS its return expression), no goto, and no mutable variables. Early exit is expressed with the ternary; accumulation with recursion or C-style comprehension for; filtering with comprehension if. Any appearance of these keywords is simply an unknown identifier (they are not reserved).

Edge cases & oddities
  • while/break/continue/return are NOT reserved words: 'return = 1;' is a legal assignment and 'while(1)' parses as an unknown-module instantiation (warned and ignored) — VERIFY both.
  • Infinite loops are still reachable via non-terminating C-style comprehension for and via non-tail recursion (bounded by the recursion guard) — see recursion entry.
  • The absence of builtins for map/filter/fold/sort means recursion + comprehensions are the ecosystem's only tools; interpreter performance on those patterns is compatibility-critical.
function sum(v, i = 0) = i >= len(v) ? 0 : v[i] + sum(v, i + 1); echo(sum([1, 2, 3]));
Implementer: Do not add extensions here; scripts feature-test versions, and a superset dialect breaks the compatibility contract. Ensure unknown-module handling covers 'while(...)' gracefully.

library search path resolution

concept pre-2015.03

How include/use (and data-file consumers) locate files: current file's directory first, then the OPENSCADPATH environment variable, then the per-user library directory, then bundled libraries.

Relative paths in include/use are resolved against the directory of the file containing the directive; on miss, each entry of OPENSCADPATH (colon-separated on POSIX, semicolon on Windows) is tried; then the platform user library folder (Windows: My Documents\OpenSCAD\libraries; macOS: ~/Documents/OpenSCAD/libraries; Linux: ~/.local/share/OpenSCAD/libraries); then the installation's bundled libraries (which ship MCAD). First hit wins. Absolute paths bypass the search.

Edge cases & oddities
  • import(), surface(), and text(font=) data files resolve relative to the invoking script's directory; VERIFY whether the library search chain also applies to these data files (seed flags this as unverified; pin per-consumer).
  • OPENSCADPATH separator is platform-dependent (: vs ;). VERIFY empty-entry handling.
  • Nested includes/uses resolve relative to THEIR containing file, not the main file — critical for libraries that include their own submodules (BOSL2 does).
  • MCAD ships in the bundled directory and is GPL/LGPL — a reimplementation must not bundle it without license review; scripts saying include <MCAD/...> rely on the bundled tier existing.
  • Case sensitivity follows the filesystem; no normalization performed.
  • A directory (not file) match: VERIFY behavior (expected: open fails, falls through / warns).
  • The GUI also honors 'Show library folder'; app-level, not language.
// with OPENSCADPATH=/opt/scadlibs and /opt/scadlibs/util/helpers.scad present:
use <util/helpers.scad>
Implementer: Make the search order a documented, testable function; expose the resolved path in verbose logs so users can debug shadowing (a local file silently shadowing a library file is a common support issue). Pin the data-file question with oracle probes before promising it.

namespaces, hoisting, and shadowing

concept pre-2015.03

One identifier can simultaneously name a module, a function, and a variable; call syntax position (statement vs expression) selects the namespace; definitions are hoisted within their scope, duplicates warn with last-one-wins.

Statement-position name(...) resolves in the module namespace; expression-position name(...) resolves in the function namespace (in 2021.01, falling back to / interacting with variables holding function values — see calling-conventions entry); bare name resolves in the variable namespace. Each scope contributes to all three; lookup proceeds outward lexically. Module/function definitions are hoisted (usable textually before definition within the scope); variables follow the slot-reorder rule instead.

Edge cases & oddities
  • x = 1; function x(a) = a; module x() cube(); — all three coexist; echo(x) prints 1, echo(x(2)) prints 2, x(); makes a cube.
  • Duplicate module or function definition in one scope: warning, LAST definition wins (mirrors variable last-write-wins) — VERIFY warning text.
  • User definitions shadow builtins in both callable namespaces — VERIFY precedence for e.g. 'module cube()' and 'function sin()'; also VERIFY whether ANY builtins are un-shadowable.
  • Inner-scope definitions shadow outer ones lexically; include<> merges definitions into the including scope (subject to duplicate-warn/last-wins), use<> populates only the two callable namespaces of the using file's top scope.
  • Hoisting + lexical capture: a hoisted function reading a scope variable sees the slot-reordered final value, even if the call textually precedes the assignment.
  • Keywords (module, function, if, else, for, let, each, echo, assert, include, use, true, false, undef) cannot be identifiers in ANY namespace — echo and assert joined the reserved set with their 2019.05 expression forms and are NOT shadowable in 2021.01; other builtin names (version, intersection_for, cube, sin, ...) are ordinary identifiers and shadowable.
x = 1; function x(n) = n * 10; module x() sphere(1); echo(x, x(2)); x();
Implementer: Three maps per scope frame. The statement/expression position rule does all disambiguation — no unified symbol table. Probe builtin shadowing and the reserved-word list against the oracle parser.

no string + concatenation

concept pre-2015.03

There is NO + concatenation for strings — arithmetic operators on strings yield undef with a console warning; str() is the only concatenation mechanism.

All arithmetic operators (+ - * / % and unary minus) are undefined for string operands. Applying them yields undef and emits a console WARNING, and evaluation continues (undef then propagates through the expression). Concatenation is exclusively via str(a, b, ...); character-level assembly can also go through chr(concat(...)) over code points.

Edge cases & oddities
  • "a" + "b" -> undef with WARNING. Approximate 2021.01 text: 'WARNING: undefined operation ("a" + "b") in file <path>, line <N>' — VERIFY exact operand rendering (values vs type names) against the oracle; users and CI parse this text.
  • Same undef+WARNING for "a" - "b", "a" * 2, "a" / 2, "a" % 2, and unary -"a".
  • string + number, number + string: undef + WARNING (no coercion in either direction).
  • The undef result is falsy and poisons downstream arithmetic silently after the single warning at the failing operation.
  • Under --hardwarnings the warning is FATAL — a reimplementation that 'helpfully' implements + concatenation both diverges observably and hides bugs scripts rely on catching.
  • str("a", "b") == "ab" is the canonical replacement; repeated in loops it is the standard (O(n^2) if naive) string-building pattern.
  • == on strings still works normally; only arithmetic is undefined.
x = "a" + "b"; echo(x, str("a", "b"));
Implementer: Emit exactly one warning per failing operation evaluation (per loop iteration if in a loop). Pin the warning wording verbatim via oracle; it is part of the observable surface.

number formatting (6 significant digits)

concept precision (6 sig digits) pre-2015.03; formatter changed to double-conversion in 2021.01 (unpadded exponents, -0 prints 0)

All user-visible number-to-text conversion in 2021.01 uses %g with 6 significant digits: trailing zeros trimmed, no decimal point for integer values, scientific notation for large/small magnitudes — famously rounding large integers.

Rules (2021.01, double-conversion library at 6 significant digits): fixed notation for 1e-5 <= |x| < 1e6, scientific otherwise; lowercase 'e' with explicit sign and NO zero padding of the exponent; at most 6 significant digits; trailing zeros trimmed and a bare decimal point stripped; -0 prints as 0 (UNIQUE_ZERO). Examples: 1/3 -> 0.333333; PI -> 3.14159; 10.0 -> 10; 100000 -> 100000; 1000000 -> 1e+6; 1234567 -> 1.23457e+6; 0.0001 -> 0.0001; 0.00001 -> 0.00001; 1.1e-6 -> 1.1e-6; 1e100 -> 1e+100; 0.1+0.2 -> 0.3 (error hidden by rounding). inf -> inf, -inf -> -inf, nan -> nan. Pre-2021 releases used printf %g (zero-padded exponents like 1e+06 and scientific already at 1e-5, printing 1e-05) — 2021.01 is exactly where the formatter changed.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Large-integer trap: any |x| >= 1e6 goes scientific AND gets rounded to 6 digits — echo(1234567) prints 1.23457e+6; str() inherits this, so user string-building silently corrupts big numbers. Must clone.
  • Exponent format: lowercase 'e', explicit sign, NO zero padding — '1e+6', '1.1e-6', '1e+100'. (Pre-2021 %g output padded to two digits: '1e+06'.)
  • -0 prints as '0' (UNIQUE_ZERO flag in the 2021.01 formatter); the sign survives arithmetic — 1/-0 == -inf.
  • 0.5 -> 0.5; the small-number scientific switch is BELOW 1e-5: 0.0001 and 0.00001 both print fixed ('0.0001', '0.00001'); 1.1e-6 prints '1.1e-6'.
  • Rounding is round-half-even per printf? VERIFY the tie behavior at the 6th digit (printf uses the current FP rounding, effectively round-half-even on the decimal conversion) — matters only for exact-tie goldens.
  • true/false/undef are NOT numbers; booleans print true/false.
  • The same formatter renders numbers INSIDE vectors and named-argument values.
  • .csg export and STL/AMF writers use DIFFERENT (higher) precision — do not unify the formatters. VERIFY each writer's precision separately.
  • Dev snapshots have moved toward shortest-round-trip full precision output — 2021.01 baseline stays 6-digit double-conversion output; only chase snapshot behavior behind an explicit flag.
  • str(1/3) == "0.333333" exactly — string-comparison user code depends on it.
echo(1/3, PI, 10.0, 1234567, 0.00001, 0.1 + 0.2);
// ECHO: 0.333333, 3.14159, 10, 1.23457e+6, 0.00001, 0.3
Implementer: Implement one canonical dtoa-%g(6) routine, byte-identical to glibc printf %g for the reachable domain, and use it for echo/str/assert/warnings ONLY. Golden-test a sweep: powers of ten 1e-7..1e7, ties, negatives, -0, inf, nan, and the 999999.5 boundary.

Numbers & output formatting

concept 2015.03

Every number is an IEEE-754 double; all angles everywhere are degrees; echo/str format numbers %g-style with 6 significant digits (so large integers go scientific and get rounded, and float error is hidden); domain errors and type mismatches produce nan/undef with WARNINGs instead of halting; the exact set and text of warnings is compatibility surface because --hardwarnings promotes any warning to a fatal error.

number representationf64 only — no integer type, no bignum; integer-looking values are doubles and lose exactness silently past 2^53.
numeric literalsDecimal and exponent forms (1.5, 1e-3); NO hex/binary/octal; no literals for inf or nan (they arise from arithmetic only).
degrees conventionsin/cos/tan take degrees; asin/acos/atan/atan2 return degrees; rotate, linear_extrude twist, rotate_extrude angle, $vpr, $vpf are degrees. Nothing in the language uses radians.
%g formatterecho and str() render numbers like printf %g with 6 significant digits: trailing zeros trimmed, integers without decimal point, scientific notation once the exponent leaves the %g window.
PIThe only numeric constant (full double precision; echoes as 3.14159). There is no E — users write exp(1).
--hardwarningsCLI/preferences mode that turns the FIRST warning into a fatal error; CI pipelines rely on it, making the warning set itself part of the contract.

Arithmetic is plain IEEE-754 double arithmetic with these language-level decorations: division by zero warns ('WARNING: division by zero') and yields inf/-inf (0/0 yields nan); modulo is C fmod (result sign follows the dividend; x % 0 is nan); domain errors in sqrt/ln/log/asin/acos produce nan with a WARNING (since 2019.05; silently nan before). Nothing numeric ever throws — evaluation continues with inf/nan/undef flowing onward. All trigonometry is in degrees, and multiples of 30 and 45 degrees are special-cased to exact results in every quadrant (sin(180) == 0 exactly, cos(60) == 0.5; introduced 2019.05), which golden echo-based tests will expose.
