The observable text surface: echo prints 'ECHO: ' plus arguments joined by ', '; numbers are formatted double-conversion-style (6 significant digits, unpadded exponents — see the number formatting entry) with 6 SIGNIFICANT digits — 1/3 prints 0.333333, PI prints 3.14159, 10.0 prints 10 (no decimal point), 1234567 prints 1.23457e+6 (large integers go scientific AND get rounded — a famous trap), values below 1e-5 print scientific with an unpadded exponent ('1.5e-6'); 1.5e-5 itself prints fixed as '0.000015', inf prints inf, nan prints nan. str() uses the IDENTICAL formatter, so user string-building inherits the 6-digit rounding; 0.1+0.2 both echoes and str()s as 0.3, hiding float error. Diagnostics follow fixed shapes — 'WARNING: <message>, in file <path>, line <N>.', 'ERROR: ...', 'DEPRECATED: ...', and assert failures with 'TRACE: called by ...' stack lines — and are parsed by users, test suites, and tooling, so the strings must match the oracle verbatim. Because --hardwarnings turns any warning fatal, emitting too many warnings breaks scripts as surely as emitting too few.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Large integers in echo go scientific and ROUNDED: echo(1234567) prints 1.23457e+6; echo(123456) prints 123456; the cutover is at 1e6 (1000000 prints 1e+6, 999999 prints 999999) with unpadded exponents.
  • echo hides float error: 0.1+0.2 prints 0.3; 6-significant-digit rounding applies inside vectors too ([1/3] prints [0.333333]).
  • str() formats numbers exactly like echo — str(1e7) is "1e+7"; any user code building filenames/labels from big numbers inherits this. The two must share one formatter implementation.
  • Integers print without decimal point: 10.0 prints 10; 2.0*5 prints 10.
  • -0: echo prints '0' (UNIQUE_ZERO in the 2021.01 double-conversion formatter); 0 == -0 is true; sign(-0) is 0; the sign shows only via arithmetic (1/-0 == -inf).
  • inf prints 'inf', nan prints 'nan'; VERIFY -inf prints '-inf'. There are no literals; 1/0 (with warning) is the accessible source of inf, 0/0 of nan.
  • 1/0 yields inf with 'WARNING: division by zero'; -1/0 yields -inf; VERIFY whether 0/0 emits the same division-by-zero warning text (result nan); x % 0 yields nan (VERIFY its warning, believed 'WARNING: modulo by zero' or the division text — pin).
  • Domain errors yield nan with WARNING since 2019.05: sqrt(-1), ln(-1), log(-1), asin(2), acos(-2). VERIFY per-function warning texts. VERIFY ln(0)/log(0) — believed -inf (C behavior), possibly with warning.
  • Exact trig: sin(180)==0 exactly (not 1.22e-16), cos(90)==0, tan(45)==1 — and 30/45/60 in every quadrant (sin(30)==0.5, cos(60)==0.5, tan(60)==sqrt(3)) via the degree-trig special cases introduced in 2019.05. Settled; matches the sin/cos/tan entries.
  • round() is half-away-from-zero: round(2.5) == 3, round(-2.5) == -3 — VERIFY against the oracle since C round vs rint differ and implementations vary.
  • % is fmod, sign follows dividend: 5%3 == 2, -5%3 == -2, 5%-3 == 2; fractional moduli work (5.5%2 == 1.5).
  • ^ exponentiation is NEW in 2021.01 (pow() is the older spelling): right-associative (2^3^2 == 512); VERIFY precedence vs unary minus (-2^2 believed -4, i.e. ^ binds tighter than unary minus); VERIFY 0^0 (believed 1, C pow); negative base with non-integer exponent yields nan.
  • min()/max(): 2+ scalar args or a single vector arg; empty vector yields WARNING + undef; VERIFY behavior with non-numeric elements (believed undef/nan propagation — pin).
  • No unary-plus certainty: VERIFY whether '+5' parses (believed the grammar accepts unary +; pin, since it affects expression parsing compatibility).
  • VERIFY numeric literal corners: leading-dot '.5' and trailing-dot '5.' acceptance; exponent forms 1E3/1e+3. No hex (0x10 is a syntax error), no binary/octal.
  • Precision loss is silent past 2^53: echo(2^53 == 2^53+1) prints true; no warning exists for it.
  • Locale independence: the decimal separator is always '.', never ',', regardless of system locale; no digit grouping.
  • Diagnostic formats to clone verbatim (capture from oracle): 'WARNING: <msg>, in file <path>, line <N>.', 'ERROR: ...', 'DEPRECATED: ...', assert: ERROR: Assertion '(<condition source text>)' failed[: <message>] in file <path>, line <N>, then 'TRACE: called by '<module>', in file ..., line ...' lines unwinding the module stack. VERIFY exact punctuation of each.
  • The warning SET is surface (compatibility-critical under --hardwarnings): variable reassignment, unknown variable, undefined operation/type mismatch, unequal-length vector +/-, scalar+vector, division by zero, deprecated reversed range, $fa/$fs clamp, unknown named parameter, empty min/max, range step problems, deprecations (assign, triangles=), unknown module/function call. VERIFY completeness by diffing against oracle runs of a warning-torture script.
  • Warning/echo emission order is depth-first instantiation order, before/independent of geometry evaluation; duplicate-warning rate limiting in the GUI console is app behavior, not language.
  • Dev snapshots have been moving echo toward full-precision shortest-roundtrip number output — 2021.01 is firmly 6-significant-digit double-conversion output; VERIFY per-snapshot before chasing snapshot parity.
  • version() returns [2021, 1] style vectors (two elements on release builds; a third day element only on date-versioned snapshots) and version_num() returns 20210100-style numbers — these numbers flow through the same formatter rules when echoed (20210100 prints as 2.02101e+7!).
echo(1/3, PI, 10.0, 1234567);   // ECHO: 0.333333, 3.14159, 10, 1.23457e+6
echo(0.1 + 0.2);                 // ECHO: 0.3
echo(sin(180), cos(90));         // ECHO: 0, 0
echo(-5 % 3, 5 % -3);            // ECHO: -2, 2
echo(2^3^2, -2^2);               // ECHO: 512, -4
echo(str(1/3), len(str(1/3)));   // ECHO: "0.333333", 8
cube(1);
Implementer: Write ONE number-formatting routine that both echo and str() call and freeze it against oracle output for a battery of magnitudes (10^-7..10^9, negative, -0, inf, nan, 6-digit boundary values like 999999.5). Implement warnings through a single channel with a --hardwarnings escalation switch. Pin via oracle probes: the %g cutover boundaries, -0, round() ties, ^ precedence vs unary minus, 0^0, x%0 warning, ln(0), every domain-error warning text, unary plus, and the exact-trig angle set and introduction release. Do not use platform printf %g blindly — verify it matches OpenSCAD's output for the boundary cases (it is believed to literally be %g, but freeze by test, not assumption).

object values & dot access (snapshot)

concept snapshot (post-2021.01)

SNAPSHOT-ONLY value type: an immutable string-keyed record returned by textmetrics()/fontmetrics(), read with dot access; tested by is_object().

Objects are not constructible from user syntax in snapshots (no literal form); they arrive only from builtins. Dot access on them generalizes the member-access operator beyond vector .x/.y/.z. is_object(x) tests the type; all other type tests return false on objects.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Baseline 2021.01 has NO object type: is_object is likewise snapshot-era, and dot access is only the vector .x/.y/.z swizzle.
  • verify vs oracle equality semantics and echo/str rendering of objects on a snapshot build before promising any behavior.
  • Accessing a missing field: VERIFY — believed undef without warning.
m = textmetrics("A");
if (is_object(m)) echo(m.position);
Implementer: Decide early whether your dialect ships objects at all; if yes, gate them behind the same feature flag and keep is_object() false-returning when disabled.

operator precedence

concept 2021.01

Full expression-operator precedence and associativity table.

Highest to lowest: 1) postfix function call f(x), indexing v[i], member v.x; 2) ^ exponentiation (right-associative, 2021.01); 3) unary !, -, + ; 4) * / % (left-assoc); 5) binary + - (left-assoc); 6) relational < <= >= > (left-assoc, non-chaining semantically); 7) == != (left-assoc); 8) && ; 9) || ; 10) ternary c?a:b (right-associative, lazy). Parentheses group. Assignment is a statement, not an expression.

Edge cases & oddities
  • ^ binds tighter than unary minus: -2^2 == -4 (VERIFY: this exact corner against oracle; grammar places unary below ^).
  • 2^-3 parses (unary minus in the exponent operand) and yields 0.125.
  • x^y^z == x^(y^z) (right-assoc).
  • a==b==c parses as (a==b)==c - comparing a boolean to c, usually false; no chained comparisons.
  • -x[0] is -(x[0]); !f(x) is !(f(x)) - postfix beats unary.
  • a?b:c?d:e == a?b:(c?d:e).
  • Calling a non-function value: WARNING and undef (VERIFY text, e.g. 'WARNING: Can't call function on ...').
  • let(...) expr, echo(...) expr, assert(...) expr, function(...) expr are prefix expression forms that extend to the right with the lowest precedence.
echo(-2^2, 2^-3, 1?2:0?3:4); // ECHO: -4, 0.125, 2
Implementer: since reflects the last material change (^ insertion). Encode the table in a Pratt parser and oracle-probe -2^2, 2^3^2, -x%y.

Ranges

concept 2015.03

[begin:end] (implicit step 1) and [begin:step:end] construct a range — a distinct lazy value type (is_list false, len undef; subscripts r[0]/r[1]/r[2] yield begin/step/end, other indices undef) consumed by for, intersection_for, each, list comprehensions, and children(); iteration runs begin, begin+step, ... while within end; negative steps descend; and the legacy quirk that [begin:end] with begin>end is silently swapped (with a deprecation warning) must be cloned.

beginFirst value produced. Any numeric expression; non-numeric/nan bounds make the range degenerate (warning — VERIFY exact behavior).
stepIncrement; omitted in the two-part form (defaults to 1). Negative steps produce descending iteration; step 0 is invalid (WARNING; yields no/limited iteration — VERIFY exact result).
endInclusive bound: values are produced while they have not passed end in the direction of step; whether a float-accumulated endpoint is included depends on the exact count formula — pin via oracle.

A range literal uses colon syntax inside square brackets and produces a value of the distinct range type: it is NOT a vector — is_list returns false, len returns undef; subscripts r[0]/r[1]/r[2] return begin/step/end (any other index is undef with the range-subscript message), and r.begin/r.step/r.end member access returns the same components — and no builtin converts it to a list except iteration ([each r] or [for (i=r) i]). Ranges are consumed lazily by the for module, intersection_for, list-comprehension for, each, and children(): a huge range like [0:1e9] iterates without materializing a billion-element vector (memory is only committed when a comprehension or each collects results). Iteration semantics: values begin, begin+step, begin+2*step, ... are produced while they do not pass end (ascending for positive step, descending for negative); end is inclusive when landed on exactly, but with fractional steps floating-point error decides whether the endpoint is reached — the element-count rule must be pinned bit-for-bit against the oracle (e.g. whether [0:0.1:1] produces 10 or 11 values). The two-part form [b:e] has implicit step 1, and carries the notorious legacy rule: when begin > end, 2021.01 SWAPS the bounds, emits 'DEPRECATED: Using ranges of the form [begin:end] with begin value greater than the end value is deprecated.', and iterates ascending anyway. Echo prints ranges with spaced colons in three-part form, e.g. [0 : 2 : 10].

Edge cases & oddities
  • Range is a distinct type: is_list(r) false, is_num(r) false, len(r) undef (VERIFY accompanying warning). Components ARE reachable: r[0]/r[1]/r[2] = begin/step/end and r.begin/r.step/r.end (2021.01); r[3] and r.x are undef. Detection is only by elimination — there is no is_range.
  • echo/str rendering: [0:2:10] prints as '[0 : 2 : 10]' (spaces around colons). VERIFY: whether the two-part form [0:5] prints its implicit step (believed yes, as '[0 : 1 : 5]') — pin exact text.
  • Legacy reversed range: [10:1] emits 'DEPRECATED: Using ranges of the form [begin:end] with begin value greater than the end value is deprecated.' and iterates 1..10 ASCENDING (bounds swapped) in 2021.01. VERIFY: newer snapshots have discussed treating it as empty — pin per targeted version.
  • verify vs oracle whether the swap applies to the explicit-step form — believed NOT: [10:1:0] (positive step, begin>end) yields ZERO iterations with no deprecation message; only the two-part form swaps. Pin.
  • Negative step: [10:-2:0] produces 10,8,6,4,2,0. Negative step with begin < end ([0:-1:5]) produces zero iterations. VERIFY whether any warning accompanies the empty case (believed silent).
  • Step 0: WARNING and no usable iteration. VERIFY exact text and result (believed the range yields nothing; older versions differed) — clone the 2021.01 behavior.
  • Fractional steps accumulate float error: [0:0.1:1] endpoint inclusion is decided by the internal count formula, not by exact arithmetic — VERIFY the produced count and the exact values (whether iteration computes begin + i*step or accumulates val += step changes the low bits of every produced value; pin which one 2021.01 uses).
  • verify vs oracle internal element-count formula (believed of the form floor((end-begin)/step)+1 evaluated in doubles, clamped at 0) and any epsilon handling — this single formula decides every off-by-one dispute; freeze it with oracle probes at boundary values like [0:0.1:0.3], [1:1/3:2], [0:0.7:7].
  • Huge ranges: for(i=[0:1e9]) iterates lazily (slow, no memory blowup); [each [0:1e9]] and comprehensions materialize and will exhaust memory. VERIFY: whether 2021.01 imposes any hard cap or warning on range element count (believed a clamp exists at the uint32 boundary internally; probe [0:1e10] and nan/inf bounds — approx 'WARNING: Bad range parameter').
  • Non-numeric components: [0:undef:5], ["a":1:5], nan/inf as begin/step/end — VERIFY behavior (believed WARNING and a degenerate range producing nothing; inf end producing effectively unbounded iteration must be probed carefully).
  • Ranges as children() selectors: children([0:2]) instantiates children 0,1,2 — ranges work anywhere the implementation iterates indices. Out-of-range values follow children()'s own error rules.
  • Range equality: VERIFY [0:1:5] == [0:1:5] (believed true, comparing begin/step/end structurally) and whether the swapped legacy form compares equal to its normalized form (probe [5:1] == [1:5]).
  • A range never == a vector, even one with identical elements (different types compare false).
  • Ranges in boolean context: VERIFY truthiness (believed truthy like any non-listed value, even when producing zero iterations).
  • for over a range binds plain numbers; the loop variable in fractional ranges carries accumulated float representation — i == 0.3 comparisons against range-produced values are unreliable in user code, and your implementation must reproduce the same bit patterns as the oracle's iteration strategy.
  • String/vector iteration is a separate for-capability, not ranges: for (c = "abc") and for (v = [[1,2],[3,4]]) — do not funnel through range machinery. A bare scalar iterates once (VERIFY, per seed).
  • Ranges may be stored in variables, passed to functions/modules, put in vectors ([[0:5], [10:20]] is a vector of two ranges), and passed through $-variables — they are ordinary values apart from their lazy consumption.
r = [0:2:10];
echo(r, is_list(r), len(r));   // ECHO: [0 : 2 : 10], false, undef
for (i = r) echo(i);            // 0 2 4 6 8 10
echo([ each [3:-1:1] ]);        // ECHO: [3, 2, 1]
echo([ for (i = [0:0.1:0.3]) i ]);  // pin endpoint inclusion vs oracle
for (i = [4:1]) echo("legacy", i);  // DEPRECATED warning, iterates 1..4
cube(1);
Implementer: Represent as {begin, step, end} doubles with normalization applied ONLY for the two-part begin>end form (swap + deprecation). Implement one canonical count/iteration routine shared by for, each, comprehensions, and children(), and freeze it against oracle probes: fractional-step endpoint cases, begin+i*step vs accumulation bit patterns, step 0, negative-step empty, nan/inf bounds, huge-count clamping, and the echo text including implicit-step display. Keep iteration lazy in for and eager only at collection points. The reversed-range swap is bug-for-bug required for 2021.01; gate any snapshot 'empty' behavior behind a version flag.

Recursion & evaluation limits

concept 2015.03 (core TCE); scope broadened later

With no loops-with-state, recursion is the language's only general computation mechanism: the lazy ternary is the sole terminator; functions get tail-call elimination (core self-recursive-ternary pattern since 2015.03; 2019.05 at most broadened tail positions — deep tail recursion into the millions must work because libraries rely on it) while non-tail recursion is stack-bounded; modules hit a fixed 'Recursion detected' error at a set depth; and assert() is the only construct that halts evaluation.

cond ? a : bThe ternary is lazy on the untaken branch and right-associative — it is THE recursion terminator. An eager-ternary implementation infinite-loops every recursive library function.
&& / ||Short-circuiting holds throughout the reference window (verified back to 2015.03; if-condition short-circuiting fixed by 2014.03); usable as auxiliary recursion guards but the ternary is the primary one.
tail-call eliminationA function whose recursive call is in tail position reuses the frame (core pattern since 2015.03); recursion depth on the order of 1,000,000 is expected to work.
module recursion limitModules recurse via self-instantiation; exceeding the implementation's depth limit raises an error naming the module, approx "ERROR: Recursion detected calling module 'name'".
assert(cond, message)The only halting construct: on failure, evaluation stops with an ERROR naming the condition's source text (plus optional message) and TRACE: lines unwinding the module call stack.

OpenSCAD has no while, break, continue, return, or mutation; every algorithm is a single-expression function using the ternary for termination and recursion for iteration, or a data-driven for/comprehension. Laziness rules make this viable: the ternary evaluates only the taken branch, and && / || short-circuit (verified back to 2015.03). Function recursion comes in two regimes. Calls in TAIL position are eliminated (core pattern since 2015.03): 'f(n, acc) = n<=0 ? acc : f(n-1, acc+n)' runs at depths around a million — the BOSL2-class libraries build sort/map/fold on this and an implementation without TCO (or an equivalent trampoline) fails them immediately. Non-tail recursion ('n + sum(n-1)') consumes real evaluator stack and is bounded; exceeding the bound produces a recursion error naming the function rather than a crash. Module recursion (a module instantiating itself, usually guarded by if) is legal and idiomatic for fractals/trees, but is bounded by a fixed depth check with an explicit error approx "ERROR: Recursion detected calling module 'name'". Errors and limits are observable surface: the recursion error texts, the assert() failure format with its TRACE stack unwind, and the practical depth thresholds all get exercised by real scripts and test suites. Nothing else halts: warnings (including under-recursion type mismatches) let evaluation continue; only assert() failure — and fatal errors like recursion detection — stop the run.

Edge cases & oddities
  • The ternary MUST be lazy: 'f(n) = n<=0 ? 0 : f(n-1)' with eager branches recurses forever. Right-associativity: 'a ? b : c ? d : e' parses as 'a ? b : (c ? d : e)' — the else-if chain idiom.
  • && and || short-circuit throughout the reference window (verified back to 2015.03), so 'n>0 && f(n-1)' works as a guard. 2021.01 semantics: short-circuiting, returning booleans (VERIFY return type, see Type chapter).
  • verify vs oracle the exact set of tail positions eligible for TCO — the ternary's taken branch certainly; believed also the body of let() expressions and the trailing subexpression of echo(...) expr and assert(...) expr wrappers; probe each (a library that traces with echo inside a tail call must not lose TCO).
  • Deep tail recursion contract: on the order of 1,000,000 frames must complete (the seed pins 'into the millions'). VERIFY the practical ceiling on the official 2021.01 binary and match or exceed it.
  • Non-tail function recursion is stack-bounded with an error naming the function: VERIFY exact text (believed "ERROR: Recursion detected calling function 'f'") and the practical depth (platform stack dependent in the C++ implementation — thousands to tens of thousands; pin the oracle's observed depth and document yours).
  • Module recursion limit: VERIFY exact error text (believed "ERROR: Recursion detected calling module 'name'") and the depth at which it fires; the seed calls it 'a fixed recursion depth limit'. After the error, VERIFY whether evaluation aborts entirely or the subtree is dropped (believed fatal for the compile).
  • Mutual recursion (a calls b calls a) hits the same machinery for both functions and modules; the error names the module/function whose re-entry tripped the check — VERIFY which name appears.
  • Recursion through children(): a module that passes children back into itself recurses through the instantiation stack the same way; $parent_modules/parent_module(n) observe the stack depth.
  • Genuinely infinite TAIL recursion is not detected — it runs until the user kills it (or memory is exhausted by accumulated data). Do not add a helpful cap; scripts distinguish 'slow' from 'error'.
  • C-style comprehension loops [for (init; cond; update) expr] can also loop forever if cond never fails. VERIFY: whether 2021.01 imposes any iteration cap or warning on generator-for loops (believed uncapped — probe; if a cap exists, clone its threshold and message).
  • Deeply nested expressions (parentheses, vector literals) can exhaust the PARSER's stack separately from evaluation: VERIFY 2021.01 behavior on pathological nesting (believed crash-or-error at platform-dependent depth; your parser should error gracefully at a documented depth).
  • assert(false) halts with: ERROR: Assertion '(false)' failed in file <path>, line <N> — with an optional ': <message>' (message formatted like an echo value) and then 'TRACE: called by '<module>', in file ..., line ...' lines for each module stack frame, innermost first. VERIFY exact punctuation and frame order; test suites parse these.
  • The expression form 'assert(cond, msg) expr' (2019.05+) evaluates to expr on success — it sits INSIDE function bodies and participates in tail-position analysis (VERIFY).
  • Errors during recursion (type mismatch producing undef, division by zero) do NOT stop recursion — they warn and continue, so a buggy terminator comparing against undef can still run to the depth limit. The resulting warning flood is observable behavior (GUI may rate-limit display).
  • Memoization: OpenSCAD does NOT memoize function calls — exponential naive-fib really is exponential. Do not add semantic-level function caching (echo side effects inside functions would be lost/reordered); geometry caching (see Geometry model) is separate and safe because instantiation echoes happen pre-cache. VERIFY: echo-expression inside a function called twice with same args prints twice.
  • Stack depth interacts with implementation platform: OpenSCAD's limits derive from the C-stack; a wasm32 build has a small shadow stack — implement the evaluator with an explicit heap stack or trampoline so YOUR limits are at least the oracle's, or deep libraries will trap where OpenSCAD succeeds.
  • Performance contracts adjacent to recursion: comprehension-built lists must be O(1) amortized append, and str()-based accumulation in deep recursion is O(n^2) unless rope-like — real libraries hit both.
function fact(n, acc=1) = n <= 1 ? acc : fact(n-1, acc*n);
echo(fact(100));                 // 9.33262e+157
function count(n) = n <= 0 ? 0 : count(n-1) + 1;   // non-tail
echo(count(1000));               // fine; huge n hits the stack limit
module steps(n) { if (n > 0) { translate([n,0,0]) cube(1); steps(n-1); } }
steps(10);
assert(fact(3) == 6, "factorial broken");
Implementer: Build the evaluator iteratively (explicit stack/trampoline) from the start — retrofitting TCO is painful. Tail-position analysis must cover ternary branches, let bodies, and echo/assert expression wrappers (verify each against the oracle first). Pin via oracle probes: exact 'Recursion detected' texts for module and function, observed depth ceilings for module recursion and non-tail function recursion, tail-recursion at 10^6, assert failure format including TRACE order, double-echo from repeated pure calls (proving no function memoization). Document your own limits where they exceed OpenSCAD's; never undercut them. Treat infinite tail loops as user responsibility — provide cancellation in the host app, not a semantic cap.

recursion limits and tail-call elimination

concept 2015.03 (core TCE); scope broadened later

Both modules and functions may recurse; modules are hard-depth-limited with a 'Recursion detected' error; functions get tail-call elimination (core self-recursive-ternary pattern since 2015.03; 2019.05 at most broadened it) so tail recursion runs to millions of frames while non-tail recursion is depth-bounded.

Module recursion (a module instantiating itself, directly or mutually) is legal and bounded by a fixed depth guard; exceeding it aborts evaluation with an error naming the module. Function recursion: since 2015.03 a self-recursive call in tail position (the taken ternary branch; 2019.05 at most broadened coverage to further tail contexts — VERIFY let bodies and mutual recursion) reuses the frame, so accumulator-style library code (BOSL2 etc.) runs 100k-1M+ deep. Non-tail function recursion hits a depth guard with an error naming the function. Evaluation halts on either error (like assert), with TRACE lines for the module stack.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Module error text approx: "ERROR: Recursion detected calling module 'name'" — VERIFY exact text and the depth limit (believed a fixed count on the order of low thousands; pin the observable number with a depth-probe script).
  • Function non-tail error approx: "ERROR: Recursion detected calling function 'name'" — VERIFY text and depth (probe function f(n)=n<=0?0:1+f(n-1); bisect the failing n).
  • TCE coverage: plain ternary tail calls definitely eliminated; VERIFY elimination through let(...) tail bodies, through echo()/assert() expression wrappers, and for mutual recursion between two functions.
  • A call under an operator (n * f(n-1)) is NOT a tail call — bounded; the same function rewritten with an accumulator is unbounded. Both behaviors are compatibility surface.
  • Function literals: VERIFY that TCE applies equally to recursive calls through variables holding function values.
  • Pre-2015.03: no TCE, so ALL recursion was depth-bounded regardless of guard style (the lazy ternary and short-circuiting &&/|| terminated recursion but could not deepen it) — relevant only when emulating ancient versions.
  • C-style comprehension for is iterative, not recursive — it does not consume recursion depth.
  • Errors here HALT evaluation (unlike warnings); under --hardwarnings nothing changes (already fatal).
function count(n, acc = 0) = n <= 0 ? acc : count(n - 1, acc + 1); echo(count(500000)); // needs TCE
Implementer: Implement the evaluator iteratively (explicit heap stack / trampoline): wasm32 shadow stacks trap far below OpenSCAD's limits otherwise. Pin both depth limits and error texts via bisection probes; libraries genuinely rely on ~1M tail frames.

statement grammar (assignment, no expression statements)

concept pre-2015.03

Statements and expressions are disjoint grammars; assignment is last-write-wins per scope, not imperative.

Legal statements: 'name = expr;', module/function definitions, module instantiations (with optional modifier prefix and { } child block), module-level if/for/intersection_for/let, and include/use directives. There are no expression statements, no compound assignment (+=, ++), and geometry is not a value ('x = cube(1);' is an error). Within one scope, the LAST textual assignment to a name wins and takes effect at the position of the FIRST assignment, with 'WARNING: variable_name was reassigned' (VERIFY exact text).

Edge cases & oddities
  • a=1; b=a; a=2; yields b==2 - last-write-wins is observable and load-bearing (customizer -D overrides depend on it).
  • Assignments in { } blocks shadow; they never mutate outer scope.
  • No semicolon after a { } child block; ';' alone is an empty instantiation (VERIFY: bare ';' legality).
  • Modifier characters (* ! # %) may prefix exactly one instantiation.
  • 'x = cube(1);' - VERIFY exact diagnostic (parse error vs 'unknown function cube' warning + undef, since cube is a module, not a function; believed the latter).
a=1; b=a; a=2; echo(b); // ECHO: 2 (with reassignment warning)
Implementer: Implement scopes as ordered maps where reassignment replaces the expression at the original slot; pin interleaving with geometry statements via oracle.

string comparison

concept pre-2015.03

Equality is total deep equality; string-string relationals compare lexicographically by Unicode code point; a string compared against any non-string yields undef under the mixed-type rule.

== and != work across all types: two strings are equal iff they have identical code-point sequences; a string compared to any non-string is unequal (false, not undef). Between two strings, the relational operators < <= > >= compare lexicographically by code point, case-sensitively, with no locale, collation, or normalization. A relational comparison where either side is not the right type yields undef. A string compared relationally against any NON-string yields undef under the mixed-type rule; other same-type pairs (numbers, booleans, vectors, ranges) have their own orderings — see the relational entry.

Edge cases & oddities
  • "a" == "a" -> true; "a" == 97 -> false (mismatched types compare unequal under ==, never undef).
  • "abc" < "abd" -> true; "ab" < "abc" -> true (proper prefix sorts first); "" < "a" -> true.
  • Case-sensitive code-point order: "Z" < "a" is true (90 < 97); digits sort before uppercase before lowercase.
  • "a" < 5 -> undef (relational across types is undef, not false). VERIFY: whether a WARNING like "undefined operation (string < number)" accompanies it in 2021.01.
  • No Unicode normalization: NFC and NFD spellings of the same visual text are DIFFERENT, unequal strings.
  • Implementation shortcut that is exactly conformant: byte-wise comparison of UTF-8 encodings yields the same order as code-point comparison (UTF-8 is order-preserving) — safe to use.
  • Sorting user-space (there is no builtin sort) is built on these operators; nan-style partiality does not apply to strings, but comparing a string against undef relationally is undef.
  • min/max: the n-ary form min(a,b,...) is numbers-only (a non-number argument yields the conversion WARNING + undef), but the single-vector form uses the generic relational less-than, so min(["b","a"]) returns "a" (lexicographic) in 2021.01; mixed-type element vectors give unwarned, comparator-dependent results.
echo("Zoo" < "apple", "ab" == "ab", "1" == 1);
Implementer: Use memcmp-style byte comparison over UTF-8. Pin the warning-vs-silent behavior of mixed-type relational comparisons via oracle (matters under --hardwarnings).

string iteration

concept 2019.05

Strings are iterable per character (per code point), each iteration binding a 1-character string.

In list comprehensions, a string used as the iterable produces one iteration per Unicode code point, binding a one-character string each time: [for (c = "abc") c] == ["a", "b", "c"]. This is the primitive from which user-space split/map/upper/lower are built. Combined with search(), ord(), chr() and str() it forms the whole string-processing toolkit — there are no substring/split/join/replace/case builtins anywhere in the language.

Edge cases & oddities
  • [for (c = "abc") c] -> ["a", "b", "c"]; empty string -> zero iterations -> [].
  • Iteration is per code point: multibyte and astral characters are single iterations; combining marks iterate separately from their base.
  • verify vs oracle the statement-level for MODULE also iterates strings character-wise in 2021.01 (for (c = "ab") translate(...) ...) — pin the exact version coverage; older releases may have treated the string as a single scalar iteration.
  • verify vs oracle 'each' applied to a string splices characters ([each "ab"] believed ["a", "b"]) — pin, since each on a non-list normally yields the value itself and strings sit ambiguously between 'iterable' and 'scalar'.
  • Characters come back as strings, so nested iteration over an iterated character iterates that 1-char string once.
  • Reversal/mapping idioms rely on this: str-join of a comprehension is the canonical map: str([for(c=s) ...]) does NOT join (it renders a vector) — joining requires recursion or a fold via str(acc, c). Reimplementation must keep these idioms fast.
  • search() with a string match_value iterates its characters as independent search terms — same per-character model (see search entry).
  • On 2015.03 a string used as a comprehension iterable is a single scalar (ONE iteration binding the whole string: [for (c="ab") c] == ["ab"]); per-code-point iteration arrived in 2019.05.
echo([for (c = "abc") ord(c)]);
Implementer: Iterator over UTF-8 code points shared by comprehensions, the for module, each, and search's per-character term expansion. Pin the for-module and each cases against the oracle before freezing (two VERIFY items above).

string Unicode model

concept pre-2015.03

Strings are immutable UTF-8 sequences whose observable unit everywhere (len, indexing, ord, iteration, search) is the Unicode code point.

String literals are double-quoted, may contain arbitrary UTF-8 directly in the source file, and support the escapes \" \\ \t \n \r plus \x##, \u#### and \U###### (since 2015.03). At runtime a string is an immutable sequence of Unicode code points: len counts code points, s[i] indexes code points, ord/chr convert code points, iteration and search operate per code point. There is no char type (1-char strings serve), no mutation, no byte-level access.

Edge cases & oddities
  • len("häx") == 3 despite 4 UTF-8 bytes; one astral emoji has len 1 despite 4 bytes.
  • \u####/\U######/\x## escapes exist since 2015.03 (raw UTF-8 in source also works). VERIFY: what an unknown escape like \q produces (believed the character literally) — must match.
  • Escapes \t \n \r produce real control characters that echo prints literally inside the quoted rendering (a \n in a string breaks the ECHO line). VERIFY exact console rendering of embedded newlines.
  • No normalization is ever applied — comparison, len and indexing see exactly the code points written.
  • Grapheme clusters are not a unit anywhere in the language.
  • verify vs oracle behavior on invalid UTF-8 (possible via file reading or malformed source): believed replacement/undefined — pin if import paths can produce it.
  • text() consumes these strings for glyph shaping; corrupting non-ASCII via byte indexing shows up visibly there.
  • Strings are valid dictionary-order keys nowhere (no maps in 2021.01); vectors of [key,value] pairs plus search() serve that role.
s = "π≈3.14159"; echo(len(s), s[0], ord(s[0]));
Implementer: Represent as UTF-8 with code-point-aware ops. The escape-set and unknown-escape behavior belongs to the lexer team but the runtime contract here is: code points everywhere, bytes nowhere.

text() alignment (halign/valign)

concept 2015.03 (with text())

Anchoring of the rendered string relative to the origin. Defaults are halign="left", valign="baseline" — the baseline default (not "bottom") is a classic user trip. Alignment uses font METRICS (advance width, ascent, descent), not the geometric ink bounding box.

halignleft: pen starts at x=0 (text extends to +X). center: total advance width centered on x=0. right: total advance ends at x=0 (text extends to -X).
valignbaseline: baseline at y=0 (descenders dip below 0). bottom: the font's descent line sits at y=0 (all metric-box text above 0). top: the font's ascent line sits at y=0 (text extends below). center: midpoint of ascent+descent band at y=0.

Horizontal offsets are computed from the shaped run's total advance width (after spacing and shaping); vertical offsets from the selected font's ascent/descent metrics scaled by size. Because metrics, not ink, drive alignment: "bottom"-aligned text usually floats slightly above y=0 (descent margin), "top"-aligned text rarely touches y=0, and short strings of different letters ("x" vs "X") align identically. VERIFY: whether 2021.01 uses pure font metrics or the glyph bounding box for each valign value — pin each of the four against the oracle with a known font, since documentation diagrams are ambiguous.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Default valign="baseline" means text("y") has geometry below y=0 — code assuming bottom-alignment mis-positions; must be cloned, not fixed.
  • Trailing/leading spaces count in the advance: halign="center" of "A " is shifted vs "A".
  • Invalid enum strings: VERIFY warning text and fallback to defaults.
  • With direction="rtl", halign values keep their geometric meaning (left = run starts at x=0) — VERIFY the exact origin/extent interaction for rtl and for vertical ttb/btt (where halign/valign roles partially swap axes); pin all 4x4x4 combinations that matter with oracle probes on a few.
  • Alignment is applied after spacing multiplication — spacing changes center/right positions.
  • Fonts with unusual metrics (huge line gap, negative descent) follow metrics faithfully; do not clamp.
text("Type", size = 10, halign = "center", valign = "top");
Implementer: Small code, high trip-hazard: write oracle probes that measure translate offsets of identical strings under each halign/valign (and each direction) and freeze the offset formulas. Do not use ink bboxes unless the oracle proves OpenSCAD does for a given value.

text() direction, language, script

concept 2015.03 (with text())

Layout direction plus the language/script tags fed to the HarfBuzz-class shaper. direction changes pen-advance geometry; language/script change which glyphs/forms the shaper selects (ligatures, contextual joining), which changes the output polygons.

directionltr (default): glyphs advance in +X. rtl: run laid out right-to-left (needed for Arabic/Hebrew visual order). ttb: vertical, each glyph below the previous (advance in -Y). btt: vertical, upward (+Y).
languageLanguage tag string, default "en" (e.g. "ar", "he", "ja"); enables language-specific shaping features in fonts that have them.
scriptScript name string, default "latin" (e.g. "arabic", "hebrew"); selects the shaping engine rules — essential for joined scripts.

The whole string is shaped as a single run with the given direction/script/language (there is no per-run bidi segmentation — mixed-direction strings are the user's problem). For rtl, glyph order is reversed and shaping produces final/medial/initial forms where the script requires. ttb/btt use vertical advances (or fall back to per-glyph height metrics for fonts without vertical tables — VERIFY fallback), stacking glyphs along Y with spacing applied to the vertical advance. Alignment interacts with the chosen axis (see alignment entry).

Edge cases & oddities
  • Arabic with default script="latin": VERIFY whether the shaper still autodetects and joins, or renders isolated forms — pin, because either behavior will be depended on.
  • Invalid direction string: VERIFY — believed WARNING + ltr fallback.
  • language/script are free strings; unknown values silently degrade to default shaping (VERIFY, no warning expected).
  • No bidi algorithm: embedding LTR digits in an rtl run yields shaper-defined, not Unicode-bidi, ordering — clone as-is.
  • ttb/btt with proportional Latin fonts stack using vertical metrics that many fonts lack — VERIFY the advance actually used (upem-based? ascent+descent?) via oracle measurement.
  • Ligature substitution (e.g. fi) depends on font+language+script and changes polygon counts — golden tests must fix all three.
  • direction affects halign/valign geometry (run extent axis), see alignment entry.
text("DOWN", direction = "ttb", halign = "center");
Implementer: If full shaping is out of scope initially, implement ltr/rtl with kerning for Latin and stub ttb/btt with measured-from-oracle advances; keep the parameters and their warnings so scripts parse identically. Document any shaping simplification as a known divergence — BOSL2-style label code mostly uses defaults.

text() font selection

concept 2015.03 (with text())

text()'s font parameter is a fontconfig-style pattern with exactly two documented components: family name and an optional style= qualifier. Additional fonts are registered per-script via use <file.ttf/.otf>.

FamilyFont family name as reported by the font (e.g. "Liberation Sans", "DejaVu Serif"). Matching is fontconfig best-match, not exact-or-error.
style=Style/subfamily name after a colon, e.g. "Liberation Serif:style=Bold Italic"; values come from the font's own style names ("Regular", "Bold", "Italic", "Bold Italic", "Condensed", ...).

The string is handed to fontconfig-style resolution: the best matching installed face is chosen. There is no size, weight-number, or other qualifier in the documented syntax. font="" (the default) resolves the environment's default sans face; OpenSCAD bundles the Liberation family and its GUI Help > Font List enumerates every available family/style. use <path/font.ttf> at file top level (same search-path rules as use of .scad files) loads that file's faces for the current compilation, making them selectable by their internal family/style names.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Unknown family: fontconfig substitutes a default face; OpenSCAD emits a WARNING (VERIFY exact text, approx "Requested font ... not found"); geometry is still produced with the substitute.
  • Unknown style on a known family: silently best-matched (usually Regular) — VERIFY whether any warning appears.
  • Family matching is effectively case-insensitive/fuzzy via fontconfig — VERIFY degree; do not implement exact string match.
  • verify vs oracle undocumented extra fontconfig properties in the string (e.g. ":weight=bold") — pin whether they are honored or ignored.
  • use <font.ttf> works with TTF and OTF (VERIFY TTC collections); the file's name-table entries define the selectable names, not the filename.
  • Fonts loaded via use are scoped to the compilation, not persisted; missing font file follows the normal use<> file-not-found error path (VERIFY message).
  • Font resolution differs per machine/OS font set — identical scripts are only reproducible with pinned/bundled fonts.
  • The default resolved face when font="" is environment-dependent; on stock installs it is Liberation Sans — VERIFY on the oracle platform before freezing golden outputs.
use <MyDisplayFont.ttf>
text("Aa", font = "Liberation Serif:style=Bold Italic");
Implementer: Implement a minimal fontconfig-equivalent: registry of faces (bundled + use<>-loaded + optionally system), family+style best-match with a deterministic tie-break, and the substitute-with-warning behavior. Determinism argues for defaulting to bundled fonts only, with system fonts opt-in.

text() spacing and metric behavior

concept 2015.03 (with text())

spacing multiplies glyph advances without scaling glyph shapes; size sets the metric scale such that the documented 'approximate ascent' equals size. Real ink extents routinely exceed or undershoot the size box — metric behavior, not ink behavior, drives layout.

spacingAdvance multiplier, default 1; applied to every advance in the run (horizontal or vertical per direction).
sizeMetric scale, default 10; ascent above baseline is approximately size, so cap-height is typically ~0.7*size and full ink height (ascent+descent) exceeds size.

Glyph outlines are scaled uniformly by the size-derived factor; the pen advances by (shaped advance) * spacing between glyphs. Kerning adjustments from shaping are part of the advance and thus also scaled by spacing. Total run advance = sum of spaced advances, which feeds halign. VERIFY the exact size mapping: whether OpenSCAD scales so that font ascent == size, or scales the em to size (making ascent ~0.8*size) — measure a known font's capital height in the oracle and pin the scale factor formula; the manual only promises 'approximate ascent' and per-font variation.

Edge cases & oddities
  • spacing applies between glyphs; VERIFY whether the advance after the final glyph (affecting total width and thus center/right alignment) includes the spacing multiplier.
  • spacing=0 collapses all glyphs onto the pen origin (legal, self-overlapping union); negative spacing walks backwards producing mirrored-order overlap — both silent (VERIFY).
  • Glyph ink can exceed the metric box: accents above ascent, swashes below descent, italic overhang left of x=0 with halign=left — never clip.
  • Two fonts at the same size have different actual heights — size is not a geometric height guarantee.
  • Metrics scale linearly with size; hinting is not applied (outlines are unhinted scalable curves) — VERIFY FreeType load flags don't quantize at small sizes.
  • Monospace fonts: advance is constant but ink varies; halign uses advances, so centering monospace text matches terminal expectations.
  • str()-built numeric labels inherit echo's %g formatting (6 significant digits) before ever reaching text() — width surprises with large numbers come from str(), not text().
text("12.5 mm", size = 6, spacing = 1.2);
Implementer: Pin the size scale factor and end-of-run spacing via oracle measurements before anything else — every alignment offset depends on them. Advances must come from the font's hmtx/shaping output, not glyph bboxes.

The $-environment

concept 2015.03

Every $-prefixed variable — builtin ($fn, $fa, $fs, $t, $vpr, $vpt, $vpd, $vpf, $children, $preview, $parent_modules) or user-invented — is dynamically scoped down the instantiation/call stack: a binding set in any scope, or passed as an undeclared named argument to any call, is visible to all descendant module instantiations and function calls, and children() bodies combine the caller's lexical scope with the callee's $-environment.

$fnFragment count override for circle tessellation; default 0 (meaning 'use $fa/$fs formula'). When > 0 the fragment count is max($fn, 3); fractional values truncated (VERIFY truncation vs ceil).
$faMinimum angle per fragment, degrees; default 12; clamped to >= 0.01 with a WARNING.
$fsMinimum arc length per fragment, units; default 2; clamped to >= 0.01 with a WARNING.
$tAnimation time in [0,1); default 0; driven by the GUI animation panel (FPS x Steps) or CLI; models read it like any variable.
$vpr / $vpt / $vpd / $vpfViewport rotation [x,y,z] degrees (default [55,0,25]), translation (default [0,0,0]), camera distance (default 140), FOV degrees (default 22.5). Reads return the live GUI camera; top-level assignments set the camera after each preview frame (animatable camera). $vpf is NEW in 2021.01.
$childrenNumber of children passed to the currently executing module; 0 at top level and in childless calls.
$previewtrue during F5/OpenCSG preview and PNG export with --preview; false during F6 render and CLI mesh/2D exports. Added 2019.05.
$parent_modulesDepth of the module instantiation stack; companion to parent_module(n).
$<user-name>Any user-invented $-name participates in the identical dynamic-scoping machinery with no declaration anywhere; unset reads yield undef.

The evaluator carries two environments: the lexical scope chain (ordinary names, resolved at definition sites) and the dynamic $-environment (a stack of $-bindings snapshotted along the instantiation/call path). Setting a $-variable with an ordinary assignment in any scope rebinds it for every module instantiation and function call made within that scope's dynamic extent — descendants only, never siblings or ancestors. Additionally, ANY module or function call may include $name=value named arguments that the callee never declared; these are accepted silently (unlike unknown plain named arguments, which warn) and become dynamic bindings for the callee's entire subtree/evaluation. children() is the subtle case: the child statements passed at a call site evaluate lazily when the callee invokes children(), using the CALLER's lexical scope for ordinary names but the CALLEE's current $-environment — so a module that sets $fn=64 before children() re-tessellates the children it was handed. Function literals capture their lexical definition environment as a closure, but $-references inside their bodies still resolve dynamically at each call. The tessellation specials feed one exact formula for the fragment count of a full circle of radius r: if r < GRID_FINE (2^-20, about 9.537e-7) then 3 fragments; else if $fn > 0 then max($fn, 3); else ceil(max(min(360/$fa, 2*PI*r/$fs), 5)) — i.e. $fa caps angle per fragment, $fs caps arc length, whichever demands FEWER fragments wins the min() — both act as caps and the coarser cap governs — with the result then floored at 5. Consumers of this formula: circle, sphere (meridian count; latitude rings = ceil(N/2) at half-step phase), cylinder (using max(r1,r2)), rotate_extrude (scaled by angle/360), offset(r), text() outline flattening, and linear_extrude's default slices under twist. Getting dynamic scoping wrong breaks BOSL2 and most serious libraries.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Every $-prefixed name is dynamically scoped — the machinery is not limited to builtins; user-defined $-flags ($slop, $my_debug) are pervasive in libraries.
  • Any call site may pass $foo=val without the callee declaring it: no 'unknown parameter' warning is emitted for $-arguments (plain unknown named arguments DO warn and are ignored).
  • Reading an unset $-variable yields undef. VERIFY: believed NO unknown-variable warning is emitted for $-names (unlike plain variables) — pin, because warning parity matters under --hardwarnings.
  • Builtin specials have global defaults, so echo($fn) at top level prints 0, not undef; user $-vars have no default and read undef until bound.
  • children() environment split: 'module m(){ $fn=64; children(); } m() sphere(5);' renders the sphere with $fn=64, while ordinary variables in the child expression (e.g. 'm() sphere(r=q);') resolve in the caller's lexical scope. Child arguments are evaluated lazily at each children() instantiation, not eagerly at the call site.
  • Each children()/children(i) call re-instantiates under the $-environment in force at THAT call — a callee can stamp the same children twice with different $fn values.
  • Function calls (named functions and function literals) receive the caller's current $-environment; closures capture lexical names only — $-names always resolve dynamically at call time.
  • Fragment formula, exact: r < 2^-20 gives 3 fragments; $fn > 0 gives max($fn, 3) (VERIFY: fractional $fn believed truncated toward zero, not rounded/ceiled); otherwise ceil(max(min(360/$fa, 2*PI*r/$fs), 5)).
  • $fa and $fs are clamped to a minimum of 0.01 with a WARNING. VERIFY exact text, approx 'WARNING: $fa too small - clamping to 0.01' / 'WARNING: $fs too small - clamping to 0.01'.
  • verify vs oracle negative or zero $fn — believed the '$fn > 0' test fails and the $fa/$fs formula applies (i.e. negative $fn behaves like unset).
  • $fn between 1 and 3 yields 3 fragments (max($fn,3) floor).
  • Setting a $-var inside a for body, if branch, or let block scopes the binding to that subtree; each for iteration can carry its own $-bindings.
  • Top-level assignments to $vpr/$vpt/$vpd/$vpf set the camera AFTER each preview frame (enabling $t-driven camera animation); reads inside the GUI return the live camera the user has navigated to, so a re-preview can observe changed values. VERIFY interaction with CLI --camera (believed CLI camera flags take precedence for image export).
  • $preview is true for F5 preview and for PNG export with --preview, false for F6 render and all CLI mesh/2D exports. Idioms to support: '$fn = $preview ? 24 : 120;' and skipping minkowski in preview. VERIFY whether -D '$preview=true' can override the pipeline-provided value.
  • $t defaults to 0 outside animation; the GUI animation panel (FPS x Steps) sweeps it through [0,1); models simply read it. Your app must define how it drives $t.
  • $children is set by the module-call machinery per invocation (0 at top level); $parent_modules likewise per instantiation stack depth. VERIFY: manually assigning $children or $parent_modules — believed treated as an ordinary dynamic binding that shadows the machinery value for descendants; pin the observable result.
  • Passing specials to primitives is per-call dynamic binding: sphere(10, $fn=64) affects only that subtree; the file-level $fn is untouched.
  • A $-argument passed to a module call is part of the callee's $-environment and therefore also reaches the callee's children() instantiations.
  • verify vs oracle $-assignments at the top level of a use'd file — believed private to that file's scope and NOT injected into callers (dynamic bindings flow from the call stack, not from definition sites).
  • $-variables hold any value type (vectors for $vpr, booleans for user flags); no type checking occurs at binding time — consumers warn/misbehave on bad types when they read them.
  • Dynamic lookup order: the innermost binding on the current instantiation/call stack wins; there is no way to read 'one level up' past a shadowing binding.
  • The $-environment never flows upward or sideways: a callee cannot alter its caller's $-values.
$fa = 6; $fs = 0.5;
module spin4() { $fn = 4; for (a=[0:90:270]) rotate(a) translate([6,0,0]) children(); }
spin4() cylinder(h=5, r=2);   // squares: children see callee's $fn=4
sphere(3, $fn=32);            // per-call override
echo($fn, $qux);              // ECHO: 0, undef
Implementer: Represent the dynamic environment as an immutable stack snapshot captured per instantiation/call; children must be stored as (AST, caller lexical env) closures whose $-env is supplied at children() time. The fragment formula and its consumers must match bit-for-bit — real models depend on exact fragment counts. Oracle probes: unset-$ warning behavior, fractional/negative $fn, clamp warning texts, $preview value under every CLI export mode, camera read/write semantics, manual assignment to $children/$parent_modules. $vpf is 2021.01-only; $preview is 2019.05+ — report undef for them only if you deliberately emulate older baselines.

truthiness

concept pre-2015.03

Exactly five falsy values — false, 0 (and -0), "", [], undef — everything else is true, including nan.

valueAny OpenSCAD value: number, string, boolean, vector, range, undef, function value.

One shared truthiness table used by every conditional context: the if statement, comprehension if clause, ternary condition, logical operators (!, &&, ||), and assert's condition. Non-empty strings, non-empty vectors, all nonzero numbers (including negative, inf, and nan — the truthiness test is a plain != 0 comparison with no isnan special case, and nan != 0 is true under IEEE-754), ranges, and function values are true.

Edge cases & oddities
  • "false", "0", " " are TRUE (non-empty strings).
  • [0], [[]], [false], [undef] are TRUE (non-empty vectors).
  • -1, inf, -inf, and nan are all TRUE (nan != 0 holds under IEEE-754; there is no isnan carve-out in truthiness).
  • -0 is falsy (equals 0).
  • verify vs oracle a range value (e.g. [0:0]) is truthy regardless of emptiness; VERIFY function values truthy.
  • nan is truthy yet is_num(nan) is false — the is_num carve-out is separate from truthiness; scripts probe both. So 0/0 ? a : b yields a.
  • undef arising from any warned type-mismatch propagates as falsy, silently steering else branches — the warning is the only trace.
echo("false" ? "truthy" : "falsy"); // ECHO: "truthy"
Implementer: Centralize one to_bool used by all six contexts; probe range/function-value truthiness once against the oracle.

Type system & truthiness

concept 2019.05

OpenSCAD values are: number (f64), boolean, string, vector, range, and undef — plus first-class function values since 2021.01 (and an experimental object type in snapshots only). Type mismatches never throw: they produce undef plus a console WARNING and evaluation continues. Equality (==/!=) is total deep structural comparison; ordering (< <= > >=) is defined for same-type pairs — numbers, strings, booleans, vectors (lexicographic), ranges — and undef for mixed types, function values, and undef. Truthiness: false, 0, "", [], and undef are falsy (exactly five values); everything else — including "false", [0], -1, and nan — is truthy.

numberIEEE-754 double; the only numeric type. nan and inf are values of it, but is_num(nan) is false.
booleantrue/false literals; never coerces to/from numbers (1 == true is false).
stringImmutable UTF-8 text indexed by Unicode code point; s[i] yields a 1-character string (no char type); not a list (is_list false) but iterable.
vectorThe only container: heterogeneous, arbitrarily nested list written [a, b, c]. Also serves as matrix (nested rows) and point.
range[begin:end] / [begin:step:end]; a distinct lazy type — is_list false, len undef; indexing defined only for [0]/[1]/[2] = begin/step/end (mirroring .begin/.step/.end), other indices undef. See the Ranges chapter.
undefThe absent/error value: undefined variables, missing parameters, out-of-range indexing, and every type-mismatched operation produce it (usually with a WARNING).
functionFirst-class function value from a 'function (x) expr' literal; NEW in 2021.01; tested with is_function.
objectSNAPSHOT-ONLY experimental dict-like type (textmetrics results), with dot access and is_object(); absent from 2021.01.

The language is dynamically typed with no coercions of any kind: arithmetic on non-numbers, '+' on strings, comparisons across categories, and similar mismatches yield undef accompanied by a console WARNING (approximately 'WARNING: undefined operation ...' naming the operand types — the warning text and count are observable surface), and evaluation always continues; nothing throws except assert(). undef then propagates silently through further operations (each new mismatched operation warns again). Equality == and inequality != are TOTAL: defined for every pair of values, comparing deeply and structurally — vectors elementwise with length equality required, mismatched types simply unequal (false, never undef), undef == undef true, and nan unequal to everything including itself. Relational operators are defined for any same-type pair except function values and undef — numbers (IEEE), strings (code-point lexicographic), booleans (false < true), vectors (lexicographic, elementwise-recursive), ranges (begin, then step, then element count); every mixed-type combination evaluates to undef with a WARNING. These are two deliberately different comparison regimes in one language and both must be cloned. Truthiness (used by if, the ternary, !, &&, ||, and comprehension filters): exactly false, 0, the empty string "", the empty vector [], and undef are falsy (five values); every other value is truthy — including the strings "false" and "0", the vectors [0], [[]] and [false], negative numbers, inf, and nan (truthiness is a plain != 0 test with no isnan carve-out). The logical operators && and || short-circuit (throughout the reference window; verified back to 2015.03 — only pre-2014 releases evaluated both sides) and return boolean true/false rather than the operand (VERIFY); ! returns a boolean. Type-test builtins (all 2019.05 except is_function, 2021.01): is_undef, is_num (false for nan), is_bool, is_string, is_list (true for [] and any vector, false for ranges and strings), is_function. There is no typeof() and no is_range — a range is detectable only by elimination.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Truthiness table, falsy side exactly five values: false, 0 (and -0), "" (empty string), [] (empty vector), undef. Everything else truthy: "false", "0", " ", [0], [[]], [false], -1, inf, and nan (the toBool test is a plain != 0; nan != 0 is true). VERIFY: range values and function values as conditions — believed truthy; pin.
  • Type-mismatch arithmetic yields undef + WARNING and continues: "a"+1, [1]+2, true+1, "a"+"b" (no string concatenation via + — str() is the only concatenation). VERIFY exact warning format, believed like 'WARNING: undefined operation (string + number)' with file/line.
  • Vector addition has NO broadcasting: [1,2]+1 is undef with warning; [1,2]+[1,2,3] is undef with warning ('+'/'-' require equal lengths, applied recursively to nested vectors). Meanwhile '*' is shape-dispatched: vector*vector is a DOT PRODUCT (scalar), matrix*vector/vector*matrix/matrix*matrix are defined; elementwise multiply does not exist.
  • == is deep structural: [1,[2,"x"]] == [1,[2,"x"]] is true; [1,2] == [1,2,3] is false; 1 == "1" is false; 1 == true is false; undef == false is false; undef == undef is TRUE.
  • nan: nan == nan is false; nan != nan is true; all relationals involving nan are false; is_num(nan) is false (a documented carve-out in is_num, NOT in truthiness); nan is TRUTHY as a condition; echo prints nan. Obtain nan via 0/0.
  • verify vs oracle [nan] == [nan] — believed false (elementwise comparison hits nan != nan); pin, libraries occasionally depend on vector equality.
  • verify vs oracle range equality — [0:1:5] == [0:1:5] believed true (component compare of begin/step/end); a range never equals its expanded vector (different types, so false).
  • verify vs oracle equality between function values — behavior unpinned (identity? always false? undef?); probe before promising anything.
  • Relational operators on MIXED-type pairs (and on function or undef operands, even same-type) yield undef: undef < 1 is undef, "1" < 2 is undef. Same-type pairs are ordered: [1,2] < [1,3] true, false < true true. VERIFY: whether a console WARNING accompanies the undef.
  • String ordering is lexicographic by Unicode code point (not locale, not bytes): "Z" < "a" is true (90 < 97).
  • is_num(inf) — VERIFY, believed true (inf is a number; only nan is excluded).
  • is_list: true for [] and any vector regardless of content; FALSE for strings, ranges, undef.
  • is_undef(never_defined_name) probes without emitting the unknown-variable warning; bare use of the same name warns and yields undef.
  • No is_range and no typeof: detecting a range requires elimination (not num/bool/string/list/undef/function). Keep this gap — do not add helpfully.
  • Indexing: v[i] with a negative or out-of-range i yields undef (no wrap); a fractional in-range index TRUNCATES toward zero (v[1.5] reads element 1).
  • String indexing s[i] yields a ONE-CHARACTER STRING (there is no character type); out-of-range undef. len(s) counts Unicode code points, not bytes.
  • Swizzles v.x/v.y/v.z (pre-2015.03; ranges also expose .begin/.step/.end) read elements 0/1/2 of a vector; on a too-short vector the missing component is undef; on non-vectors undef (VERIFY warning). Only x/y/z exist — no .w, no compound swizzles.
  • len() on non-list/non-string (numbers, booleans, ranges, undef) yields undef. VERIFY whether a warning accompanies it.
  • && and || short-circuit throughout the reference window — verified in the 2015.03 source; only pre-2014 releases evaluated both sides (old code has guard idioms shaped by that); they return boolean true/false, not the last operand (VERIFY — seed flags this). The ternary is lazy on the untaken branch and is THE recursion terminator.
  • Strings are iterable despite not being lists: [for (c="abc") c] gives ["a","b","c"]; each "ab" splices characters. VERIFY version coverage of string iteration in the statement-level for module.
  • Vectors are heterogeneous and nested arbitrarily: [1, "a", [true, [0:2]]] is legal; deep equality recurses through all of it.
  • undef propagation is per-operation: each mismatched operation emits its own WARNING; the GUI console may rate-limit duplicate lines (app behavior, not language semantics).
  • Snapshot-only: object values (from textmetrics()/fontmetrics(), behind --enable=textmetrics) with dot access and is_object(); absent in 2021.01 — is_object is an unknown function there (warning + undef).
echo(is_num(0/0));            // false  (nan; also emits division warning)
echo(1 == "1", 1 == true);  // false, false
echo(true == undef);         // false (mismatched types are unequal)
echo([1,2] < [1,3]);          // true (lexicographic vector ordering, 2021.01)
echo([1,2] + 1);              // undef (WARNING: no broadcasting)
echo([1,2] * [3,4]);          // 11 (dot product)
if ("false") echo("non-empty strings are truthy");
if (!([] || 0)) echo("[] and 0 are falsy");
Implementer: Implement Value as a tagged union with exactly these variants; resist normalizing (no int fast-path leaking into semantics, no bool-number coercion). Two comparison code paths: total structural == and partial IEEE/lexicographic ordering. Route every mismatch through a single warn-and-return-undef helper so warning text/count is centralized and --hardwarnings can hook it. Oracle probes: warning text for each mismatch class, relational-mismatch warning presence, bool ordering, range/function equality, [nan]==[nan], is_num(inf), out-of-range index warning silence, &&/|| return type. The truthiness set and == totality are frozen contracts — test each listed literal.

user-defined $-variables and dynamic scoping

concept pre-2015.03

Every $-prefixed identifier — built-in or invented — is dynamically scoped down the instantiation/call stack, and any call may pass any $name=value as an undeclared named argument.

Setting a $-variable in a scope makes it visible to ALL descendant module instantiations and function calls made while that scope is active (dynamic scoping), unlike plain variables (lexical). Passing $foo=1 as a named argument to any module or function is always accepted without the callee declaring $foo, never triggers the unknown-named-argument warning, and binds $foo for the callee's entire dynamic extent. children() bodies evaluate in the CALLER's lexical scope but under the INSTANTIATING module's current $-environment — the mechanism behind '$fn propagates into children'. Function literals capture their lexical environment for plain names but resolve $-names dynamically at call time.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Reading a never-set $-variable yields undef. VERIFY: whether 2021.01 emits the 'unknown variable' WARNING for unset $-variables (plain variables definitely warn; the seed believes $-vars do not) — pin via oracle; libraries read optional flags like $slop expecting silence.
  • Unknown named NON-$ arguments warn ('unknown parameter') and are ignored; $-named arguments are silently accepted everywhere — this asymmetry is load-bearing for BOSL2.
  • A module parameter literally named with $ (module m($x=1)) is legal: it declares a default for the special and callers may omit it; the default applies only if no dynamic value is inherited? VERIFY exact precedence of declared-default vs inherited dynamic value.
  • for ($i = [0:2]) is legal; the loop variable is then dynamically scoped into calls made from the body. VERIFY.
  • let($fn=12) expr scopes the special for the expression's dynamic extent (including functions it calls).
  • Modifier: assignments to $-vars inside a block do not leak out (scoping rules for blocks still apply); dynamic means 'flows into calls', not 'flows back up'.
  • echo($foo=5) both binds and prints 'VERIFY: whether the named $-arg is printed like a normal named arg ($foo = 5) — pin.'
  • is_undef($never_set) suppresses any warning (same guard behavior as plain variables).
  • Cost warning: every function call must consult the dynamic chain for $-lookups; naive deep-copy of $-environments per call is a known performance trap.
$slop = 0.2;
module socket() { cube(10 + $slop, center = true); }
socket();            // sees 0.2
socket($slop = 0.5); // sees 0.5
Implementer: Implement as a parallel dynamic environment (linked frames) threaded through module instantiation, function application, AND children() re-instantiation. Getting children()'s hybrid scoping wrong is the number-one library breaker; write oracle tests mixing lexical capture and $-inheritance through children().

value display forms (echo/str rendering of every type)

concept pre-2015.03 (function rendering: 2021.01)

The per-type textual rendering shared by echo, assert messages, and (with the string-quoting exception) str().

Vectors: '[' + elements joined by ', ' + ']', recursive, with strings quoted inside regardless of echo/str context. Ranges: bracketed with SPACED colons, e.g. [0 : 1 : 10]. Booleans: true/false. undef: undef. Function values (2021.01): a source-like rendering of the literal. Strings: quoted with '"' by echo and inside containers; emitted bare by str() at top level. Numbers: the shared %g6 formatter.

Edge cases & oddities
  • verify vs oracle range display always shows three fields including an implicit step 1 ([0 : 1 : 10] for [0:10]) — pin; also whether the ORIGINAL begin/end are shown for the deprecated reversed range or the swapped ones.
  • str([1, "a"]) -> '[1, "a"]' — strings inside vectors ARE quoted even via str(); only a top-level string argument is bare. Must clone this asymmetry.
  • Interior quotes/backslashes in strings are NOT escaped on output (echo("a\"b") -> "a"b") — output is not re-parseable; VERIFY exact bytes.
  • Nested vectors: '[[1, 2], [3, 4]]' — comma-space everywhere, no line breaks, no length limits (a huge vector prints huge; GUI may truncate display — app behavior; VERIFY CLI never truncates).
  • Function literal rendering: approximately 'function(x, y = 1) body-expression-ish' — VERIFY exact text (whether the body prints or elides) via oracle; it appears in echo output of BOSL2-style debugging.
  • Empty vector: '[]'; empty string via echo: '""'; via str(): ''.
  • Named-arg wrapper: 'name = ' + display(value).
  • Ranges never expand to elements in display (they are a distinct type).
  • verify vs oracle -0, inf, nan inside vectors use the same tokens (e.g. [inf, nan]).
echo([1, "a", true, undef], [0:10], str("x=", [1, "a"]));
// ECHO: [1, "a", true, undef], [0 : 1 : 10], "x=[1, \"a\"]"  <- VERIFY: quotes NOT escaped in real output
Implementer: One recursive display function with an 'outermost string bare?' flag covers echo vs str. Pin range and function-literal renderings via oracle first — they are the two genuinely uncertain spots.

variable assignment and scoping

concept pre-2015.03

Assignment is declarative, not imperative: within one scope the LAST textual assignment to a name wins, taking effect at the position of the FIRST assignment; every { } block is a child scope that shadows rather than mutates.

nameIdentifier (letters/digits/underscore; $-prefixed names become dynamically scoped specials).
expressionRHS evaluated once per scope evaluation, in dependency-free textual slot order.

A scope (file top level, module body, block, function body's parameter frame) collects its assignments; reassigning a name replaces the expression at the original slot, so a=1; b=a; a=2; gives b==2. Blocks (if/for/let/module bodies) are child scopes: an inner assignment shadows the outer name for that block and can never mutate the outer binding — conditional values must be written x = cond ? a : b. Variables are not hoisted the way module/function definitions are: an expression referencing a name defined only later in the SAME scope sees the (reordered) value per the slot rule, while a truly undefined name warns and yields undef.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Reassignment in one scope warns, approx 'WARNING: variable a was reassigned' — VERIFY exact 2021.01 text (and that the warning points at the later line).
  • The last-write-wins + first-slot rule is the mechanism that makes customizer/-D overrides (injected as TRAILING top-level assignments) override in-file values, and lets an including file override an include'd library's defaults regardless of textual order.
  • Referencing a never-assigned name: 'WARNING: Ignoring unknown variable ...' + undef — VERIFY text; is_undef(name) suppresses this warning (defined-guard idiom).
  • Inner-scope shadowing emits no warning in 2021.01 — VERIFY (some versions discussed a shadow warning).
  • Assignments interleave with geometry statements in a scope: pin against the oracle exactly when RHS evaluation happens relative to instantiations (echo ordering exposes it).
  • There are no compound assignments (+=, ++), no multiple-target assignment, and assignment is a statement only — never an expression.
  • Assigning a $-name at any scope makes it dynamically visible to the whole subtree/call-chain below.
  • Geometry is not assignable: x = cube(1); is a syntax error (instantiations are statements, not expressions).
a = 1; b = a; a = 2; echo(b); // ECHO: 2 (with a reassignment warning)
Implementer: Implement scopes as ordered maps where reassignment replaces in place; evaluate slots in order. This rule is the foundation of include-override and -D semantics — golden-test it early. Warning texts are compat surface under --hardwarnings.

Variables & scoping

concept 2015.03

OpenSCAD assignment is declarative, not imperative: within one scope the textually LAST assignment to a name provides its single value, effective at the position of the FIRST assignment; every { } block is a child scope that can only shadow, never mutate, outer bindings; variables, functions, and modules live in three separate namespaces with hoisted definitions; there is no mutation anywhere in the language.

name = expr;Assignment statement. The first textual assignment to a name in a scope creates that name's slot at that position; later assignments in the SAME scope replace the expression stored in that original slot (last-write-wins) and emit a reassignment warning. No compound assignment (+=, ++, --) exists.
{ } block scopesModule bodies, if/else branches, for bodies, let bodies, and bare { } grouping blocks are each a child scope. Assignments inside always create fresh shadowing bindings; they can never modify an enclosing scope's variable.
module/function definitionsLexically scoped and hoisted within their scope: callable before their textual definition. Duplicate definitions in one scope warn, last wins. They occupy namespaces separate from variables — one identifier can simultaneously name a variable, a function, and a module; syntax disambiguates (statement call = module, expression call = function, bare name = variable).
let (a=e1, b=e2) ...Expression form and (2019.05+) statement form. Bindings evaluate SEQUENTIALLY, each seeing earlier ones; creates a new scope shadowing outer names; there is no last-write-wins across let bindings.
assign (a=1) { ... }Deprecated ancestor of the let statement; still parses and works in 2021.01 with a deprecation warning. Legacy scripts use it — must be supported.
include <f> / use <f>include splices the file's top level into the including scope (its assignments join the same last-write-wins arena, with the main file's assignment overriding the included file's); use imports only module/function definitions, evaluating the used file's top-level variables privately (2019.05+).
-D name=value / customizer presetsInjected as assignments appended at the END of the top-level scope, so they beat in-file assignments via last-write-wins. The script cannot distinguish overridden values from literals.

Each lexical scope holds an ordered list of assignment slots plus a list of other statements (module instantiations, echo, assert, if/for, etc.). The first textual assignment to a name creates that name's slot at that position; any later assignment to the same name in the same scope replaces the expression in the ORIGINAL slot rather than creating a new binding, and emits a reassignment warning. When the scope is instantiated, assignment slots are evaluated in slot order, and the scope's non-assignment statements then execute against the final values — so 'a=1; echo(a); a=5;' prints ECHO: 5, and 'a=1; b=a; a=2;' yields b==2 (a's slot, holding the winning expression 2, evaluates before b's slot). Forward references between assignments do NOT work: an assignment whose RHS names a variable whose only slot comes later in the same scope (and which is not bound in an enclosing scope) reads undef with an unknown-variable warning — variables are not hoisted the way definitions are. Child scopes see enclosing bindings lexically; an inner assignment shadows for that scope only, which is why 'x=5; if (c) { x=6; }' leaves outer x at 5 and conditional values must be written 'x = c ? a : b;'. There is no while, break, continue, return, or any mutation; all iteration is data-driven and all function logic is a single expression. Module parameters bind positionally then by name (named args in any order); a missing parameter without a default is undef; defaults may reference EARLIER parameters of the same signature. This model is a compatibility contract: an implementation that 'fixes' last-write-wins or block shadowing to be imperative breaks real scripts, and the documented library-override idiom (assign in the including file to override an include'd library default) depends on it.

Edge cases & oddities
  • a=1; b=a; a=2; yields b==2: the winning expression evaluates at a's original (first) slot position, before b's slot. A warning is emitted. VERIFY: exact 2021.01 reassignment warning text — believed of the form 'WARNING: a was assigned on line 1 but was overwritten' plus file/line; capture verbatim from the oracle.
  • a=1; echo(a); a=5; prints ECHO: 5 — non-assignment statements in a scope observe the scope's FINAL variable values, even when textually between the two assignments.
  • verify vs oracle exact interleaving of assignment evaluation vs statement execution (believed: all of a scope's assignments evaluate, in slot order, before any of its instantiations run) — pin with echo probes mixing assignments and echoes.
  • Forward reference among assignments fails: 'b=a; a=2;' gives b=undef with an unknown-variable warning (approx 'WARNING: Ignoring unknown variable 'a''). VERIFY the warning fires here and the exact text.
  • Self-reference: in a child scope with an outer x, 'x = x + 1;' reads the OUTER x (outer x=5 gives inner x==6) because the RHS is evaluated before the inner binding exists. VERIFY: at top level 'x=1; x=x+1;' collapses both assignments into one slot whose winning expression references its own name — believed to produce undef plus unknown-variable and reassignment warnings, NOT 2. Pin both cases.
  • verify vs oracle 'x=5; if (true) { echo(x); x=6; }' — believed the inner echo prints 6 (the inner scope has its own hoisted-slot x whose assignments complete before inner statements run), while echo after the block prints 5.
  • verify vs oracle whether 2021.01 emits any warning when a child-scope assignment shadows an outer variable (some historical versions warned; believed silent in 2021.01).
  • Reading a never-assigned variable warns approx 'WARNING: Ignoring unknown variable 'foo'' and yields undef; is_undef(foo) performs the same probe WITHOUT the warning — the idiomatic defined-guard. This asymmetry is load-bearing for libraries.
