  • Non-manifold/broken child meshes (bad polyhedron, damaged STL import): failures surface only at F6/CSG time with CGAL assertion errors (approx. 'ERROR: CGAL error in CGAL_Nef_polyhedron3()...') — preview happily draws them. VERIFY representative texts.
  • %-background children are EXCLUDED from the union (ghost drawn in preview only); *-disabled children are excluded everywhere; #-highlight children are included normally.
  • Coincident/overlapping identical children are fine (idempotent), though exact kernels pay for coplanar-face work — performance, not semantics.
  • 2D union: hole/island resolution follows the clipping kernel's rules; results are polygons-with-holes, and micro-scale quantization differs from 3D (fixed-point grid).
  • Unknown parameters (e.g. union(convexity=2)): believed WARNING + ignored — union takes no parameters at all; VERIFY warning text for unexpected named/positional args to builtin modules.
  • Preview vs render: F5 shows children independently (visual overlap only, GPU-composited); F6 computes the real union — geometry-dependent differences (e.g. internal walls visible through transparency in preview) are expected app behavior.
  • An empty union at top level with nothing else: export attempts emit approx. 'Current top level object is empty.' VERIFY text and severity.
  • Modifier ! on a child of a union prunes the design to just that child.
union() {
  cube(10);
  translate([5, 5, 5]) sphere(8);
}
Implementer: Implement one n-ary union primitive and reuse it for every implicit grouping so explicit/implicit union are bit-identical. An always-exact pipeline is fine, but keep a per-subtree geometry cache (identical subtree => reuse) or real-world for-loop scripts will be unusably slow — the cache is de facto compatibility surface. Pin via oracle: mixing-dimensionality error text, 2-manifold warning text, empty-top-level message, and unknown-argument warnings on parameterless booleans.

Modifier characters

! (show-only / root modifier)

modifier pre-2015.03 
! module_instantiation — e.g. !cube(5); — prefixes exactly one instantiation; the marked subtree becomes the design root

Makes the marked subtree the ONLY thing evaluated — everything else in the design disappears from preview, render, and exports — while transformations from the marked node's ancestors still apply to it.

The bang designates its instantiation as the design root: the rest of the tree is pruned and only the marked subtree is produced. Crucially, the chain of ANCESTOR nodes from the true root down to the marked node stays in effect for it — ancestor translate/rotate/scale/multmatrix (and believed ancestor color(); VERIFY) still apply, so the isolated part stays in its correct position rather than jumping to the origin. This makes ! the standard 'zoom into one part' debugging tool. Unlike # and %, the root modifier is NOT preview-only: F6 render and CLI exports also honor it — an accidentally committed ! silently exports only a fragment of a model. Side effects elsewhere in the design (echo, assert, warnings from pruned statements) are believed to still execute, since pruning happens at CSG-tree level, not at instantiation (VERIFY).

Edge cases & oddities
  • VERIFY (the classic pin-against-oracle item): MULTIPLE ! marks in one design — candidates: the first !-node encountered in depth-first instantiation order wins and later ones are ignored, vs. the last/deepest wins, vs. a nested ! INSIDE the chosen root's subtree re-roots deeper. Probe with sibling bangs, nested bangs, and a bang inside a module instantiated repeatedly.
  • ! inside a module definition marks the node at EVERY instantiation of that module — with multiple instantiations you get the multiple-! resolution rule; the visible result typically isolates one instantiation (which one = the VERIFY above).
  • Ancestor transforms apply; sibling and cousin geometry vanishes — including geometry in completely separate top-level statements and include'd files.
  • ! inside a *-disabled subtree is never reached (disable short-circuits instantiation — VERIFY, coupled to the * entry's probe).
  • Stacking: !#x and !%x are legal — the subtree becomes the root AND keeps highlight/background treatment (a !%-marked subtree is believed to display as ghost with an EMPTY actual result — VERIFY this amusing corner).
  • echo/assert/warning side effects in PRUNED regions: believed still emitted (full instantiation happens first). VERIFY with echo-probes on both sides of the bang.
  • Affects F6 and all CLI exports, not just preview — 'preview-only' mental models are wrong for !.
  • Applies to exactly one instantiation; !{ ... } bare block believed a parse error (as with all modifiers — VERIFY); !for(...) / !if(...) believed accepted (VERIFY).
  • The pruned design's $-variable environment and evaluation order are unchanged — ! never alters scoping, only which geometry survives.
  • With ! on a child of difference()/intersection(), the boolean itself is discarded; you see the raw child (unsubtracted/unclipped), positioned by its ancestors.
  • verify vs oracle .csg export of a design containing ! — believed to contain only the rooted subtree (with its ancestor transform chain); pin exact structure.
sphere(10);                         // disappears
translate([20, 0, 0]) !cube(3);     // only this cube shows, still at x=20
Implementer: Implement as a 'root override' pointer captured during tree building: keep the ancestor chain, drop everything else. The multiple-!/nested-! winner rule and side-effect observability are the two things you must pin with oracle probes before freezing — both are noted as unpinned in the seed. Keep ! active in your export path (not just interactive view) to match the official binary.

# (highlight / debug modifier)

modifier pre-2015.03 
# module_instantiation — e.g. #cylinder(h=10, r=2); — prefixes exactly one instantiation

Highlights the marked subtree in translucent red/pink in F5 preview while leaving it FULLY part of the CSG result — it still cuts, still unions, still renders at F6, and still appears in every export.

The hash tags its subtree for debug display: in F5/OpenCSG preview the geometry is drawn an additional time in the color scheme's highlight color (translucent red/pink, ~50% alpha in the default scheme), superimposed on wherever the geometry logically is — including geometry that is being SUBTRACTED. That is the killer feature: #-marking a subtrahend of difference() shows the cutter as a pink ghost in place while it continues to cut, letting users see and align their negative space. Geometrically # is a complete no-op: F6 render output, CSG evaluation, and all exports (STL/OFF/AMF/3MF/DXF/SVG/PNG --render) contain the marked geometry exactly as if unmarked. The preview-vs-render asymmetry (visible tint in F5, none in the exported geometry) is part of the observable language surface.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Zero geometric effect, everywhere: booleans, hull, extrusions, bounding boxes, exports all see the geometry normally. Only preview DISPLAY changes.
  • verify vs oracle whether the pink highlight is also drawn in the post-F6 render VIEW (believed the highlight display is F5-only and the rendered view shows plain geometry) — display-level, but observable and worth pinning for a reimplementation's viewer.
  • #-marked subtrahends both cut AND ghost — the debug idiom; the pink ghost occupies the cut volume in preview.
  • The highlight color comes from the active color scheme (app preference), is translucent, and OVERRIDES color() for the highlight pass — a #color("blue") x shows the blue object plus pink ghost overlay (VERIFY exact visual compositing).
  • Stacking: #% together — believed both effects apply (background exclusion wins geometrically: the object leaves the CSG result per %, displays with some combination of ghost colors). VERIFY precedence/visuals for #%, %#, !#, and *# (star wins, nothing shows).
  • Inside hull()/minkowski(): child modifiers are dropped/consumed — a # inside contributes as plain geometry and the highlight of inner children is believed not drawn (VERIFY).
  • Applies to one instantiation incl. its whole subtree; nested #'s are redundant but legal; #{ ... } bare block believed parse error; #for(...)/#if(...) believed accepted (VERIFY, as with all modifiers).
  • Works on 2D children too (pink slab in preview); no effect on exported DXF/SVG.
  • # does not affect $-variables, evaluation order, side effects, or caching — purely a display tag on the node.
  • verify vs oracle .csg export preserves the # prefix character on the corresponding node (believed yes) — pin, since it makes csg dumps a cheap oracle for modifier plumbing.
  • CLI/headless: --export-format outputs are unaffected by #; PNG via --preview shows the highlight, PNG via --render believed not (VERIFY).
difference() {
  cube(10);
  #translate([5, 5, -1]) cylinder(h = 12, r = 3);  // see the cutter
}
Implementer: Implement as a display flag propagated on the CSG node into your preview renderer's 'highlight products' pass; keep it entirely out of the geometry pipeline. Even an always-exact single-pipeline implementation must emulate the F5 ghost (draw the marked geometry again, translucent red, depth-composited) because scripts are written around seeing their cutters. Pin the default highlight RGBA and the #%-stacking visuals against the oracle if pixel-level preview parity matters.

% (background / transparent modifier)

modifier pre-2015.03 
% module_instantiation — e.g. %cube([60, 60, 1]); — prefixes exactly one instantiation

Turns the marked subtree into reference-only 'background' geometry: drawn as a translucent gray ghost in F5 preview but EXCLUDED from the CSG result, the F6 render, and every export.

The percent sign removes its subtree from the actual design while keeping it visible: in F5 preview the geometry is drawn in the color scheme's background style (translucent gray) at its correct location, but it participates in NO Boolean operation, contributes nothing to F6 render output, and is absent from all exports (STL/OFF/AMF/3MF/DXF/SVG and --render PNGs). It is the canonical marker for jigs, build-plate outlines, enclosure envelopes, and other context geometry. The subtree IS still instantiated and tessellated (its echoes print, its asserts fire, its evaluation costs time) — only its geometry products are diverted from the CSG tree into the preview-only background display list. The preview-vs-render asymmetry is observable language surface, the mirror image of #.

Edge cases & oddities
  • %-marked subtrahend of difference(): does NOT cut (excluded from CSG); shows the would-be cutter as a gray ghost — standard alignment-preview trick.
  • %-marked FIRST child of difference(): believed excluded from the operation so the NEXT child becomes the minuend (parallel to the *-promotion case) — VERIFY with oracle; the alternative (empty minuend => empty result) changes real scripts' output.
  • % child of intersection(): believed excluded, so it cannot annihilate the intersection (VERIFY).
  • A design whose only geometry is %-marked renders/exports EMPTY: export emits approx. 'Current top level object is empty.' (VERIFY text).
  • Side effects still run: %echo-containing subtrees print; %assert(false) fires; tessellation cost is still paid (unlike *). VERIFY the assert case specifically.
  • Display color: color() inside a %-marked subtree DOES tint the background ghost — since 2013.06 the % operator preserves manually set colors; only uncolored background geometry draws in the color scheme's background gray.
  • verify vs oracle whether background ghosts remain visible in the viewport AFTER an F6 render (believed the render view retains the ghost overlay; candidates: visible vs. hidden until next F5). Display-level but user-visible.
  • Inside hull()/minkowski(): modifiers of inner children are dropped — a %-child is believed to contribute as NORMAL geometry to the hull/minkowski result (VERIFY; seed gotcha).
  • Inside resize()/render() and for bounding-box purposes: background geometry produces no CSG geometry, so it is excluded from measured bounding boxes (VERIFY for resize specifically).
  • Stacking: %* = gone entirely (star wins); %# both-marked — VERIFY visual/geometric precedence; !% roots a background-only subtree (believed ghost-only, empty result — VERIFY).
  • Applies to one instantiation and its whole subtree; nested %'s redundant; %{ ... } bare block believed parse error; %for(...)/%if(...) believed accepted (VERIFY).
  • 2D children: ghost slab in preview, excluded from 2D booleans/extrusions consuming the parent scope? — NO: exclusion is from the CSG products of wherever the node sits; a % 2D child under linear_extrude contributes nothing to the extrusion (VERIFY).
  • Preview PNG (--preview) includes ghosts; render PNG (--render) does not (VERIFY).
  • verify vs oracle .csg export preserves the % prefix on the node (believed yes).
%cube([60, 60, 1]);          // build plate, reference only
translate([10, 10, 1]) cylinder(h = 8, r = 5);
Implementer: Implement as a 'background products' diversion at CSG-tree build time: the subtree evaluates normally (side effects and cost included) but its geometry is routed to a preview-only display list and never reaches Boolean evaluation or export. The difference()-first-child promotion question and the hull/minkowski modifier-dropping rule are the two semantics-changing VERIFY items — probe them before freezing. Everything else is display plumbing; pin the ghost RGBA only if pixel parity matters.

* (disable modifier)

modifier pre-2015.03 
* module_instantiation — e.g. *cube(5); or *translate([1,0,0]) { ... } — prefixes exactly one instantiation statement

Comments out one instantiation and its entire subtree: the marked geometry is ignored in preview, render, and every export — the quick way to remove a statement without deleting it.

The asterisk is a parser-level prefix on a single module instantiation (which may be a transform, boolean, if/for/let statement, or user-module call heading an arbitrarily large subtree). A disabled node is dropped from the design in BOTH F5 preview and F6 render and from all exports — unlike # and %, disable has no preview-vs-render asymmetry. Disabling is total: the subtree contributes no geometry anywhere. Modifier characters are grammar-recursive, so several may stack before one statement (*!x, %*x); when * is present it dominates — the subtree is simply gone. Because the node disappears from the tree, its position among siblings matters: disabling the FIRST child of a difference() promotes the next child to minuend.

Edge cases & oddities
  • VERIFY (important): whether a *-disabled subtree is instantiated at all — believed instantiation short-circuits, so echo()/assert() inside a disabled subtree do NOT print/fire, and expensive children are never evaluated (this is why * is the standard way to skip a heavy subtree). Pin with *echo("x"); and *assert(false); probes — an implementation that evaluates-then-discards would diverge observably on both.
  • Applies to exactly ONE following instantiation; to disable several sibling statements users must star each one or wrap them in a starred union()/translate(). VERIFY: a modifier before a bare { } block ( *{ ... } ) is believed a PARSE ERROR — modifiers attach to module instantiations, not naked blocks.
  • *-first-child-of-difference promotes the second child to minuend; *-child-of-intersection removes it from the intersection (cannot annihilate).
  • Stacking with other modifiers: * wins over !, #, % on the same statement (a disabled root-marked node is just gone; a ! inside a disabled subtree is never seen). VERIFY each pairing against the oracle.
  • Believed accepted before for/if/let/echo/assert statements (grammar treats them as instantiations): *for (i=[0:5]) ... disables the whole loop. VERIFY.
  • Inside a module definition, the * disables that statement in EVERY instantiation of the module.
  • No expression-level meaning — '*' in expressions is multiplication; the modifier exists only in statement position, immediately before an instantiation (whitespace between * and the name is allowed — VERIFY).
  • A disabled node still requires syntactically valid content (parse errors inside are still errors — this is not comment syntax).
  • Disabling the only top-level geometry yields an empty design; export then emits approx. 'Current top level object is empty.' (VERIFY).
  • verify vs oracle .csg export omits disabled subtrees entirely (believed absent from the dump); the AST export retains them.
difference() {
  cube(10);
  *translate([5, 5, -1]) cylinder(h = 12, r = 3);  // hole disabled
}
Implementer: Implement as an early-out at instantiation time (matching the believed no-side-effects behavior once verified) rather than a post-hoc tree filter — both the performance expectation and echo/assert observability depend on it. Grammar: modifier characters are recursive productions before module_instantiation; support arbitrary stacking with defined precedence pinned by oracle probes of every 2-modifier combination.

Extrusions

linear_extrude

module pre-2015.03 (core); geometry-aware default-slices heuristics new in 2021.01; segments=, v=, h= snapshot-only (post-2021.01) 
linear_extrude(height = 100, center = false, convexity = 1, twist = 0, slices = <computed>, scale = 1)  //  true positional order (legacy): (file, layer, height, origin, scale, center, twist, slices); snapshot-only: v = [x,y,z], h (alias for height), segments

Extrudes its (implicitly unioned) 2D children along +Z into a 3D solid, optionally twisting about the Z axis and linearly scaling the cross-section from bottom to top. One of only three 2D<->3D bridges in the language (with rotate_extrude and projection).

heightExtrusion length along Z. DEFAULT IS 100 (historical, inherited from dxf_linear_extrude) — 'linear_extrude() square(5);' is 100 units tall. Reachable as a bare numeric first argument only via an explicit special case (see edge cases). Must be a finite positive number; non-finite/non-numeric falls back to the default.
centerBoolean, default false. false: solid spans z=0..height. true: spans z=-height/2..+height/2. Affects Z placement only; twist/scale semantics unchanged (twist still measured bottom-to-top).
convexityPreview-only hint (max number of front-facing surfaces a ray can cross); default 1, values <= 0 clamped back to 1, fractional values truncated to int. Never affects rendered/exported geometry. See the 'convexity' concept entry.
twistTotal rotation in DEGREES of the top cross-section relative to the bottom, about the Z axis THROUGH THE ORIGIN (not the shape centroid — an off-origin profile sweeps a helix). POSITIVE twist turns the top CLOCKWISE seen from +Z (left-hand rule): the section at height fraction t is rotate(-twist*t) of the profile. May be negative (CCW) or exceed 360 (multiple turns). Default 0.
slicesNumber of intermediate layers along Z. Explicit value used verbatim. Default is computed: 1 with no twist and uniform (or no) scale; with twist and/or non-uniform scale, 2021.01 derives it from $fn/$fa/$fs via helix-arc-length heuristics (see edge cases for the believed exact formulas). Controls the polygonal silhouette of twisted parts.
scaleScalar or [sx, sy] vector, default 1. Cross-section is scaled linearly from 1 at the bottom to this value at the top; scale=0 collapses the top to a point (cone/pyramid); a single zero component collapses the top to a line. Negative components are clamped to 0 (no mirrored tops). Named-only in 2021.01 (not reachable positionally). In legacy file-mode, scale instead scales the DXF data.
file / layer / origin / scale (file-mode)DEPRECATED legacy dxf_linear_extrude mode, still functional in 2021.01: linear_extrude(file="x.dxf", layer=..., origin=[x,y], scale=s, height=...) extrudes a DXF outline directly, emitting a deprecation warning telling you to use a child import() instead.
v (snapshot)SNAPSHOT-ONLY (2023+): [x,y,z] extrusion direction vector for oblique extrusion; 'h' is likewise accepted as an alias for height in snapshots only. Rejected/warned as unknown parameters in 2021.01.

All children are evaluated, forced to 2D, implicitly unioned and sanitized through the 2D polygon kernel (self-intersections resolved, winding normalized) before extrusion. The resulting polygon-with-holes is stamped as (slices+1) rings from bottom to top; the ring at height fraction t = 0..1 is placed at z = t*height (shifted by -height/2 when center=true), with each 2D point p transformed by first applying the interpolated scale (lerp from [1,1] to [sx,sy]) and then the interpolated rotation of -twist*t degrees about the origin (VERIFY order: scale-in-profile-frame-then-rotate; observable when sx != sy combined with twist). Side walls connect consecutive rings as quads split into two triangles; bottom and top caps come from triangulating the 2D polygon (holes supported). Output is always 3D.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Default height is 100 when the parameter is omitted — a famous historical trap; 'linear_extrude() square(5);' silently produces a 100-tall bar.
  • verify vs oracle height <= 0 (zero or negative) produces empty geometry; believed silent (no warning) in 2021.01. Snapshots have discussed negative-height behavior changes — do not extrapolate; pin 2021.01.
  • verify vs oracle non-numeric height (string, boolean, vector, undef) and non-finite height (nan/inf) fall back to the default 100; believed silent in 2021.01 (the finite-double getter simply declines to overwrite the default).
  • A 3D child is skipped with 'WARNING: Ignoring 3D child object for 2D operation' (approximate text; suffixed with ', in file <path>, line <N>.') — it is a warning plus ignore, NOT a hard error; remaining 2D children still extrude.
  • Multiple 2D children are implicitly unioned first; overlaps merge, and the union runs through the 2D (Clipper-class, fixed-point) kernel, so extruded output inherits that kernel's coordinate quantization.
  • An empty 2D child set (e.g. a fully annihilated offset()) yields an empty 3D result with no error.
  • Twist sign: POSITIVE twist rotates the top CLOCKWISE viewed from +Z (top ring equals rotate(-twist) of the profile). Getting this backwards mirrors every twisted model — pin with an asymmetric-profile oracle probe.
  • Twist axis is the Z axis through the ORIGIN, not the child's centroid: translate([6,0]) square(2) with twist=360 sweeps a helical band of radius ~6, it does not twist in place.
  • twist > 360 and negative twist are legal (multiple turns / CCW).
  • Default slices without twist: 1 for uniform or no scale. VERIFY (2021.01 heuristic): with non-uniform scale (sx != sy) and no twist, slices defaults to a 'diagonal' estimate — believed max(1, ceil(sqrt(max_delta^2 + height^2)/$fs)) where max_delta is the largest per-vertex displacement between unscaled and scaled outline, or trunc($fn) when $fn > 0.
  • Default slices with twist (2021.01, believed formulas — VERIFY each against oracle): minimum slices = max(ceil(|twist|/120), 1) (never more than 120 deg of twist per slice); if $fn > 0, slices = max(ceil(|twist|/360 * $fn), minimum); else slices = max(min(ceil(|twist|/$fa), ceil(helix_arc_length/$fs)), minimum), where helix_arc_length is computed from the profile's maximum vertex radius, height, and twist. With both twist and uniform scale != 1 a conical-helix variant is used; with twist plus non-uniform scale the max of the twist estimate and the diagonal estimate is used.
  • VERIFY version difference: releases before 2021.01 used a cruder default (approximately fragments*|twist|/360 with a small minimum); the geometry-aware heuristics above change the silhouette of twisted parts between versions — pin which formula your baseline clones.
  • verify vs oracle explicit slices is truncated to an integer and clamped to a minimum of 1; slices=0 believed treated as 1.
  • With twist active, outlines are additionally auto-subdivided from $fn/$fa/$fs (splitOutlineByFn-style, longest-edges-first) so straight profile edges follow the helix even without segments=; VERIFY the exact subdivision rule and its interaction with an explicit segments=.
  • segments= is SNAPSHOT-ONLY (post-2021.01) — in 2021.01 passing segments= triggers the ordinary unknown-parameter warning and is ignored. In snapshots it only changes the mesh when it exceeds the outline vertex count; at twist=0/scale=1 the added vertices are collinear (shape unchanged, exported vertex count changed).
  • scale accepts scalar or [sx,sy]; VERIFY: a 3-element vector's z component is ignored (believed) rather than warned; non-numeric scale falls back to 1.
  • Negative scale components are clamped to 0 (believed, VERIFY): scale=[-1,1] behaves as [0,1]; you cannot produce a mirrored top via scale.
  • scale=0 produces an apex: the top ring degenerates to the single point (0,0,ztop); one zero component degenerates the top to a segment. Both produce valid, closed meshes in 2021.01 (degenerate triangles culled).
  • scale interacts with twist per-slice: both are linearly interpolated in t; VERIFY transform order (believed: scale applied in the un-rotated profile frame, then rotation) — with scale=[2,1] and twist=90 the two orders give visibly different solids.
  • Insufficient slices with large twist produces self-intersecting side walls; 2021.01 accepts this silently in preview and it typically only fails (or produces garbage) when the result participates in CSG at F6 — clone the non-validation.
  • Legacy file-mode: linear_extrude(file="...") still works in 2021.01 and emits approximately 'DEPRECATED: Support for reading files in linear_extrude(-modules) will be removed in future releases. Use a child import() instead.' (VERIFY exact text); layer/origin select and shift DXF data. In file-mode, VERIFY which meaning 'scale' takes (DXF scaling vs top-scale).
  • convexity <= 0 or fractional: truncated to int, clamped to 1; affects preview only.
  • $fn/$fa/$fs are read from the dynamic environment at the linear_extrude node itself (they feed the slices default and outline subdivision) AND independently by circle/text children when those tessellate — the same $fn does double duty.
  • SNAPSHOT-ONLY (2023+): v=[x,y,z] oblique extrusion direction and h= alias for height; in 2021.01 these are unknown parameters (warned and ignored) — an implementation targeting 2021.01 must NOT accept them silently.
  • verify vs oracle unknown named arguments to this builtin produce a warning of approximately 'WARNING: variable v not specified as parameter, in file <path>, line <N>.' and are ignored — except $-prefixed ones, which are always accepted as dynamically scoped specials.
  • The declared positional order is the legacy (file, layer, height, origin, scale, center, twist, slices); linear_extrude(10) works only via a special case treating a numeric first unnamed argument as height when height is unbound. scale is formally reachable positionally (5th slot), practically only via the legacy file-mode call shape.
  • Negative scale components are clamped to 0; height <= 0 is clamped to 0 silently; a non-bool center is silently ignored; file-mode deprecation text: 'Support for reading files in linear_extrude will be removed in future releases. Use a child import() instead.'
linear_extrude(height = 30, center = true, twist = 180, slices = 60, scale = [2, 0.5], convexity = 4)
    translate([6, 0]) square([8, 4], center = true);
Implementer: Pin via oracle before freezing: (1) the exact default-slices formulas for all four regimes (none / twist / non-uniform scale / twist+scale) including rounding and minimums, by counting mesh rings in exported STL across $fn/$fa/$fs sweeps; (2) twist sign with an L-shaped profile; (3) scale-then-rotate vs rotate-then-scale with scale=[2,1], twist=90; (4) negative-scale clamping; (5) segments availability and subdivision distribution; (6) whether height<=0 warns. The .csg dump normalizes the node (e.g. 'linear_extrude(height = 30, center = true, convexity = 4, twist = 180, slices = 60, scale = [2, 0.5], $fn = 0, $fa = 12, $fs = 2)') and is the cheapest oracle for parameter parsing/defaults — VERIFY which parameters are omitted from the dump when unset. Safe to simplify: cap triangulation choice and side-quad diagonal direction (promise mesh equivalence, not identical triangulation) unless you clone .csg/STL byte tests. Not safe to simplify: default height 100, twist sign/origin, slices default (changes silhouettes), warning-not-error on 3D children.

rotate_extrude

module pre-2015.03 (core); angle= added 2019.05; start= snapshot-only 
rotate_extrude(angle = 360, convexity = 2)  //  legacy named-only: file, layer, origin, scale; snapshot-only: start = 0

Revolves its (implicitly unioned) 2D children about the Z axis: the profile's +X becomes the radius and its +Y becomes +Z. Since 2019.05 a partial sweep angle is supported; all profile points must lie on one side of the X=0 line.

angleSweep angle in degrees, default 360 (full revolution). Added in 2019.05 — earlier releases always revolve fully and warn about the unknown parameter. Positive sweeps CCW (viewed from +Z) starting at the +X half-plane, covering [0, angle]; negative sweeps CW covering [angle, 0]. Values are clamped: angle > 360 becomes 360, and (VERIFY) angle <= -360 also becomes a full 360 revolution. Partial sweeps get flat planar end caps.
convexityPreview-only hint; for rotate_extrude the default is believed to be 2 (VERIFY — values <= 0 are reset to 2, unlike most nodes' 1). Never affects exported geometry.
file / layer / origin / scaleDEPRECATED legacy dxf_rotate_extrude mode, still functional in 2021.01: revolve a DXF outline read directly from file; emits the same 'use a child import() instead' deprecation warning as linear_extrude's file mode.
start (snapshot)SNAPSHOT-ONLY (2023+): starting angle of the sweep, so the revolution covers [start, start+angle] instead of beginning at the +X half-plane. Unknown parameter (warned, ignored) in 2021.01.

Children are forced to 2D and implicitly unioned. Every profile point (x, y) maps to (x*cos(theta), x*sin(theta), y) — i.e. the 2D Y axis becomes the 3D Z axis, and at theta=0 the profile appears unmodified in the XZ half-plane on the +X side. The sweep is discretized into fragments copies of the profile: fragments = max(1, trunc(full_circle_fragments(max_radius, $fn, $fa, $fs) * |angle| / 360)) (VERIFY trunc-vs-ceil), where max_radius is the profile's largest |x| and full_circle_fragments is the standard circle formula (r < 2^-20 -> 3; $fn>0 -> max(trunc($fn),3); else ceil(max(min(360/$fa, 2*pi*r/$fs), 5))). For a full revolution the first and last rings are welded (no caps); for partial sweeps flat caps are placed at theta=0 (the y=0, x>=0 half-plane) and theta=angle. The seam / vertex phase is fixed and observable: ring 0 is exactly at theta=0 on +X, matching circle()'s first-vertex-at-angle-0 convention. Profiles with holes revolve into hollow solids. Output is always 3D.

Edge cases & oddities
  • X-sign rule: every profile point must satisfy x >= 0, or every point x <= 0. A profile crossing X=0 aborts the node with approximately 'ERROR: all points for rotate_extrude() must have the same X coordinate sign (range is -a.aa -> b.bb)' (the range formatted with two decimals — VERIFY exact format); evaluation of the rest of the script continues and this node contributes empty geometry.
  • Points exactly at x=0 are legal (profile touching the axis); their revolved vertices coincide on the axis and are merged/handled to produce a valid closed solid.
  • An all-negative-x profile is accepted and produces (VERIFY) the same solid as the mirrored positive profile, with face orientation corrected so normals still point outward — pin mesh orientation with an oracle probe.
  • verify vs oracle no epsilon tolerance for slightly-negative x in 2021.01 — a vertex at x = -1e-9 in an otherwise positive profile triggers the sign error (some historical releases were laxer).
  • A 3D child is skipped with 'WARNING: Ignoring 3D child object for 2D operation' — warning plus ignore, not a hard error.
  • angle parameter only exists since 2019.05; under 2015.03 semantics passing angle= produces an unknown-parameter warning and a full revolution.
  • angle clamping (VERIFY exact rule, believed 2021.01): angle > 360 -> 360; angle <= -360 -> 360 (i.e. large negative requests become a full CCW revolution, not a full CW one); angle exactly 360 or -360 -> full weld with no caps.
  • verify vs oracle angle = 0 produces empty geometry (believed silent).
  • Negative angle sweeps clockwise from the +X half-plane down into negative Y, occupying angular range [angle, 0]; the profile copy at theta=0 is still the seam/start.
  • Partial-angle fragment starvation: fragments = max(1, trunc(circle_fragments * |angle|/360)) means small angles collapse to 1 fragment regardless of $fn ($fn=100, angle=3 -> ONE flat wedge, not a smooth 3-degree arc). This is upstream behavior users complain about — clone it; VERIFY trunc vs ceil vs round with $fn=100, angle=90.5.
  • Fragment count is computed from the LARGEST radius in the profile; inner features share the same angular resolution (they are not tessellated finer or coarser).
  • Full-revolution seam: the profile ring at theta=0 is the mesh seam; VERIFY whether seam vertices are duplicated or welded in exported STL/OFF (affects vertex-count golden tests).
  • A zero-width profile lying entirely on the X=0 axis degenerates; VERIFY: believed to produce empty geometry or a CGAL error at render rather than a clean error at instantiation.
  • Empty 2D children yield an empty result silently; empty children also do not trigger the X-sign check.
  • There is no twist, scale, or slices for rotate_extrude in any release; those parameters are warned as unknown.
  • convexity <= 0 resets to the default (believed 2 — VERIFY); fractional truncated; preview-only.
  • Legacy file= mode still live in 2021.01 with a DEPRECATED warning; origin/scale/layer apply to the DXF data.
  • SNAPSHOT-ONLY (2023+): start= shifts the sweep's starting half-plane; combined with angle it covers [start, start+angle]. In 2021.01 it is an unknown parameter.
  • The 2D profile is sanitized (self-intersections resolved, winding normalized) by the 2D kernel before revolving, exactly as for linear_extrude.
rotate_extrude(angle = 270, convexity = 2, $fn = 64)
    translate([12, 0]) circle(r = 4);
Implementer: Oracle probes to pin: (1) fragment rounding for partial angles (sweep $fn and angle, count profile rings in exported mesh); (2) the angle-clamp rule for |angle| > 360 and the sign of the clamped result; (3) exact X-sign error text and number formatting; (4) all-negative-x orientation; (5) default convexity (dump a bare rotate_extrude to .csg — expected 'rotate_extrude(angle = 360, convexity = 2, $fn = 0, $fa = 12, $fs = 2)'); (6) seam vertex welding. Safe to simplify: cap triangulation. Not safe: seam phase at theta=0 (+X), Y-becomes-Z mapping, per-angle fragment starvation, warning-not-error for 3D children. Note the profile-at-theta-0 orientation means users' 2D drawings read directly as the cross-section in the XZ plane — any hidden mirroring breaks every model.

Other geometry operations

dxf_cross

function pre-2015.03 (legacy; still live and un-deprecated in 2021.01) 
dxf_cross(file = "...", layer = "", origin = [0, 0], scale = 1)

Legacy DXF metadata reader FUNCTION, still live in 2021.01: finds a cross drawn as two intersecting LINE entities on a DXF layer and returns the [x, y] intersection point. Historically used to extract anchor/rotation points from 2D CAD drawings.

filePath to the DXF file, resolved relative to the including script.
layerDXF layer to search for exactly two LINE entities forming the cross; empty string for the default/all layers (VERIFY matching rule).
origin[x, y] applied when transforming the found point into model coordinates (VERIFY whether the point is returned as (p - origin) * scale or p * scale + origin — pin the direction).
scaleScale factor applied to the returned coordinates, default 1.

Parses the DXF (cached, same limited entity support as dxf_dim), collects the LINE entities on the requested layer, computes the intersection point of the two lines forming the cross, applies the origin/scale transform, and returns it as a 2-element vector [x, y]. Expression-level function; the classic idiom is translate(dxf_cross(...)) to anchor 3D features at a point marked in a 2D drawing.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Failure (missing file, no such layer, fewer or more than the expected two lines, or parallel lines with no intersection): returns undef with a WARNING of approximately 'WARNING: Can't find cross in 'file', layer 'layer'' (VERIFY exact text and each failure mode's behavior — especially >2 lines on the layer: believed first-two-found win or outright failure; pin it).
  • verify vs oracle whether the two lines must literally intersect within their segments or are treated as infinite lines for the intersection computation.
  • Return type is a plain 2-vector usable directly in translate() (translate pads the missing z with 0).
  • Still live and not deprecation-warned in 2021.01 (VERIFY); removal in future releases is not part of the 2021.01 contract.
  • Same limited DXF parser and caching semantics as dxf_dim; unsupported entities skipped with warnings.
  • origin/scale transform direction must be pinned (see params) — an inverted convention silently shifts every anchored feature.
  • Calling with no file: warning + undef (VERIFY).
c = dxf_cross(file = "plate.dxf", layer = "axis");
translate(c) circle(r = 2);
Implementer: Same support decision as dxf_dim (full clone vs warn-and-undef stub); if cloning, author oracle DXFs with a two-line cross, a three-line layer, parallel lines, and non-touching segments to pin every failure branch and the origin/scale convention. The function must exist in the function namespace either way so scripts don't additionally trigger unknown-function warnings.

dxf_dim

function pre-2015.03 (legacy; still live and un-deprecated in 2021.01) 
dxf_dim(file = "...", name = "", layer = "", origin = [0, 0], scale = 1)

Legacy DXF metadata reader FUNCTION, still fully live (not even deprecation-warned) in 2021.01: returns the numeric value of a named DIMENSION entity from a DXF file. Historically used to drive parametric models from 2D CAD drawings.

filePath to the DXF file, resolved relative to the including script (same resolution as import()).
nameThe dimension entity's name/label to look up in the file. Empty string matches an unnamed dimension (VERIFY).
layerDXF layer to search; empty string means all layers / the default layer (VERIFY exact matching rule and case sensitivity).
origin[x, y] offset applied when interpreting the DXF's coordinates (relevant for dimension types whose value derives from coordinates, e.g. ordinate dimensions). VERIFY whether origin affects plain linear dimension values at all.
scaleMultiplier applied to the returned value, default 1.

Parses the DXF (through the same limited-entity DXF reader used by import(): lines, polylines, arcs, circles, plus DIMENSION entities; results are cached per file/parameters), finds the DIMENSION entity matching name (and layer), and returns its measured value as a number, scaled by scale. The dimension TYPE matters: linear/aligned dimensions return a length; radius/diameter dimensions return that measure; rotated/ordinate types have their own extraction rules (VERIFY per-type formulas against the oracle — upstream computes the value from the dimension's definition points for some types rather than reading stored text). This is an expression-level FUNCTION (usable in assignments and arguments), not a module.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Part of the legacy DXF-era surface, but unlike dxf_linear_extrude()/dxf_rotate_extrude() (deprecated module aliases — see their entry), dxf_dim()/dxf_cross() are NOT deprecated in 2021.01: they remain the supported way to read dimension/cross values out of DXF files.
  • Lookup failure (no such dimension/name/layer, or unreadable file): returns undef with a WARNING of approximately 'WARNING: Can't find dimension 'name' in 'file', layer 'layer'' (VERIFY exact text and whether file-open failure has a distinct message).
  • verify vs oracle dimensions with a text override in the DXF (user-typed measurement text) — believed upstream returns the GEOMETRIC value computed from definition points, ignoring the override text; pin this, it changes results on hand-edited drawings.
  • VERIFY per-dimension-type behavior (linear, aligned, angular, radius, diameter, ordinate): which types are supported at all, and the returned unit/interpretation for angular dimensions (degrees expected).
  • The DXF parser is the limited 2021.01 one: entities it cannot parse are skipped with warnings; splines are unsupported; files from modern CAD exports may parse partially — faithful compatibility includes those warnings.
  • Results are cached: repeated calls with identical (file, layer, name, origin, scale) do not re-read the file within a compile (performance surface; also means mid-compile file changes are not observed).
  • Missing arguments follow normal function-call rules (undef); calling with no file warns and returns undef (VERIFY).
  • scale multiplies the value AFTER extraction; origin participates only in coordinate-derived types (VERIFY).
  • This and dxf_cross are the only geometry-file-reading FUNCTIONS in the language — everything else file-related is a module; the reimplementation's expression evaluator therefore needs file-system access.
w = dxf_dim(file = "plate.dxf", name = "width");
echo("width from DXF", w);
square([w, 10]);
Implementer: Decide support level early: (a) full clone — requires DIMENSION entity parsing in your DXF reader plus per-type value extraction (pin each type via oracle DXFs authored in LibreCAD/QCAD); or (b) stub — parse the call, warn ('dxf_dim not supported'), return undef; a stub keeps old scripts loadable but silently changes their geometry wherever the value fed dimensions, so document it loudly. Either way the function must exist in the function namespace (an unknown-function warning would differ from upstream). Probe set: one DXF containing every dimension type, a text-override dimension, a wrong-layer lookup, and a missing file.

fill

module snapshot (2022+) 
fill()  // SNAPSHOT-ONLY (2022+ dev snapshots)

SNAPSHOT-ONLY: 2D operation that unions its children and removes all holes, returning only the outer boundaries. Not present in 2021.01 (where it is an unknown module).

Children are forced to 2D and implicitly unioned; the result's outlines are classified, hole outlines are discarded, and only outer boundaries are kept as filled regions. Equivalent to 'every point enclosed by an outer boundary becomes solid'. Distinct separate islands remain separate. Output is 2D.

Edge cases & oddities
  • SNAPSHOT-ONLY: 2021.01 emits 'WARNING: Ignoring unknown module 'fill'' and produces nothing; clone that exactly for a 2021.01 baseline. VERIFY whether fill() is feature-flag-gated in snapshots (believed NOT gated, available by default in 2022+ snapshots).
  • Nested structure: a hole containing an island containing a hole — VERIFY: believed everything inside the outermost boundary becomes solid (inner islands merge into the fill), but pin the nesting rule with a bullseye probe.
  • 3D children: warned and ignored with the standard 'Ignoring 3D child object for 2D operation'.
  • Empty input yields empty output silently.
  • Canonical use is text(): fill() text("B") yields the letterform without its counters (holes).
  • Result winding/vertex order after hole removal is implementation-defined; promise region equivalence.
  • fill() takes no parameters; unknown arguments warn and are ignored as usual.
// snapshot only
fill() text("B", size = 20); // letter B without its two holes
Implementer: Trivial atop a working 2D kernel: union children, keep positive-winding (outer) contours, drop the rest — but pin the nested-island rule first. Decide whether your product exposes it at all when pinned to 2021.01; if exposed, gate it behind an explicit snapshot-features opt-in mirroring upstream availability, and keep the unknown-module warning in strict-2021.01 mode.

hull

module pre-2015.03 
hull()

Replaces its children with the convex hull of their union. Works on all-2D or all-3D children; the idiomatic rounding tool (hull over spheres/circles). Takes no parameters.

All children are evaluated (implicit union) and the convex hull of the combined vertex set is produced: a single convex polygon for 2D children (CCW winding), a single convex polyhedron for 3D children. Because hulls operate on the children's TESSELLATED vertices, $fn/$fa/$fs on curved children directly changes the hull (hull over two coarse spheres is visibly faceted). Holes, concavities, internal structure, and colors of the children are discarded by construction. hull() forces exact geometry evaluation of its subtree even during F5 preview — the computed hull mesh is what gets drawn, and slow previews over complex children are expected upstream behavior.

Edge cases & oddities
  • hull() declares no parameters; VERIFY: passing any argument (e.g. hull(convexity=2)) produces an unknown-parameter warning (approximately "WARNING: variable convexity not specified as parameter") and is ignored; $-prefixed named arguments are always accepted.
  • Mixing 2D and 3D children emits approximately 'WARNING: Mixing 2D and 3D objects is not supported' — VERIFY severity (believed message is warning-level in 2021.01 even though docs call it an error) and VERIFY the resulting geometry (believed: the operation proceeds with children matching the first non-empty child's dimensionality; mismatched children are dropped, possibly with additional per-child 'Ignoring ... child object' warnings).
  • Empty children are skipped silently and do not participate in the dimensionality decision; hull with all-empty (or zero) children yields empty geometry with no warning (VERIFY the zero-children case).
  • A single child is legal: the result is that child convexified.
  • 2D result of a 2D hull is real 2D geometry (usable under linear_extrude etc.), living at z=0.
  • %-modified (background) children are excluded from the hull entirely (they are excluded from CSG); #-modified children ARE included but their highlight does not survive the hull (VERIFY highlight behavior in preview).
  • color() on children is dropped — the hull has no color unless color() wraps the hull itself.
  • Degenerate 3D hulls (all points coplanar/collinear/coincident) produce zero-volume geometry; VERIFY: believed to yield an empty or degenerate mesh, possibly with a CGAL warning, and to fail only later if used in CSG at render — clone the deferral.
  • verify vs oracle collinear points on a 2D hull boundary are removed (strict convex hull), so the output vertex count can be lower than the input's even for already-convex input.
  • 3D hull output faces: coplanar triangles are merged into larger faces by the hull implementation and the final triangulation is implementation-defined — promise point-set/solid equivalence, not identical meshes.
  • hull() over children built at very different scales can hit exact-arithmetic slowness; there is no timeout — hangs are upstream-faithful behavior.
  • hull() of 2D children with self-intersections: children are sanitized by the 2D kernel first, so the hull is of the cleaned union.
  • Preview behavior: unlike booleans, hull is NOT composited by OpenCSG — the exact hull is computed even at F5. An always-exact reimplementation gets this for free but must not defer hull errors to F6.
hull() {
    translate([20, 0, 0]) sphere(r = 4);
    cylinder(r = 6, h = 2);
}
Implementer: Implement as convex hull over the union's vertex set (2D: any O(n log n) hull; 3D: quickhull-class). Pin via oracle: mixed-dimension message text and result, zero/empty-children behavior, degenerate (coplanar) 3D input, and whether 2D hull output drops collinear vertices. Safe to simplify: face merging/triangulation of the 3D hull (compare solids, not triangle lists). Not safe: operating on tessellated vertices (hull must see the same $fn-dependent points as the oracle), exclusion of % children, 2D-in/2D-out dimensionality.

minkowski

module pre-2015.03 
minkowski(convexity = 1)

Minkowski sum of its children (folded pairwise left-to-right for more than two), in 2D or 3D. The canonical global-rounding/outsetting tool; famously expensive in 3D.

convexityPreview-only hint, default believed 1 (VERIFY; <=0 clamped). Never affects rendered/exported geometry.

Computes the Minkowski sum A (+) B = { a + b : a in A, b in B } of its first two children, then folds any further children in pairwise from the left: minkowski(){a; b; c;} = (a (+) b) (+) c. All children must share dimensionality; the result has that dimensionality. In 2D the sum runs through the fixed-point polygon-clipping kernel (Clipper-class Minkowski), in 3D upstream decomposes both operands into convex pieces, takes the convex-pair sums (hulls of pairwise vertex sums), and unions the results exactly — correct for arbitrary non-convex input but with combinatorially exploding cost. Because the sum is positional, an operand not centered on the origin translates the result by its offset (summing positions is part of the semantics, not a bug). minkowski() forces exact geometry evaluation even during F5 preview; multi-second/minute previews are expected upstream UX. Colors and modifiers of children are dropped.

Edge cases & oddities
  • A single child passes through unchanged (identity); zero children yield empty geometry.
  • VERIFY (known cross-version change): behavior with an EMPTY child. Believed 2021.01/CGAL: empty children are skipped, so minkowski(){cube(5); <empty>;} returns the cube (mathematically wrong but load-bearing); Manifold-era snapshots return the mathematically correct EMPTY result. Pin your baseline's behavior and treat the other as a version note.
  • Mixing 2D and 3D children: same 'Mixing 2D and 3D objects is not supported' warning/behavior as other multi-child ops (VERIFY severity and surviving geometry).
  • 2D minkowski result is 2D; hole handling in 2D operands is subtle (sums involving holes go through the clipping kernel's Minkowski + union machinery) — VERIFY hole-in-either-operand results against the oracle with a washer (+) square probe; do not assume textbook Minkowski-with-holes semantics without pinning.
  • The result is strictly larger than the first operand in every direction the second operand extends — users rounding a cube with a sphere of radius r get a cube grown by r on ALL sides; this expectation-vs-reality is semantics to preserve, not to fix.
  • Second operand centered off-origin shifts the whole result by that offset (e.g. cylinder(h=1) not z-centered raises the result by 0.5); classic user surprise, exact upstream behavior.
  • 3D performance cliff: concave (+) concave decomposes into many convex pairs; runtime can be minutes/hours with no progress guarantee and no timeout — an implementation that is 'too fast to be believed' is fine, but one that errors out where upstream grinds is a compatibility break.
  • verify vs oracle on internal CGAL failure upstream logs approximately 'WARNING: [minkowski] CGAL exception: <what>' and falls back to a slower exact (Nef) Minkowski path; only if that also fails does the node yield empty/partial output — decide how to map this to a single-path implementation and document it.
  • Degenerate operands (zero-thickness 3D children, zero-area 2D children): VERIFY — believed to either error via the geometry kernel at evaluation time or produce empty output; upstream does not pre-validate.
  • Colors, # highlight, and %-background status of children are dropped/handled as in hull: % children are excluded from the sum entirely.
  • convexity is accepted and appears in .csg dumps (VERIFY dump default) but influences nothing except OpenCSG preview of the containing tree.
  • Preview: like hull and render, minkowski is computed exactly even at F5; there is no OpenCSG approximation of a Minkowski sum.
  • Result triangulation and interior vertex placement are implementation-defined — promise solid equivalence, not identical meshes.
minkowski() {
    cube([20, 12, 4]);
    cylinder(r = 3, h = 1, $fn = 32);
}
Implementer: Pin via oracle FIRST: empty-child behavior in 2021.01 (skipped vs empty result) — this is the highest-risk divergence; then 2D hole semantics, mixed-dimension handling, and the n-ary fold order (probe with three asymmetric operands). Convex-pair decomposition + hull-of-sums + union is a sound implementation strategy (Minkowski sum distributes over union of convex decompositions). Safe to simplify: internal decomposition choice, mesh triangulation, being much faster than upstream. Not safe: pairwise-left fold, positional offset semantics, dimensionality rules, forcing exact evaluation in preview.

projection

module pre-2015.03 
projection(cut = false)

Converts 3D children to 2D geometry on the z=0 plane: cut=false gives the full silhouette (shadow) of the solid, cut=true gives the planar cross-section where the solid intersects z=0. The only 3D-to-2D bridge in the language.

cutBoolean, default false. false: orthographic projection of the ENTIRE solid onto z=0 — the union of the projections of all faces, regardless of where the solid sits in Z (an object floating at z=50 still casts its full shadow). true: the exact 2D region where the solid intersects the z=0 plane; empty if the solid does not touch z=0. To slice at height h, translate the child by [0,0,-h] first.

Children are evaluated (implicit union) as 3D geometry; the node outputs true 2D geometry at z=0 (usable under linear_extrude/rotate_extrude/2D booleans). cut=true is implemented as an exact planar section of the (Nef/exact) solid with z=0 — its boundary is exact up to the 2D kernel's quantization. cut=false is implemented (2021.01) by tessellating the solid, orthographically projecting every facet onto z=0, discarding degenerate (vertical) facets, and unioning all projected triangles in the fixed-point 2D clipping kernel — correct silhouettes, but notoriously slow on large meshes, and the output polygon runs through the clipper's sanitize/cleanup (collinear vertices may be merged). Both modes force exact geometry evaluation of the children even during F5 preview.

Edge cases & oddities
  • cut=true with a solid that does not intersect z=0 yields empty 2D geometry, silently.
  • cut=true tangency degenerate: a solid whose FACE lies exactly in the z=0 plane (e.g. cube(10) sitting on the plane, or touching only at an edge/vertex) is boundary-ambiguous; VERIFY: believed implementation-defined between returning the face region and returning empty — pin with cube-on-plane and cube-above-plane probes and clone the answer.
  • cut=false ignores Z position entirely: translate([0,0,999]) sphere(5) still projects to a radius-5 circle at the origin of the XY plane.
  • VERIFY 2D-child behavior (known soft spot): candidates are (a) 'WARNING: Ignoring 2D child object for 3D operation' + child skipped (believed 2021.01), and (b) pass-through of the 2D child unchanged (behavior discussed/changed in snapshots). Pin 2021.01 and note the snapshot difference.
  • Non-manifold / CSG-invalid 3D children fail during the forced exact evaluation — projection surfaces render-type CGAL errors at preview time, earlier than the same child would fail standing alone.
  • verify vs oracle projection accepts (and .csg-dumps) a convexity parameter even though documentation omits it — believed 'projection(cut = false, convexity = 0)' appears in dumps; accept it silently.
  • Empty 3D children yield empty 2D output silently; multiple children are unioned BEFORE projecting (the silhouette of the union, not the union of silhouettes — identical result, but error/warning attribution differs).
  • Output vertex fidelity: cut=false output has passed through fixed-point clipping (Clipper-class quantization) and cleanup — vertices are NOT bit-identical to input coordinates; micro-scale geometry can collapse. Clone-level detail for golden 2D exports (DXF/SVG of a projection).
  • cut=false on high-triangle-count children is extremely slow upstream (per-facet union); being faster is fine, erroring is not.
  • The result lives at z=0 as genuine 2D geometry: previewed as a thin slab, exportable only via 2D formats, and mixing it with 3D geometry in booleans triggers the standard mixing warning.
  • projection under linear_extrude (flatten-then-re-extrude) is a load-bearing idiom; make the round trip robust.
  • verify vs oracle warnings emitted when the child evaluates to null geometry (historic 'projection() failed' style messages) — probe with an empty 3D child and clone any message.
projection(cut = true) translate([0, 0, -6]) cylinder(r1 = 10, r2 = 0, h = 10); // r=4 disc
translate([25, 0]) projection(cut = false) cylinder(r1 = 10, r2 = 0, h = 10);  // r=10 disc
Implementer: cut=true: implement as exact plane section (cheap, robust). cut=false: any correct silhouette algorithm is acceptable — per-facet projection + 2D union matches upstream's output shape; run the result through the same 2D-kernel cleanup you use for booleans so vertex-merging behavior is self-consistent. Oracle probes: tangent-face cut, 2D-child handling, convexity acceptance, empty-child messages, and DXF export of a projected mesh for quantization comparison. Safe to simplify: performance, internal algorithm. Not safe: cut-vs-silhouette semantics, z-ignorance of cut=false, true-2D output type.

render

module pre-2015.03 
render(convexity = 1)

Semantically the identity operation on its (implicitly unioned) children, but forces full exact geometry evaluation of the subtree even during F5 preview. The classic workaround for OpenCSG preview artifacts and slow interactive CSG.

convexityPreview-only hint for how the resulting baked mesh composites in the surrounding OpenCSG preview tree; default believed 1 (VERIFY). Never affects F6/exported geometry.

Children are implicitly unioned and evaluated to exact geometry immediately (the same computation F6 would do for that subtree), and the resulting mesh is inserted into the tree as an opaque leaf. At F6/render/export time render() changes nothing about the final geometry — it is a pure evaluation-order/preview directive. In preview, the subtree is drawn as the baked mesh instead of being composited live by OpenCSG, which eliminates preview-only artifacts (the notorious difference() ghosting) at the cost of a slow first preview; the result is cached, so subsequent previews are fast until the subtree changes. render() works on 2D children too (mostly a no-op there since 2D is always computed exactly).

Edge cases & oddities
  • Final geometry is ALWAYS identical with or without render(); any observable geometric difference in an implementation is a bug.
  • Errors surface earlier: non-manifold or CSG-breaking children fail during F5 preview inside render() (CGAL-style error text) instead of only at F6 — the timing of error messages is observable surface.
  • verify vs oracle %-background children inside a render()ed subtree disappear from the preview entirely (they are excluded from CSG, and the baked mesh is all that is drawn — the usual translucent-gray ghost is not shown). Known upstream quirk; clone it.
  • verify vs oracle #-highlight children inside render() lose their pink debug highlight in preview (baked away) while still being part of the geometry.
  • verify vs oracle color() applied to children inside render() is lost in 2021.01 preview — the baked mesh draws in the default material color (colors survive only outside/above the render() node). Manifold-era snapshots preserve color through exact evaluation; version-note it.
  • render() with multiple children implicitly unions them first — 'render() { a(); b(); }' equals 'render() union() { a(); b(); }'.
  • Nested render() nodes are harmless (inner ones are subsumed).
  • The baked result participates in caching keyed by the subtree; an identical render() subtree used twice evaluates once — performance-only surface, but real scripts depend on it to finish.
  • convexity is accepted, truncated to int, clamped (VERIFY default/clamp), appears in .csg dumps as 'render(convexity = 1)', and affects only preview compositing of the baked mesh.
  • Passing unknown parameters warns and ignores as usual; $-prefixed named arguments are accepted and dynamically scoped to the subtree.
  • render() of an empty subtree is empty, silently.
  • In an always-exact reimplementation, render() may be treated as a pure group node — but it must still parse everywhere, accept convexity, implicitly union its children, and (if the implementation has any preview/render mode distinction) reproduce the %-and-# visibility quirks above.
render(convexity = 2)
    difference() {
        cube(20, center = true);
        sphere(r = 12);
    }
Implementer: For an always-exact pipeline this is a no-op grouping node: accept and ignore convexity, keep implicit union. Pin via oracle only if you emulate preview: the %-child disappearance and #-highlight loss inside render() are the two behaviors users actually notice. If you clone .csg dumps, note render() appears in them with its convexity. Decide and document whether your 'preview' concept exists at all; if it does, render() must flip the same internal switches your F6 path uses so error timing matches.

roof

module snapshot (2022+, experimental, --enable=roof) 
roof(method = "straight", convexity = 1)  // SNAPSHOT-ONLY, behind --enable=roof

SNAPSHOT-ONLY (2022+ dev snapshots, experimental, gated by --enable=roof): extrudes 2D children into a 3D 'hip roof' where the boundary stays at z=0 and the interior rises at a 45-degree pitch, via the straight skeleton (or a Voronoi-based variant). Not present in 2021.01.

method"straight" (default): heights follow the straight skeleton of the polygon — classic hip-roof geometry with planar roof faces. "voronoi": heights follow (an approximation of) the Euclidean distance-to-boundary field computed via Voronoi diagrams — same ridge heights, differently shaped valleys around reflex corners. VERIFY the exact method-name set and error text for unknown methods (approximately 'ERROR: Invalid roof method ...').
convexityStandard preview-only hint (roofs over concave outlines are concave); VERIFY presence and default in current snapshots.

Children are forced to 2D and implicitly unioned. Every point of the outline (including hole boundaries) stays at z=0; every interior point is lifted to z equal to its distance from the boundary under the chosen method's metric, giving a fixed 45-degree pitch everywhere (steeper/shallower roofs are made by scaling in Z afterwards). The apex height is therefore determined entirely by the shape (its maximal inscribed distance), not by a parameter. Holes produce inner roof slopes descending back to z=0 at the hole boundary. Output is 3D.

Edge cases & oddities
  • SNAPSHOT-ONLY: in 2021.01 'roof' is an unknown module — 'WARNING: Ignoring unknown module 'roof'' — and a 2021.01-compatible implementation must reproduce exactly that unless a snapshot-features mode is enabled.
  • Gated: even in snapshots it requires the experimental flag (--enable=roof or the GUI Features checkbox); VERIFY whether a gated-off builtin warns as unknown module or with a distinct feature-disabled message.
  • 3D children: warned and ignored ('Ignoring 3D child object for 2D operation'), as with the extrusions.
  • Straight-skeleton degeneracies (collinear edges, near-parallel edge events, self-touching outlines) are numerically delicate; upstream had known crash/robustness issues in early snapshot versions — decide whether to clone failures or be more robust, and document the divergence.
  • verify vs oracle method="voronoi" output is a triangulated approximation of the distance field, so its mesh differs per implementation; promise field equivalence, not mesh equality.
  • The 45-degree pitch is fixed; there is no height/pitch parameter in the initial snapshot versions (VERIFY against the current snapshot if chasing snapshot parity).
  • Empty 2D input yields empty output silently.
// snapshot only; run with: openscad --enable=roof
roof(method = "straight") square([20, 10], center = true);
Implementer: Only implement if you commit to snapshot features; it requires a from-scratch straight-skeleton algorithm, a significant standalone project with hard degeneracy handling. If implemented, mirror upstream's flag-gating model (off by default; unknown-module warning when off). Pin: method names, unknown-method error text, hole behavior, and apex heights on an L-shaped probe (skeleton heights are exactly computable by hand for rectilinear shapes).

surface

module pre-2015.03 (.dat mode); 2015.03 (PNG mode + invert) 
surface(file = "...", center = false, invert = false, convexity = 1)

Builds a closed 3D heightmap solid from a text grid (.dat/anything non-PNG) or a PNG image (2015.03+), one grid unit per sample in X/Y, closed below by a flat base. A leaf 3D primitive (no children).

filePath to the heightmap, resolved relative to the including script's directory (VERIFY whether library search paths also apply to data files). Files whose extension is .png (VERIFY case-insensitivity) are decoded as images; everything else is parsed as a whitespace-separated text grid of heights.
centerBoolean, default false. false: the grid occupies x in [0, cols-1], y in [0, rows-1]. true: centered on the origin in X and Y only — Z is never centered.
invertBoolean, default false. PNG mode only: inverts the luminance-to-height mapping (white and black swap roles). VERIFY: silently ignored for text-mode files.
convexityPreview-only hint, default believed 1 (VERIFY). Heightmaps are almost always non-convex, so real scripts routinely set it; it still never affects rendered/exported geometry.

Text mode: each non-comment line is a row of whitespace-separated numeric heights (strtod-class parsing: negative, fractional, exponent forms accepted — VERIFY exponent support); lines starting with '#' are comments and skipped (VERIFY: '#' must be at line start; VERIFY blank-line handling). A grid of R rows by C columns produces vertices at integer X (0..C-1) and integer Y (0..R-1) with Z = the sample value; VERIFY orientation: believed the FIRST line of the file maps to the LARGEST Y (the text reads like a map viewed from +Z). PNG mode (2015.03+): the image is decoded, each pixel converted to a luminance value and mapped into a fixed 0..100 height band (height = luma/255*100); invert=true flips the sense (height = (255-luma)/255*100); VERIFY channel weighting (candidates: plain RGB average vs Rec.709 0.2126/0.7152/0.0722) and VERIFY that alpha is ignored; VERIFY that the image's top scanline maps to the largest Y so the picture looks correct from above. The top surface is triangulated over the grid — VERIFY (believed): each grid cell is split into FOUR triangles around an added center vertex whose height is the average of the cell's corners, an observable mesh signature. The solid is closed by vertical skirt walls from the boundary samples down to a flat base; VERIFY base height (believed z = min(0, min_sample - 1), i.e. base at 0 when all samples are >= 1, else one unit below the minimum; competing reading: always min_sample - 1). Output is 3D and participates in CSG normally.

Edge cases & oddities
  • VERIFY base-plane formula — the single most important pin for this module: candidates are z = min(0, min_value - 1) versus z = min_value - 1 unconditionally; probe with an all-positive file (e.g. all 5s) and an all-negative file and measure the exported bounding box.
  • VERIFY .dat row orientation (first line at max Y, believed) and PNG scanline orientation, with a deliberately asymmetric map; also pin that the two modes agree with each other.
  • Grid spacing is fixed at 1 unit per sample with no scaling parameters; users wrap in scale() — do not add convenience parameters.
  • A grid smaller than 2x2 (single row, single column, or single sample) cannot form faces; VERIFY: believed to produce empty geometry, possibly silently.
  • VERIFY ragged text rows (lines with differing counts): candidates are error, truncate-to-shortest, or pad; probe and clone exactly, including any warning text.
  • VERIFY non-numeric garbage tokens mid-file: believed to stop parsing of that line or the file with a warning; pin message and resulting geometry.
  • Missing or unreadable file: warning (approximate text 'WARNING: Can't open file ...' / 'Can't read heightmap data' — VERIFY exact wording) and EMPTY geometry; evaluation continues.
  • surface() with no file parameter at all: VERIFY — believed warning + empty geometry rather than hard error.
  • PNG height range is hard-coded 0..100 (0 = black, 100 = white without invert); there is no parameter to change it — clone the constant.
  • VERIFY 16-bit and paletted/greyscale-alpha PNG handling: believed decoded via lodepng to 8-bit RGBA first (losing 16-bit precision); indexed and greyscale images work.
  • invert has no effect in text mode (VERIFY: silently ignored, no warning).
  • center=true centers X and Y by shifting by -(C-1)/2 and -(R-1)/2 (half-integer shifts for even sizes — vertices land on .5 coordinates); Z is unaffected.
  • Windows CRLF line endings and trailing whitespace/blank last lines are tolerated in text mode (VERIFY).
  • Comment lines ('#...') are permitted anywhere among data lines, not just as a header (VERIFY).
  • surface() is a leaf primitive: children are not extruded/consumed; VERIFY whether 2021.01 warns (candidate text 'module surface() does not support child modules') or silently ignores children.
  • Negative heights are legal in text mode and pull the base down with them (subject to the base formula above); PNG mode can never produce negative heights.
  • The believed 4-triangles-per-cell-with-center-vertex tessellation (VERIFY) changes vertex counts vs the naive 2-triangle split — golden STL tests will catch a mismatch immediately.
  • File contents are read at evaluation time; caching is per-file upstream (edits are picked up on recompile, and identical uses are not re-parsed — performance surface only).
// heights.dat contains these 4 lines:
// 0 0 0 0
// 0 2 1 0
// 0 1 3 0
// 0 0 0 0
surface(file = "heights.dat", center = true, convexity = 5);
Implementer: This module is almost entirely 'pin the constants': write oracle probes for (1) base plane height with all-positive/all-negative data, (2) row/scanline orientation, (3) PNG luma weighting (pure-red vs pure-green vs pure-blue images make the weights directly readable off the mesh height), (4) cell triangulation (vertex/triangle counts in exported STL), (5) ragged-row and garbage-token handling, (6) missing-file warning text. PNG support requires a from-scratch PNG decoder (zlib inflate + chunk parsing) under a zero-dependency constraint. Safe to simplify: internal caching. Not safe: the 0..100 PNG band, 1-unit grid pitch, closed-solid construction, center affecting XY only.

Control flow and binding

assign (deprecated statement)

syntax pre-2015.03 (deprecated by 2015.03) 
assign (name1 = expr1 [, ...]) statement-or-{block}

Deprecated ancestor of the let statement: binds names in a child scope over its children; still parses and works in 2021.01 with a deprecation warning on every use.

nameN = exprNBindings established for the child scope. VERIFY: whether bindings are sequential (each sees earlier ones, like let) or all evaluated in the outer scope — probe assign(a=1, b=a+1) against the oracle.
bodyChild statement or block; implicit union; bindings visible throughout.

Functionally the pre-2019.05 way to introduce per-scope bindings (historically the only way to 'change' a variable per iteration inside a for body). Emits a deprecation diagnostic each time it is instantiated, then behaves like a scoped binding construct. Never actually removed as of 2021.01; legacy Thingiverse-era scripts use it heavily.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Deprecation text approx: 'DEPRECATED: Assign is deprecated. Use a regular assignment instead.' — VERIFY exact wording, and that it is emitted per instantiation (so a loop over assign spams it; note GUI may rate-limit repeats).
  • VERIFY sequential vs simultaneous binding evaluation (assign(a=1,b=a+1)) — must be cloned exactly for legacy scripts.
  • $-variables may be bound via assign and dynamically scope like any binding — VERIFY.
  • assign is not a reserved word: VERIFY whether user code can define 'module assign()' and shadow it.
  • Under --hardwarnings: VERIFY whether the DEPRECATED diagnostic counts as fatal (deprecations vs warnings may be treated differently).
  • Children see the bindings exactly as with let; nothing leaks to the outer scope.
for (i = [0:2]) assign (r = i * 2 + 1) circle(r);
Implementer: Keep it working forever (compat surface), emit the deprecation verbatim, and pin binding order with an oracle probe. Internally alias to the let-statement implementation once binding-order parity is confirmed.

for (statement)

syntax pre-2015.03 
for (var1 = iterable1 [, var2 = iterable2, ...]) statement-or-{block}

Module-level loop: instantiates its body once per element of each iterable, binding the loop variable(s) immutably per iteration; all iteration results are implicitly unioned into one group.

varN = iterableNOne or more comma-separated bindings. Iterable may be a vector (elements in order, heterogeneous allowed), a range [b:e]/[b:s:e] (expanded lazily, never materialized), a string (per code point), or a bare scalar/other value (single iteration with that value). Multiple bindings form NESTED loops over the cross product, first binding outermost — NOT parallel/zip iteration.
bodyA single child statement or a { } block. The block is a child scope; local assignments inside are allowed and invisible outside; multiple geometry statements inside one iteration are implicitly unioned.

Each iteration evaluates the body in a fresh child scope with the loop variable bound; the variable shadows any outer variable of the same name and cannot be mutated. The results of all iterations are collected under an implicit union (a group node), so 'for' inside difference() counts as ONE child. Ranges iterate begin, begin+step, ... while <= end (ascending) or >= end (descending for negative step) — endpoint inclusion follows the floating-point count, matching range semantics elsewhere. for is a statement, not an expression; there is no value-producing for outside list comprehensions. $-variables assigned in the body scope dynamically affect calls made within that iteration. Modifier characters (*,!,#,%) may prefix the whole for.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Multiple bindings in one for are a CROSS PRODUCT: for (a=[0,1], b=[0,1,2]) yields 6 iterations ordered a-major (a outer, b inner). Classic trap for users expecting zip.
  • Empty vector iterable: zero iterations, empty result, no warning.
  • Bare scalar (or boolean, undef, function value): exactly one iteration with the variable bound to that value — VERIFY: undef and other non-iterables all iterate once rather than zero times.
  • String iterable iterates one 1-character string per Unicode code point — VERIFY: version in which the for STATEMENT (as opposed to comprehensions) gained per-character string iteration; test for(c="abc") against the 2021.01 oracle.
  • Range with begin>end and implicit step 1: DEPRECATED legacy swap — iterates ascending anyway with a deprecation warning approx 'DEPRECATED: Using ranges of the form [begin:end] with begin value greater than the end value is deprecated' — VERIFY exact text; snapshots may instead treat it as empty.
  • Range step 0 (or a step whose element count explodes): warning approx 'WARNING: Bad range parameter in for statement: too many elements (N)' and the loop does not run — VERIFY exact text and the element-count threshold that triggers it.
  • Negative step with begin<end: zero iterations (empty), no warning — VERIFY.
  • Non-numeric range parts: warning + loop skipped — VERIFY message.
  • Loop variable is per-iteration immutable; assignments to it inside the body create a shadowing local for that block only.
  • The for body scope may contain module/function definitions; they are hoisted within that scope and redefined fresh per iteration.
  • Because iterations are implicitly unioned, intersection(){ for(...) ... } intersects one blob with nothing — this is why intersection_for exists.
  • for with zero children after modifier * is wholly ignored; ! inside a for body shows only the marked subtree of (VERIFY) the last-evaluated iteration — pin multi-! behavior against oracle.
  • Huge ranges are lazy: for(i=[0:1e6]) runs 1000001 iterations without allocating a vector; per-iteration module instantiation must hit the geometry cache for identical subtrees or real scripts crawl.
for (a = [0, 2], b = [0:1]) translate([a, b*3, 0]) cube(1);
Implementer: Pin against oracle: cross-product iteration order, scalar/undef single-iteration behavior, string iteration in statement form, all range warning texts, and the too-many-elements threshold. Implement ranges lazily. The implicit-union grouping must be a single CSG child (observable via difference/intersection).

if / else (statement)

syntax pre-2015.03 
if (condition) statement-or-{block} [else statement-or-{block}]

Module-level conditional instantiation: evaluates one branch's geometry/statements; 'else if' chains by nesting; branches are child scopes.

conditionAny expression, tested with the standard truthiness rules (see truthiness entry).
then/else branchSingle statement or { } block; each branch is its own child scope — assignments inside do NOT escape; multiple statements in a taken branch are implicitly unioned.

Only the taken branch is instantiated at all: the untaken branch produces no geometry, no echo output, no warnings, and no evaluation of its expressions. 'else if' is ordinary nesting (else followed by another if statement). The if statement as a whole is one child in its parent (so a modifier prefix applies to whichever branch runs). This is statement-level only; the expression-level conditional is the ternary ?:, and comprehensions have their own if clause.

Edge cases & oddities
  • Assignments inside a branch are scope-local: x=5; if(c){x=6;} leaves outer x==5 — the single most common user trap; must NOT be 'fixed'. VERIFY whether 2021.01 emits any shadow/unused warning here (believed: none).
  • Truthiness: false, 0, -0, "", [], undef are false; everything else true including "false", "0", [0], [[]], -1, inf, nan, ranges, function values — a nan condition takes the THEN branch. VERIFY ranges/function values truthy.
  • Condition expression errors (unknown variable etc.) warn and yield undef, which is falsy — evaluation continues with the else branch (if any).
  • if with no else and false condition contributes an empty group — VERIFY whether it appears as an empty group node or nothing at all in .csg output.
  • Dangling else binds to the nearest unmatched if, C-style.
  • Module/function definitions inside a branch are scoped to that branch's block only.
  • $-variable assignments inside a taken branch dynamically scope over calls within the branch only.
  • A modifier character may prefix the if itself (#if(c)...) and applies to the instantiated result — VERIFY oracle behavior.
  • There is no elif keyword; 'else if' is two tokens.
